# Agent-Economic Evidence Audit — does Loa-Arcturus have a defensible registry-independent public-good core?

**Date:** 2026-08-06 · **Status:** FINAL · **Method:** source-grounded evidence audit with parallel independent research agents and mandatory adversarial verification of every hypothesis verdict.

---

## 0. Verdict up front

**The evidence does not justify building a distinct Loa-Arcturus system today.** The null hypothesis (G) survived adversarial review intact — its attacker concluded the verdict should be *strengthened* to fully supported. Every "something is missing" hypothesis (C, D, E, F) was refuted or reduced to a narrower core under attack; the two "existing things suffice" hypotheses landed asymmetrically (A refuted as a universal claim; B *revised upward* — an adapter SDK + conformance suite covers the entire v0 build at N=1). What survives is **not a protocol, a registry, or a system** — it is:

1. **One genuinely uncovered semantic gap** (the *smallest gap that survived adversarial review*, §5): no adopted standard, and no composition of adopted infrastructure, defines **(i)** a single signed, portable, third-party-verifiable artifact binding *(payer, payee, task, amount, outcome)*, or **(ii)** portable signed schemas for credit-lifecycle events (repayment, default, write-off, recovery) with counterparty-role definitions, cross-document conservation checks, and deterministic replay. The *semantics* exist (ISO 20022, production-scale); the *portable-verifiability layer* is unowned. **But** — the adversarial pass demonstrated this absence is equally consistent with *absence of demand* (five natural experiments: EAS's economically-empty rails, GOBL, Catena ACK, UBL DSig, Pipe's wound-down market), so the gap justifies **contribution to existing venues plus at most ~4–5 schemas of vocabulary**, published as a byproduct of a real loan book — not a system built in advance of one.

2. **A conditional future trigger:** the "protocol" question becomes real only at N≥2 independent operators wanting to exchange comparable credit histories. Today there is at most one prospective operator (0xHoneyJar), it has not built the system, no facility has ever been originated, and the oracle has never scored a real transaction — so the trigger is nowhere in sight.

Everything else in docs 09–11 is either **commodity** (identity, signatures, delegation, envelopes — adopt SIWE/EIP-712/EIP-1271, VC 2.0, JCS/JWS, EAS off-chain), **the operator's private book** (obligor admission, underwriting policy, the Return Ledger's chart of accounts), or **competitive service territory** (underwriting, capital, servicing, collections — §7).

This verdict is consistent with the docs' own strongest instincts — 09 §1.2 already rejects "marketplace / agent registry / SDK" — and inconsistent mainly with their packaging: the claim that the 10 unwritten schemas constitute a "Protocol anyone can implement" is not supported by any current artifact, consumer, or demand signal.

---

## 0.1 Charter and epistemic rules

This audit was commissioned to determine whether Loa-Arcturus has a defensible **registry-independent public-good core**, under binding rules: payments are not proof of work; public statements are not interviews; draft/proposed standards are not adopted infrastructure; a registry must not be recommended merely because identity is fragmented; a gap counts only if it survives adversarial review; if evidence is unavailable, report the gap rather than fill it by inference. Every material conclusion below carries exact file/repo/standard/source references. Web sources were accessed 2026-08-06 unless noted.

**Process:** three waves of parallel agents — (1) claim-by-claim audits of the prior document corpus, (2) capability/defect audits of all eleven repositories at current commits, (3) standards/adoption research across ten function areas — followed by (4) seven independent hypothesis tests (A–G) and (5) adversarial attack agents instructed to refute each verdict, with load-bearing citations re-verified. The orchestrator independently reproduced key on-chain and code facts (§0.3). Working notes: `/tmp/arcturus-audit/notes/` (temporary).

## 0.3 Facts independently reproduced by the orchestrator (2026-08-06)

On-chain, via `rpc.berachain.com`:
- `eth_chainId` = `0x138de` (80094) ✓
- HONEY `0xFCBD14DC51f0A4d49d5E53C2E0950e0bC26d0Dce`: `decimals()` = `0x12` (18) ✓; `DOMAIN_SEPARATOR` = `0xbedfba06…64d1c` ✓ (matches doc 10 §0.1)
- x402 canonical `0x4020420042004200420042004200420042004200`: `eth_getCode` = `0x` — **still absent on 80094** ✓
- ERC-8004 `IdentityRegistry` `0x8004A169FB4a3325136EB29fA0ceB6D2e539a432` on Ethereum mainnet: `eth_getCode` returns EIP-1967 proxy bytecode — **the Draft EIP's registries are deployed** (adoption quality is a separate question, §3.1)

In-repo, at the SHAs above:
- Defects F-2 and F-3 confirmed by direct read: `src/oracle/oracle.ts:208` (`BigInt(Math.floor(Number(...)))`), `:222` (`Number(qualified)/Number(raw)`); `src/routes/x402.routes.ts:253` calls `verifyNonceUnique(pool, …)` whose implementation (`src/services/x402-settlement.ts:181-195`) *inserts* the nonce (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING id`) — the route call commits it, `settle()`'s in-transaction recheck at `:238` then throws `"x402 nonce replay detected"` for every first-time payment. `createX402Router` (`x402.routes.ts:106`) has zero importers and the package has no server entrypoint (`package.json` scripts = typecheck/test/seed:bepolia/verify) — the route is dead code at HEAD.
- hounfour `signature-envelope.schema.json` (8.7.0): Ed25519 over sha256 of RFC-8785-canonicalized payload; `signature_type ∈ {attestation, authorization, commitment, dev_signature}`; **hounfour never verifies signatures** — `validate()` fail-closes with `CRYPTO_DEFERRED` unless `acceptDeferred: true`; verification is consumer-side.
- hounfour has **no** facility/obligor/qualified-revenue/return-ledger/evidence-bundle schema (grep over all 262 `*.schema.json` files); nearest economic schemas are `mutual-credit`, `credit-note`, `dispute-record`, `x402-settlement` (amounts in 6-decimal `*_micro` — the micro-USD weld exists at schema level), `billing-entry`.
- `loa-freeside packages/events/src/envelope.ts`: `acvp-l1-v2` — signature covers all load-bearing fields via JCS canonical (the v1 routing-metadata forgery vector EVT-001 is documented and closed); real code, but not imported by any money path.
- finn `receipt-verifier.ts` (`parseTransferLogs`): strict emitter/recipient/exact-amount match, exactly-one-match fail-closed, payer = the Transfer log's `from`, never `tx.from` — doc 11 D-3's citations are accurate. finn's gateway *does* mount x402 routes when `x402Deps` is injected (`src/gateway/server.ts:578-580`); no production entrypoint builds those deps (§2.3).

Web (fetched 2026-08-06): ERC-8004 status "⚠️ Draft" (eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-8004); its Reputation and Validation registries take the Identity Registry address at `initialize()` — a hard dependency; payments explicitly out of scope. W3C VC Data Model 2.0 = "W3C Recommendation 15 May 2025". UCAN spec v1.0.0. Fusaka (Fulu-Osaka) live on Ethereum mainnet 2025-12-03 (ethereum.org/en/history, block 23,935,694); EIP-7607 lists EIP-7951 (secp256r1 precompile) as a Fusaka Core EIP. x402: repo now under `x402-foundation/x402`; exact-EVM scheme supports EIP-3009 (default), Permit2 witness proxy, and ERC-7710 delegation; **no settlement-response format, no facilitator signature, no receipt binding payment↔resource is defined in the exact scheme**. AP2 v0.2 donated to FIDO Alliance WGs; mandates are signed Verifiable Digital Credentials; adoption is announcement-stage. EAS contracts: MIT, deployed on 15+ mainnets, **protocol predeploy at `0x4200…0021`/`0x4200…0020` on OP-Stack chains** (Optimism, Base, Blast, Soneium, Ink, Unichain); not deployed on Berachain per its own deployment list. A2A v1.0 under the Linux Foundation, zero economic semantics.

One correction to the docs' claims discovered here: the Arcturus memory/corpus dates "Fusaka live 2026-06-24"; Ethereum mainnet Fusaka was 2025-12-03. Whether Berachain 80094 has the P-256 precompile live is **UNVERIFIED** (an `eth_call` probe was inconclusive); any design leaning on "P-256 passkey wallets post-Fusaka" on Berachain needs chain-specific verification first.

## 1. Claim-by-claim audit of the prior corpus

Method: five parallel agents extracted the most material claims from docs 09 (two halves), 10, 11, and the superseded corpus 00–08, classified each as supported fact / reasonable inference / unsupported assertion / contradiction / unanswered question, and verified code-backed claims against the pinned clones. Aggregate over the 108 audited claims: **53 supported facts, 25 reasonable inferences, 13 unsupported assertions, 10 contradictions, 7 unanswered questions.** The corpus is factually strong on repo-level verdicts and honest about its own confounds (doc 10 §8.3/§8.4 are exemplary), and weakest exactly where it packages design intent as existing capability (portability, forkability, "small and cheap").

Headline problems that bear on the core question:
- **The anti-registry argument is overstated at its source.** Doc 09 §1.2 "finn EXP-002 settled this empirically: 39,999 registered → ~0 transacting" — the raw funnel data was never committed; the formal pre-registered verdict was **B1=INSUFFICIENT, not FALSIFIED**; the evidence lives on an unmerged branch (`feature/exp2-agent-commerce-forensics` @ `96df0145`). Direction plausible, "settled" unsupported.
- **The reuse substrate's runtime defects go undisclosed.** Docs 09 §5/§7.2/§9 and 10 §1.1 present billing-api's PgCreditLedger + overspend as adoptable without mentioning that this audit live-proved it runtime-broken (BILL-D1: `SET LOCAL app.community_id = $1` is a PostgreSQL syntax error; BILL-D2: `lot_entries.lot_id NOT NULL` blocks the overspend rows migration 0005 requires). billing-api has no CI.
- **Portability and forkability are aspirational stated as present.** "Signed QualifiedRevenueAttestations the obligor can present elsewhere" (09 §6) — the schema does not exist and the artifact as later specified carries no obligor linkage; "forked and re-run by anyone from a snapshot" (10 §3.1) — billing-api/identity-api are `private: true` and no snapshot mechanism exists.
- **Unresolved cross-document contradictions a builder will hit:** obligor model (10 C-20 "adopted verbatim" vs 11 D-4 override); pilot duration (12 weeks vs weeks 4–16); HONEY-pause mitigation (10 §9.7 "USDC fallback admissible" silently voided by 11 R-6); schema counts (234 / 264 / this audit's reproducible 262).
- **Silently dropped without refutation** (from the superseded corpus): the third-party trust plane (DisputeGame/MOOv2 bonds, 3-of-5 attestors, Merkle pre-commitments — red-team-mandated in 01/06, zero mentions in 09–11) and the Base-pivot survival hedge.

A note on one figure this audit itself propagates: the claims tables below cite hounfour vector counts of 1,241 (`RELEASE-INTEGRITY.json`, the release manifest), 1,275 (one agent's count of `*.json` fixtures), and 1,369 (this audit's `find vectors -type f`, which includes runner scripts and `_meta` manifests). All three bases are reproducible; the differences are counting-method, not drift. The same applies to the 234/262/264 schema-file counts. None of these numbers is load-bearing for any conclusion.

## 2. Repository audit at current commits

Method: parallel agents audited the eleven repos at the §0.2 SHAs (read-only clones), each mapping reusable capabilities with maturity grades, verifying previously-claimed defects at current SHAs, and listing assumptions that must not carry forward. One agent additionally live-verified billing-api's SQL and adapter layers against a throwaway Postgres 16 container. Everything below is at the audit SHAs; the sonar-api and score-api sections double as drift reports against the docs' July-27 pins. Sections: 2.1 loa-arcturus · 2.2 loa-hounfour · 2.3 loa-finn · 2.4 loa-freeside · 2.5 billing-api · 2.6 ledger-api · 2.7 identity-api · 2.8 sonar-api · 2.9 score-api · 2.10 loa · 2.11 loa-dixie.

Cross-repo summary bearing on the core question:
- **The strongest verified assets are disciplines, not systems:** arcturus's deterministic asOf-threaded oracle (mock-mode CI-green) and honest-red harness; hounfour's 262-schema/1,369-file vector corpus with true-BigInt conservation DSL and fail-closed CRYPTO_DEFERRED signature semantics (9,568/9,568 tests green); finn's fail-closed receipt-verifier library; freeside's acvp-l1-v2 signed envelope (real, tested, unwired); dixie's Bayesian/EMA/decay reputation math and hash-chained audit-trail store (tested, but semantically about model quality, not repayment).
- **The recurring pathology is real-but-unmounted:** finn's x402 stack is a tested library with no production deps injection (`verifyEOASignature` defaults to reject-all; the openx402.ai facilitator exists only in comments); freeside's only genuinely on-chain-verifying payment adapter is uninjected; the mounted freeside settlement endpoint fabricates receipts without on-chain reads; ledger-api is a scaffold with zero signature code despite its README.
- **All six arcturus defects (F-1..F-6) are present at HEAD `1920bd6`**, exact lines re-confirmed. Two new billing-api runtime defects (BILL-D1/D2) were proved live. No defect claimed by the docs was found to be fixed.
- **No lending primitives exist in any repo** (facility, draw, amortization, delinquency, write-off) — re-verified by independent grep; doc 09's C-13 stands.

**Assumptions that must NOT carry forward**

- 'Reconciliation sweep will catch up' (x402-settlement.ts:336) — no sweep exists anywhere in the repo; Redis/Postgres divergence is unrepaired.
- 'dune = real data' / 'rpc = live' modes prove anything about filters on real data — scripts/verify.ts:103-117 constructs MockChainProvider(SYNTHETIC_GRAPH) unconditionally regardless of CHAIN_PROVIDER; the real provider classes do not even compile (F-1), and provider-factory.ts imports a nonexistent '@freeside/core/ports' package. Only Assertion C (tx resolution) touches a real chain.
- Payments/tx_hashes as proof of revenue or work performed — every proof field is client-asserted (F-4); the oracle measures what clients claim, gated only by dedup. Do not describe this as 'verified cash flow' without the missing on-chain confirmation step.
- Anti-self-grading is operational — the affiliate allowlist ships intentionally-unmatchable placeholder sentinels (src/oracle/config.ts:48-58), so Filter 1 matches nothing until someone populates real, cited addresses.
- The x402 HTTP payment path works — the router is never mounted (no server entrypoint), and if mounted, every first-time payment fails via the nonce double-burn (F-3).
- Score precision at ecosystem scale — Number() conversion silently saturates above ~$9B cumulative micro-USD (F-2).
- Multi-tenant isolation — RLS is stripped in all five migrations (F-5); community_id scoping is cosmetic.
- 'Proof-object seam' = signed receipts — the proof has no signature, no receipt verification, and the client-signed-payer constraint is a demand on a rail that does not exist yet (PROOF-SCHEMA.md §3, §5 'Later').

**Assumptions that must NOT carry forward**

- Do NOT treat hounfour as an adopted standard or public infrastructure. Exact maturity: self-published internal protocol library (GitHub Packages npm registry, package.json:14), AGPL-3.0, v8.7.0, with two named reference consumers from the same org (loa-finn, loa-freeside per README). No evidence in-repo of any third-party implementation, deployed contracts, or external adoption. It is a well-tested spec artifact, not deployed protocol infrastructure.
- Do NOT describe current schemas as 'signed economic events'. Signature semantics exist (SignatureEnvelope) and hash-chaining exists (phase-completion/revocation-list), but domain-event, billing-entry, transfer-event, mutual-credit, and x402-settlement carry neither. Composition is future work.
- Do NOT treat validate()==valid as trust: the library never verifies Ed25519 signatures (CRYPTO_DEFERRED by design, src/validators/index.ts:1744); consumer-side verification and Keyring resolution are mandatory. Only ModelProviderSpec has in-library JWS verification.
- Do NOT treat schema-level validation as invariant enforcement for money: credit-note's over-credit protections and similar monetary invariants are explicitly service-layer; cross-field constraint evaluation is consumer-side per ADR-010 (cross-language runners emit 'pass-cross-field-deferred').
- Do NOT quote README/manifest inventory numbers (234 schemas / 44 builtins / 219 vectors) — stale; use RELEASE-INTEGRITY.json (262/1241/140) and evaluator.ts (52 builtins).
- Registry-independence caveat: no runtime registry or central party is required (static schema files validate offline, proven via Python runner), but two nominal central touchpoints remain: the npm distribution channel (npm.pkg.github.com — requires GitHub auth to install as a package) and the $id URL namespace (schemas.0xhoneyjar.com — not required to resolve, files ship in-repo). RegistryBridge is a modeled concept in schemas, not a runtime dependency.
- Do NOT assume Go/Rust cross-language parity — those runners exist but were not executed in this audit and are not CI-enforced (UNVERIFIED).

**Assumptions that must NOT carry forward**

- Do NOT assume freeside settlement receipts reference on-chain state: the mounted /api/settlement/settle endpoint fabricates transactionHash from a UUID and keeps quotes in an in-memory Map (FS-D1). Every 'receipt' from this path is client/server-asserted fiction in on-chain terms.
- Do NOT assume the sietch x402 top-up path is live: the only genuinely on-chain-verifying payment adapter (X402PaymentAdapter) is reachable only from tests; its router is never mounted (FS-D2).
- Do NOT describe the signed-envelope machinery as 'production-hardened': it is real, well-designed, and passes its 109 tests, but has zero CI enforcement (FS-D4), a documented chain-fork gap (FS-D5), and essentially no production consumer inside freeside itself (only the operator-dash trace tool; the main worker consumer does not verify envelopes, FS-D7). Correct framing: high-quality, standalone-reusable primitive with unproven operational track record.
- Do NOT conflate the two envelope stacks: @0xhoneyjar/events (acvp-l1-v2) and @freeside/trust-envelope-protocol (CR-009) are separate, non-interoperable signing schemes.
- Do NOT treat dependency-ledger as a working end-to-end evidence ledger: it is consumer-side only; the producer (CR-011A) is explicitly open and end-to-end replay cannot pass (its own README says so).
- Do NOT treat freeside-registry as infrastructure: its README self-declares scaffold status with a 'planned shape'.
- Do NOT source commitment-root capability from freeside: the shape lives in loa-hounfour and is shape-only (INTEGRITY_DEFERRED, no Merkle machinery, no anchor adapter, no deployed contract) — on-chain anchoring for Arcturus D5 is net-new work.
- Do NOT read the 8-point on-chain checks in X402PaymentAdapter as covering payment-for-work: it verifies a USDC transfer happened; it binds nothing to any delivered service (payments-as-proof-of-work is unsupported here too).

**Assumptions that must NOT carry forward**

- That PgCreditLedger 'preserves the lifted invariants' at runtime — the production adapter has never run against real Postgres and fails on its first tenant-scoped query (BILL-D1); only the in-memory semantics are proven.
- That overspend is 'recorded, never rejected' — true only in-memory; on live PG the overspend insert raises NOT NULL and the endpoint returns 502, producing the infinite-DLQ-retry pathology the design explicitly claims to prevent (BILL-D2).
- That the beacon-declared ACVP invariants (idempotency, ledger_conservation, audit_replay) are proven — they hold at the SQL layer (this audit) and in-memory, but no live-PG integration test exists and no CI exists at all.
- That credit_lots.source values imply payment integration — 'purchase'/'x402'/'tba_deposit' are unwired enum strings; there are no payment rails, no webhooks, no payout/treasury code.
- That the ledger is attested or tamper-evident — entries carry no signatures or hash chain; integrity is purely Postgres privilege discipline.
- That 'account' is a first-class economic concept — context.ts hard-codes tenant==account ('one community = one billing account'); there is no obligor/entity/multi-account model to build credit on.

- That ledger-api and billing-api share an overdraft policy — ledger-api refuses underflow atomically (CL-Ledger-3) while billing-api records overspend; reconciling these is a design decision, not a given.

Nothing depends on external/proposed identity standards or on-chain registries. |

Remote CI on the same SHA is green ('Test (bun + postgres)' success). | Treat as an environment/ordering flake, not a code defect; flagged for honesty since one run did fail. |

**Assumptions that must NOT carry forward**

- That identity-api can anchor credit obligations — it has no legal-entity/obligor/KYB layer (IDEN-D1); a user_id is a wallet-cluster, not an economic counterparty. Any MRF/credit design must add an obligor concept elsewhere or extend the spine.
- That an agent/delegation capability model exists — svc-JWT `role` strings are operator-managed cell-to-cell ACLs; there is no controller-authorized, scoped agent-key issuance (IDEN-D2).
- That identity-api issues portable credentials — it issues only short-lived session/service JWTs; no verifiable credentials or attestations of any maturity level are implemented or referenced (IDEN-D3).
- That `did:cell:*` identifiers are DIDs — they are a self-declared stand-in prefix pending an identifier-space spec (IDEN-D4).
- That JWT schema fields equal live capabilities — pool_id is 'Reserved … slice-B does not populate', tier is optional and unpopulated by the mint path, 'berachain' exists only as an enum value; minted sessions hardcode tenant='freeside' with a single ethereum-chain wallet.
- That contract wallets can authenticate — EOA personal_sign only (IDEN-D5).
- That audit_events is tamper-proof — append-only is code discipline, not a DB constraint (explicit 0001 comment), unlike billing-api's trigger-enforced ledger.

Entry points: audit_emit / audit_emit_signed / audit_verify_chain / audit_seal_chain | tested+CI-configured — 143 integration .bats files including audit-envelope-concurrent-write.bats, audit-envelope-bootstrap.bats, audit-verify-for-merge.bats; .github/workflows/bats-tests.yml runs them with pinned dependencies (current CI green status UNVERIFIED from local clone) | .claude/scripts/audit-envelope.sh:1-64,560-620 (1,277 lines) @ 76458ff2; Ed25519 via .claude/scripts/lib/audit-signing-helper.py (Python cryptography lib); envelope schema .claude/data/trajectory-schemas/agent-network-envelope.schema.json v1.2.0; trust store at grimoires/loa/trust-store.yaml, root pubkey .claude/data/maintainer-root-pubkey.txt |
| RFC 8785 JCS canonicalization with cross-runtime byte-identity (bash adapter delegating to Python rfc8785; Node adapter jcs.mjs; conformance suite verifying bash/Python/Node identity) — the canonicalization substrate any content-addressed or signed economic document format (e.g. doc 10's EvidenceBundle, which specifies RFC-8785 + SHA-256 + Ed25519) would need | tested+conformance-suite | lib/jcs.sh:1-21 (144 lines) @ 76458ff2; .claude/scripts/lib/jcs-helper.py; .claude/scripts/lib/jcs.mjs; tests/conformance/jcs/run.sh |
| L4 graduated-trust ledger: per-(scope, capability, actor) tier ledger, ratchet-up on operator grant, auto-drop on observed override, cooldown enforcement, hash-chained, git-TRACKED for reconstructability (trust_grant / trust_record_override / trust_verify_chain / trust_disable) | IMPLEMENTED-BUT-DORMANT — .claude/loa/reference/agent-network-reference.md:61 states explicitly: enabled:false by default, no runtime footprint, no on-disk trust ledger, never ratcheted, 'NOT a live control' | .claude/scripts/lib/graduated-trust-lib.sh (1,665 lines) @ 76458ff2; ~70 bats per reference; tests/integration/trust-chain-and-force-grant.bats, trust-concurrent-and-disable.bats |
| L6 structured-handoff (content-addressed handoff docs, SHA-256 id over JCS canonical frontmatter, INDEX lifecycle, untrusted-content sanitization at surfacing) and L3 scheduled-cycle chassis (whole-cycle flock, content-addressed idempotency, env -i phase isolation, timeout-bounded phases) — patterns relevant to unattended economic-agent operation | tested (bats integration suites); framework-internal | .claude/scripts/lib/structured-handoff-lib.sh (1,510 lines), .claude/scripts/lib/scheduled-cycle-lib.sh (1,713 lines) @ 76458ff2; tests/integration/structured-handoff-6c.bats, scheduled-cycle-lib-3-remediation.bats; soul-identity-lib.sh (627), cross-repo-status-lib.sh (990) also present |

**Defects (status at audit SHA)**

| ID | Status | Citation | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| loa-D1-l4-dormant | verified-present-at-current-sha | .claude/loa/reference/agent-network-reference.md:61 @ 76458ff2 — 'enabled: false by default with no runtime footprint … NOT a live control' | Any design that leans on graduated trust as an existing enforcement mechanism is leaning on dormant code. |
| loa-D2-closed-primitive-enum | verified-present-at-current-sha | .claude/data/trajectory-schemas/agent-network-envelope.schema.json @ 76458ff2 — primitive_id enum: [L1..L7, MODELINV]; schema doc notes 1.1.0 readers hard-reject MODELINV envelopes, illustrating that enum extension is a coordinated reader/writer upgrade | Reusing the envelope for signed economic events is a schema fork with a documented-but-nontrivial reader-upgrade-ordering protocol, not a drop-in. |
| loa-D3-single-machine-single-writer | verified-present-at-current-sha | .claude/scripts/lib/structured-handoff-lib.sh:164-171 (_handoff_assert_same_machine, exit 6 CROSS-HOST-REFUSED) and audit-envelope.sh flock-per-log design @ 76458ff2 | The chain integrity model assumes one machine, one writer per log, keys in the local home dir. It provides tamper-EVIDENCE (verify + git history), not tamper-PREVENTION, and no multi-party consensus. Do not present it as a distributed or adversarially-robust economic ledger. |

**Assumptions that must NOT carry forward**

- Do not treat the L4 trust ledger as a live control — it has never run in production (dormant, enabled:false).
- Do not treat the audit chain as a multi-party or cross-host economic ledger — it is a single-writer, same-machine, flock-guarded JSONL with tamper-evidence semantics only.
- Do not assume the envelope schema accepts new event domains without a coordinated schema bump — primitive_id is a closed enum with strict reader-upgrade ordering.
- Per a cycle-117 finding recorded in agent-network-reference.md, CronCreate-style platform schedules are session-scoped/non-durable — durable scheduling in this stack is real crontab + cycle_register, relevant if any Arcturus design assumed platform-native persistent scheduling.

### 2.11 loa-dixie

*SHA:* `d36c0846` — unchanged (pin = HEAD, d36c0846f03bfd097d35dd2c001de19eec817cf0, 'Phase 49N: record Dixie gate 10 evidence response (#255)')

**Reusable capabilities**

| Capability | Maturity | Citation |
|---|---|---|
| Bayesian blended reputation scoring — blends personal score with collection-level prior via (k*q_collection + n*q_personal)/(k+n), with personal weight w = n/(k+n) | tested (app/tests/unit/reputation-service.test.ts, reputation-conservation.test.ts); CI runs typecheck/lint/test/build (.github/workflows/ci.yml:58-70) | /tmp/arcturus-audit/loa-dixie/app/src/services/reputation-service.ts:497-529 (computeBlended, computeWeight) |
| Temporal exponential decay of sample counts — n_effective = n * exp(-lambda*days), default 30-day half-life, prevents stale aggregates retaining weight | tested; part of same CI suite | /tmp/arcturus-audit/loa-dixie/app/src/services/reputation-service.ts:531-545 (computeDecayed) |
| EMA feedback-dampening with configurable alpha ramp (ascending 'conservative-first' Dixie default vs hounfour's descending canonical) — pure-function scoring engine, 391 lines | tested (app/src/services/__tests__/reputation-scoring-engine.test.ts); NOTE line 123 TODO to replace ascending alpha with canonical hounfour delegation | /tmp/arcturus-audit/loa-dixie/app/src/services/reputation-scoring-engine.ts:86-129 (dampened score: oldScore + alpha*(newScore-oldScore)); per-dimension blending at :267-276 |
| Hash-chained audit-trail store — SHA-256 chain-bound entries, per-resource_type independent chains with genesis hashes, version-aware verification (legacy v9 double-hash vs canonical, ADR-006), TOCTOU-safe append via serialized transaction with FOR UPDATE tip lock | tested (app/tests/unit/audit-trail-store.test.ts, audit-trail-version-aware.test.ts); Postgres-backed via DbPool (audit-trail-store.ts:29,196) | /tmp/arcturus-audit/loa-dixie/app/src/services/audit-trail-store.ts:1-21 (design), :96-168 (computeChainBoundHash_v9 + version dispatch), :195-296 (AuditTrailStore, transactional append) |
| Reputation state machine with cold->warming->established->authoritative lifecycle and universal reset-to-cold (sanctions/decay), plus cross-model meta-score weighted by per-cohort sample counts | tested (app/tests/unit/reputation-evolution.test.ts, reputation-activation.test.ts) | /tmp/arcturus-audit/loa-dixie/app/src/services/reputation-service.ts:480-494 (isValidTransition), :547-560 (computeCrossModel); types at app/src/types/reputation-evolution.ts |

**Assumptions that must NOT carry forward**

- Dixie is NOT a standalone reputation library — it is 'dixie-bff', a governed multi-agent Backend-for-Frontend for the HoneyJar ecosystem (README.md:3): 47-endpoint API, 15-layer middleware, 5-tier conviction staking, soul memory. persona/oracle.md is an Oracle knowledge-interface persona generated from loa-finn, not a Discord bot; no Discord integration found.
- The reputation math is NOT self-contained: canonical EMA config/functions and AuditTrail types come from @0xhoneyjar/loa-hounfour, pinned to github SHA c041cdd4 / tag v8.6.0 (app/package.json:23,27). Any extraction carries the hounfour dependency.
- The audit-trail store requires a live Postgres pool (DbPool, app/src/db/client.js) — it is not a file-based or embeddable store.
- Reputation aggregates are declared governance_class: 'registry-extensible' (reputation-service.ts:449,458) and sit inside Dixie's GovernedResource/mutation-log governance framework — the scoring path assumes the governance substrate exists.
- Domain mismatch for doc 09's claim: Dixie's reputation scores model/agent QUALITY observations (quality dimensions, model cohorts, feedback dampening), not repayment history. The machinery (Bayesian blend, decay, hash chain) is genuinely reusable, but 'repayment-history scoring' would be a repurposing — the semantics do not exist in dixie and must not be described as already built.

---

## 3. Standards and implementations comparison

Method: four parallel research agents covered ten function areas (controller identity, credentials, capability delegation, smart accounts, agent communication, machine payments, task/payment binding, receipts, revocation, economic history), each required to quote exact maturity status lines and separate spec existence from adoption evidence. All web sources accessed 2026-08-06. The charter rules applied: draft ≠ adopted; payments ≠ proof of work; announcements ≠ deployments.

One consistency caveat that applies wherever EIP-7951/Berachain appears below: EIP-7951 is Final and shipped in Ethereum mainnet Fusaka (2025-12-03). Statements that Berachain has the P-256 precompile live derive from Berachain documentation; this audit's direct chain probe was **inconclusive** (§0.3), so treat "live on Berachain" as docs-asserted, chain-unverified.

### 3.1 Controller identity & smart accounts

| Standard / system | Maturity (exact, quoted) | Registry dependency | Covers | Does not cover |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W3C DID Core 1.0 / 1.1 (+ methods did:pkh, did:ethr, did:web, did:key) | DID Core 1.0: "W3C Recommendation 19 July 2022" (https://www.w3.org/TR/did-core/, accessed 2026-08-06). DID 1.1: "W3C Candidate Recommendation Snapshot 05 March 2026" — "not expected to advance to Recommendation any earlier than 05 April 2026" (https://www.w3.org/TR/did-1.1/, accessed 2026-08-06). Method specs are NOT W3C Recommendations: did:pkh header "Status: Draft" (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/w3c-ccg/did-pkh/main/did-pkh-method-draft.md, accessed 2026-08-06; repo notes CCG meetings "on indefinite hiatus"); did:web respec config specStatus "unofficial" (https://w3c-ccg.github.io/did-method-web/ page source, accessed 2026-08-06); did:key respec config "CG-DRAFT", titled "The did:key Method v0.9" (https://w3c-ccg.github.io/did-key-spec/, accessed 2026-08-06); did:ethr spec has no formal status label, editor Mircea Nistor, DIF-hosted (https://github.com/decentralized-identity/ethr-did-resolver/blob/master/doc/did-method-spec.md, accessed 2026-08-06) | did:pkh/did:key: none (generative). did:web: DNS + HTTPS hosting (controller-owned, no shared registry). did:ethr: optional shared on-chain ERC-1056 registry per chain (default resolution works registry-free until you rotate/delegate). DID Core itself is method-agnostic. | did:pkh: deterministic wrap of a CAIP-10 chain account as a DID — zero registry, zero ceremony; the agent's EOA/smart-account address IS the identifier; DID document 'controller' property + verificationMethod/authentication/capabilityInvocation/capabilityDelegation relationships — the DATA MODEL for 'controller X controls key Y for purpose Z' exists in DID Core 1.0; did:web: identity document served from a DNS domain the controller already owns — key rotation and revocation by editing the hosted document; no shared registry beyond DNS/TLS; did:key: pure key-as-identifier for ephemeral agent keys; did:ethr: on-chain delegate management (addDelegate/revokeDelegate with expiry) on ERC-1056 registry — controller/delegate/revocation semantics on-chain | KEY QUESTION verdict: the DID Core data model CAN express controller→agent-key with purpose-scoped verification relationships and revocation (rotate the doc), registry-free via did:web/did:pkh — but scoping is coarse (relationship categories, not spend limits/targets), and NOTHING enforces it at transaction time on-chain; a DID doc is advisory metadata unless every verifier resolves and honors it; did:pkh and did:key have NO revocation or rotation at all (identifier = key; compromise = new identity); No binding from DID to LEGAL entity — that requires a separate VC/attestation layer (e.g. vLEI), out of scope of DID Core; did:ethr's revocable-delegate feature depends on the ERC-1056 registry contract — which the 4,572-tx count shows nobody economically relevant uses; did:web trust reduces to DNS+TLS+webhost integrity; document can be swapped silently; no history/audit |
| SIWE / EIP-4361 + EIP-191 + EIP-1271 + EIP-712 (signature/auth stack) | ERC-4361: "status: Final", created 2021-10-11, requires 55,137,155,191,1271,1328 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ethereum/ERCs/master/ERCS/erc-4361.md, accessed 2026-08-06). EIP-191: "Final", created 2016-01-20 (https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-191). EIP-1271: "Final", created 2018-07-25 (https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-1271). EIP-712: "Final", created 2017-09-12 (https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-712). All accessed 2026-08-06. | None. EIP-191/712/4361 are pure signature formats. EIP-1271 needs only the controller's own contract deployed — no shared/third-party registry. | EIP-712: typed, domain-separated, human-auditable off-chain authorization messages — the substrate for every permit/order/mandate signature in DeFi; EIP-1271: lets a CONTRACT (i.e., a controller's smart account / Safe) be the signature-verifying identity — this is the exact primitive that lets 'controller = contract, not key', and internal contract logic (multisig, session module) decides validity, revocably, with zero registry; EIP-4361/SIWE: standardized message format for account→service authentication, incl. Resources field (URIs of what's being authorized) and expiration-time/not-before — a weak scoped-capability envelope; EIP-191: framing that prevents signed messages replaying as transactions | KEY QUESTION verdict: EIP-1271 + EIP-712 together are the strongest registry-free answer found — a controller smart account can validate/invalidate an agent key's signatures by internal policy, revocable instantly by the controller, no shared registry — BUT the SCOPE of authority is whatever the verifying contract implements; there is no standard scope vocabulary (SIWE Resources are opaque URIs with no semantics); SIWE authenticates a session; it does not delegate on-chain execution authority; No controller→agent relationship model: SIWE proves 'this address signed', not 'this address is an agent of legal entity X'; EIP-1271 requires the controller identity to BE a deployed contract on some chain (per-chain, ~gas cost); pure off-chain counterparties must run eth_call to verify |
| ERC-8004 "Trustless Agents" (Identity / Reputation / Validation Registries) | "status: Draft", created 2025-08-13, requires 155, 712, 721, 1271 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ethereum/ERCs/master/ERCS/erc-8004.md YAML front matter, accessed 2026-08-06). VERIFIED still Draft as of today. Official contracts repo notes the Validation Registry is "still under active update and discussion with the TEE community" (github.com/erc-8004/erc-8004-contracts README, accessed 2026-08-06). | HARD dependency: per-chain singleton registries (spec Rationale: "We expect the registries to be deployed with singletons per chain"), deployed as EIP-1967 upgradeable proxies by a single team — a shared registry AND a de facto central party (proxy admin). | Identity Registry: agent = ERC-721 token; agentId = tokenId; global identity = '{namespace}:{chainId}:{identityRegistry}' + agentId; tokenURI → off-chain registration file (ipfs/https/data:) with services (A2A, MCP, web, ENS, DID), x402Support, supportedTrust; Ownership/transfer semantics from ERC-721: the token OWNER (can be a Safe / legal-entity-controlled contract) controls the agent record — a real controller→agent link; Reserved agentWallet metadata key: changing it requires signature proof ("EIP-712 for EOAs, ERC-1271 for smart contract wallets") and it "is automatically cleared" on token transfer — an owner-controlled, revocable agent-key binding; Reputation Registry: standard interface for feedback signals bound to a specific identity registry via initialize(address identityRegistry_) | KEY QUESTION verdict: partially — owner(Safe)→agentWallet is controller→agent-key, revocable — but authority is binary (one wallet binding), NOT scoped (no spend limits, no action scopes), and it REQUIRES the shared per-chain singleton registry; What breaks WITHOUT the on-chain registry (per spec text, accessed 2026-08-06): 'without a registry entry there is no defined agent identity under this ERC' — agentId is the registry-assigned tokenId, the registration file must contain a registrations array pointing BACK to the registry, and domain verification matches against 'the on-chain agent'. ERC-8004-SHAPED data off-registry gives you: no canonical agentId, no ownership/transfer semantics, no agentWallet binding or its auto-clear-on-transfer revocation, no Reputation/Validation anchoring (both bind to a registry address), and no Sybil floor (and even WITH the registry the Sybil floor is near zero per reality-check). What survives off-registry: the registration-file JSON schema as a self-describing agent card, and EIP-712/1271 wallet-proof patterns — i.e., it degrades to did:web-with-extra-steps; Draft status: interfaces may still change (v1.2 already added unsetAgentWallet, int128 feedback values); Validation Registry unfinished, no mainnet deployment; No legal-entity binding: token owner is just an address; mapping owner→legal controller is out of scope; Registry contracts are upgradeable proxies controlled by the '8004 team' deployer (0x914d7Fec... created both mainnet+Base) — a central party can change registry logic |
| Smart accounts: ERC-4337 + EIP-7702 + Safe + session keys / ERC-7579 / ERC-7710/7715 | ERC-4337: "status: Final", created 2021-09-29 (raw.githubusercontent.com/ethereum/ERCs/master/ERCS/erc-4337.md, accessed 2026-08-06). EIP-7702: "Final" (Standards Track: Core), created 2024-05-07 (eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-7702); live in Pectra — "successfully activated on Ethereum mainnet at epoch 364032, on 07-May-2025 at 10:05 (UTC)" (ethereum.org/en/roadmap/pectra/, accessed 2026-08-06). ERC-7579: "status: Draft", created 2023-12-14 (raw ERCs repo, accessed 2026-08-06). ERC-7715: "Draft", created 2024-05-24; ERC-7710: "Draft", created 2024-05-20 (eips.ethereum.org, accessed 2026-08-06). Smart Sessions module: "This software is in beta and should be used at your own risk" (github.com/erc7579/smartsessions README, accessed 2026-08-06). | None for authority itself (account-local storage). ERC-4337 relies on shared-but-permissionless singleton EntryPoint contracts and a bundler market; paymasters are optional third parties. EIP-7702 needs no registry at all. | THE strongest current answer to the KEY QUESTION: controller (legal entity) holds owner/admin rights on a smart account (Safe multisig or 7579 account); agent key is installed as a session key / validator module with SCOPED policies (target contracts, function selectors, spend limits, time bounds — Smart Sessions policy contracts); revocation = uninstall module / disable session, instant, controller-only; NO shared registry — authority lives in the account's own storage; EIP-7702 gives EOAs the same capability natively post-Pectra (delegate designation, revocable by re-signing authorization to zero/another delegate); ERC-4337 adds gas sponsorship (paymasters) and a permissionless execution mempool — an agent can transact without holding ETH; EIP-1271 exposure: the account can also validate the agent's OFF-chain signatures under the same scoped policy (ERC-7739 nested typed sigs in Smart Sessions); Safe provides the audited, $60B-TVS-grade controller vessel; Safe7579 adapter connects it to the module ecosystem | Scope semantics are per-chain and per-account: a session key's authority is not legible to off-chain counterparties without simulating the account's validation logic; there is no portable 'authority certificate'; The module/permission layer is NOT final: ERC-7579 Draft, ERC-7710/7715 Draft, Smart Sessions self-declared beta — the controller→agent scoping standard is real code but draft standards; No legal-entity binding: 'controller' is a multisig address; entity→address attestation is out of scope; Cross-chain: authority must be replicated per chain (no canonical cross-chain revocation); EIP-7702 delegation is all-or-nothing at the EOA level (the delegate contract then implements scoping); a compromised controller EOA key can re-delegate |
| P-256/secp256r1 on-chain: EIP-7951 (L1, Fusaka) / RIP-7212 (L2s) — passkey verification | EIP-7951: "🎉 Final" (Standards Track: Core), created 2025-05-27 — P256VERIFY precompile at 0x100, 6900 gas, "supersedes RIP-7212" fixing two security flaws (eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-7951, accessed 2026-08-06). Included in Fusaka: EIP-7607 "Hardfork Meta - Fusaka" status "Final" lists "EIP-7951: Precompile for secp256r1 Curve Support" under Included EIPs (eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-7607, accessed 2026-08-06). Ethereum mainnet Fusaka activation: "Dec 3, 2025, 9:49:11 PM UTC" block 23,935,694 (ethereum.org/en/history/, accessed 2026-08-06). RIP-7212: status "Final" (github.com/ethereum/RIPs/blob/master/RIPS/rip-7212.md, accessed 2026-08-06). | None — protocol-level precompile at address 0x100. | Cheap native verification of WebAuthn/passkey/Secure-Enclave/Android-Keystore/HSM P-256 signatures on Ethereum L1 (since 2025-12-03), Berachain (since 2026-06-24), OP Stack (Fjord), Arbitrum (ArbOS 30); Enables agent or controller keys held in hardware (enclave/HSM/passkey) to be first-class on-chain signers via smart-account validator modules — key custody without seed phrases | KEY QUESTION verdict: not an identity or authority standard at all — it is a curve verifier; it makes the smart-account answer (row above) practically custody-safe but expresses nothing about controllers, scopes, or revocation by itself; No EOA can be a P-256 key: P-256 signers only work behind a contract account (4337/7702/Safe module) that calls the precompile; WebAuthn envelope parsing (authenticatorData/clientDataJSON) still must be done in contract code; the precompile only checks the raw curve signature |
| ENS + agent naming (ENSIP-25 / ENSIP-26 / ENSIP-27) | ENSIP-26: "Status: draft", "Created: May 17, 2025" (docs.ens.domains/ensip/26/, accessed 2026-08-06). ENSIP-25 announced 2026-03-04 by enslabs.eth, "uses only existing ENS primitives. No new contracts or resolver upgrades" — no formal lifecycle status stated on the blog post (ens.domains/blog/post/ensip-25, accessed 2026-08-06). ENSIP-27 (agent.json card schema): third-party proposal stage only (blog.tobira.ai via search, accessed 2026-08-06) — UNVERIFIED as accepted. | Hard: ENS registry + resolvers on Ethereum (DAO-governed). ENSIP-25 additionally depends on ERC-8004 registries. | Human-readable, owner-controlled, multichain name for an agent; text records freely settable/revocable by the name owner (who can be a Safe); ENSIP-25: bidirectional verification between an ENS name and an ERC-8004 registry entry (record key agent-registration[registry][agentId]; one-sided claims treated as unverified); ENSIP-26: agent-context + agent-endpoint[mcp\|a2a\|web] text records for discovery | KEY QUESTION verdict: ENS can express 'this name (controlled by entity's Safe) points at this agent', revocably (delete the text record) — but ENS IS a shared registry (the ENS registry contracts + DAO-governed root), authority scoping doesn't exist (records are unstructured strings), and the agent-specific layer (ENSIP-25/26) has draft status and zero observed adoption; Names cost rent and are transferable/expirable — name lapse is an identity-hijack vector for long-lived agents; ENSIP-25 inherits ERC-8004's registry dependency for the agent side of the link |
| (Adjacent) UCAN — capability delegation tokens | "Version 1.0.0" per spec README (github.com/ucan-wg/spec, accessed 2026-08-06) — community spec, NOT an IETF RFC or W3C Recommendation; 285 stars | None (self-certifying token chains). | The cleanest off-chain MODEL for the key question: DID-identified issuer delegates attenuated capabilities (resource + ability + caveats) to another DID, chainable, with expiry and revocation-by-reference — controller→agent scoped authority with no registry | No on-chain verification path (verifiers are off-chain services); revocation is best-effort (revocation lists the verifier must check); no EVM ecosystem adoption for economic authority found |

**Adoption evidence (with sources):**

- *W3C DID Core 1.0 / 1.1 (+ methods did:pkh, did:ethr, did:web, did:key)*:
  - npm weekly downloads 2026-07-29..2026-08-04 (api.npmjs.org, accessed 2026-08-06): did-resolver 110,495; did-jwt 79,223; web-did-resolver 20,934; key-did-resolver 14,301; @veramo/core 12,050; ethr-did-resolver 9,045; pkh-did-resolver 934 — shows the resolver stack is used, but did:pkh's dedicated resolver is near-zero, and none of this distinguishes economic use from SSI pilots
  - did:ethr on-chain reality: EthereumDIDRegistry 0xdca7ef03e98e0dc2b855be647c39abe984fcf21b on Ethereum mainnet has only 4,572 transactions ALL-TIME (eth.blockscout.com/api/v2/addresses/.../counters, accessed 2026-08-06) — essentially no on-chain economic usage; did:ethr is used almost entirely in its default 'no registry write' mode
  - did:pkh production use is via Ceramic's did-session library pairing did:pkh with SIWE/CACAO (github.com/ceramicnetwork/docs did-session.md; blog.ceramic.network 'unhackable apps with SIWE' — DIDSession "treats your Ethereum address as a did:pkh"; accessed 2026-08-06 via search) — data-network auth, not payments/credit
  - did:web at scale: AT Protocol/Bluesky — "Currently, atproto supports two DID methods": did:plc and did:web (https://atproto.com/specs/did, accessed 2026-08-06); this is social-identity scale adoption, not economic identity
  - Google AP2 agent-payments mandates use verifiable digital credentials; multiple secondary sources tie them to W3C VCs, but NO source found showing DIDs as the identifier layer in AP2 (ap2-protocol.org snippets via search, accessed 2026-08-06) — UNVERIFIED that any major agent-payments rail uses DIDs
  - No evidence found of did:pkh/did:ethr/did:web/did:key anchoring ECONOMIC identity (credit, invoicing, settlement) in production anywhere — stated conservatively: none found
- *SIWE / EIP-4361 + EIP-191 + EIP-1271 + EIP-712 (signature/auth stack)*:
  - siwe npm package: 251,068 weekly downloads (api.npmjs.org/downloads/point/last-week/siwe, 2026-07-29..08-04, accessed 2026-08-06); spruceid/siwe repo 1.1k stars, sponsored by Ethereum Foundation and ENS (github.com/spruceid/siwe, accessed 2026-08-06)
  - EIP-712/EIP-1271 are load-bearing in the highest-volume DeFi infrastructure: Safe (57M+ wallets, $1T+ processed — safe.global homepage, accessed 2026-08-06) validates all owner signatures via EIP-712 domain-separated hashes and exposes EIP-1271 isValidSignature; ERC-8004's own agentWallet change requires "EIP-712 for EOAs, ERC-1271 for smart contract wallets" (spec text, accessed 2026-08-06)
  - In-repo evidence of economic use: 0xHoneyJar/identity-api runs "SIWE + ES256 svc-JWT/JWKS" in production (grimoires/loa/next/11-v0-reconciliation.md, table row identity-api, SHA bca4412c; repo doc at git 1920bd6)
  - Ceramic did-session uses SIWE+CACAO to bind did:pkh sessions (blog.ceramic.network, accessed 2026-08-06 via search)
- *ERC-8004 "Trustless Agents" (Identity / Reputation / Validation Registries)*:
  - Mainnet deployments EXIST and are real: IdentityRegistry 0x8004A169FB4a3325136EB29fA0ceB6D2e539a432 + ReputationRegistry 0x8004BAa17C55a88189AE136b182e5fdA19dE9b63 deployed at same vanity addresses on 24 mainnets incl. Ethereum, Base, Arbitrum, OP, Polygon, BSC (github.com/erc-8004/erc-8004-contracts README, accessed 2026-08-06)
  - On-chain volume (eth.blockscout.com + base.blockscout.com API, accessed 2026-08-06): Ethereum mainnet IdentityRegistry created 2026-01-29 (tx 0xd0adc6f92123673d7e0b9132d160cf73f11df11bf8e1a253d8b73ebedc726cf3), 17,008 txs, 9,159 ERC-721 holders, EIP-1967 proxy → IdentityRegistryUpgradeable (NOTE: upgradeable proxy = admin-key risk); Base: 74,380 txs. NO Validation Registry on mainnet.
  - Quality of that volume is poor — independent audit (YoungsukHwang/erc8004-reality-check, ETHGlobal NY 2026, BigQuery-verified, accessed 2026-08-06): of 34,566 mainnet registrations, only 224 (0.65%) have a non-empty service endpoint, only 105 (0.30%) pass a ≥3-unique-reviewer Sybil bar, only 32 (0.09%) of x402-claiming agents ever received a USDC transfer; top wallet minted 28.8% of all agents; feedback URIs reused ~17x (reputation washing)
  - Ecosystem integrations exist but are early: 3,632 GitHub code hits for the mainnet address incl. valory-xyz/open-autonomy and heurist-agent-framework (gh api search/code, accessed 2026-08-06); ENS ENSIP-25 (ens.domains/blog/post/ensip-25, 2026-03-04) defines ENS↔ERC-8004 linkage; press claims of Graph Foundation backing (knowyouragent.network via search) — announcements, not volume
  - EPISTEMIC NOTE: registration counts are NOT proof of economic activity; the reality-check funnel shows ~99.3% of registrations have no credible service or reputation
- *Smart accounts: ERC-4337 + EIP-7702 + Safe + session keys / ERC-7579 / ERC-7710/7715*:
  - ERC-4337 volume (bundlebear.com/overview/all, accessed 2026-08-06): "Total UserOps 1,230,582,014"; "Accounts with 1+ Userops 63,376,033"; Total Bundle Txns 799,149,755; Total Paymaster gas volume $13,310,868; across Base, Polygon, Worldchain, Arbitrum, Optimism, BSC, Ethereum et al.
  - EntryPoint on-chain (Blockscout API, accessed 2026-08-06): v0.7 0x0000000071727De22E5E9d8BAf0edAc6f37da032 — Ethereum mainnet 2,957,761 txs, Base 74,851,919 txs; v0.6 mainnet 1,654,897; v0.8 mainnet 646,446. Note: 4337 volume is overwhelmingly on L2s, not L1.
  - EIP-7702 (bundlebear.com/eip7702-overview/all, accessed 2026-08-06): "Currently live smart accounts: 44,664,094"; "Total authorizations: 213,964,907"; "Total set code transactions: 88,882,240" across Ethereum/BSC/Base/OP/Arbitrum/Unichain/Polygon/Gnosis. Caveat: BundleBear does not attribute this to agent use; large fractions are wallet-default upgrades.
  - Safe (safe.global homepage, accessed 2026-08-06): "$1T+ Total value processed", "57M+ Wallets deployed", "$60B+ Total value secured"; @safe-global/protocol-kit 165,031 npm weekly downloads
  - SDK downloads weekly 2026-07-29..08-04 (api.npmjs.org): permissionless 128,995; @zerodev/sdk 60,667; @rhinestone/module-sdk 13,913; @account-abstraction/contracts 35,691
  - ERC-7579 supporters listed: Biconomy, ZeroDev, Rhinestone, OKX, Etherspot, Safe, Pimlico, WalletConnect, ZK Email (erc7579.com, accessed 2026-08-06); Smart Sessions module co-authored by Rhinestone + Biconomy (github.com/erc7579/smartsessions)
- *P-256/secp256r1 on-chain: EIP-7951 (L1, Fusaka) / RIP-7212 (L2s) — passkey verification*:
  - ARCTURUS CLAIM CHECK: docs claim "Fusaka (mainnet 2026-06-24): EIP-7951 P-256 precompile ... EIP-7702" (grimoires/loa/next/09-decision-architecture.md:62, git 1920bd6). VERDICT: CORRECT for BERACHAIN, wrong if read as Ethereum. Berachain docs: "Fulu + Osaka = Fusaka" mainnet activation 2026-06-24 (Bepolia 2026-05-27), status Live, listing EIP-7951 ("Native verification of passkey and hardware-key signatures") and EIP-7702 among supported EIPs (docs.berachain.com/build/protocol/overview.md, accessed 2026-08-06). Ethereum's Fusaka shipped earlier: 2025-12-03. So on Berachain the P-256 precompile has been live only since 2026-06-24 (~6 weeks as of today); note the separate Berachain "Osaka1" fork (fee floors only) activated mainnet 2026-08-05 16:00 UTC (docs.berachain.com/nodes/operations/osaka1.md, accessed 2026-08-06).
  - L2 precedent: OP Stack — P256VERIFY "specified as part of RIP-7212 and was added to the OP-Stack protocol in the Fjord release" (specs.optimism.io/protocol/precompiles.html, accessed 2026-08-06); Arbitrum adopted RIP-7212 in ArbOS 30 via AIP (forum.arbitrum.foundation t/23298, accessed 2026-08-06 via search)
  - Passkey ecosystem demand signal: @simplewebauthn/server 2,903,867 npm weekly downloads (api.npmjs.org, 2026-07-29..08-04) — WebAuthn itself is mass-adopted; on-chain P-256 verification volume specifically: no per-precompile usage stats found — UNVERIFIED
  - Gas: 6900 on L1 (EIP-7951) vs 3450 in RIP-7212 L2 deployments (EIP-7951 text, accessed 2026-08-06)
- *ENS + agent naming (ENSIP-25 / ENSIP-26 / ENSIP-27)*:
  - ENS base layer is mature: "1,345,030 names", "638,435 owners", "600 integrations" (ens.domains homepage counters, accessed 2026-08-06); @ensdomains/ensjs 39,859 npm weekly downloads
  - Agent-naming layer specifically: NO production adoption found. ENSIP-25 post cites zero live integrations (wallet badges described as potential); ENSIP-26 spec contains only a hypothetical 'Swap Agent' example and says it "intentionally remains minimal to encourage adoption and experimentation" (docs.ens.domains/ensip/26/, accessed 2026-08-06)
  - Ecosystem experiments: ENS8004.xyz by Unruggable (discuss.ens.domains t/22164), ens.tools agent-identity docs — community tooling, no volume data found
- *(Adjacent) UCAN — capability delegation tokens*:
  - Storacha (web3.storage successor) acknowledged for "feedback from real world use cases" (spec README, accessed 2026-08-06) — storage-service auth in production; no economic/payments deployment found — none found beyond storage auth

**Coverage gaps found in this domain (conservative statements):**

- No standard expresses machine-readable SCOPED authority that is legible OFF-chain and enforced ON-chain simultaneously: session-key policies (Smart Sessions/ERC-7579) enforce scopes on-chain but are opaque account-local storage; DID capabilityDelegation and UCAN caveats are legible documents but unenforced at transaction time; SIWE Resources are semantic-free URIs. A counterparty today cannot cryptographically learn 'agent key K may spend ≤X of token T against contract C until date D on behalf of controller E' from any standard artifact.
- No standard binds CONTROLLER-as-LEGAL-ENTITY to the on-chain controller address: every stack ends at 'an address controls it'; entity→address attestation (vLEI, KYB attestations, EAS schemas) exists as ingredients but no found standard covers it for agents — the Arcturus identity-api 2-of-3 manual allowlist (11-v0-reconciliation.md O-3) has no standards-based replacement to adopt today.
- No registry-free canonical agent identifier with rotation: did:pkh/did:key can't rotate; did:web depends on DNS; ERC-8004 requires its singleton upgradeable-proxy registry; smart-account address is per-chain. 'Same agent, new key, no shared registry, verifiable continuity' is uncovered.
- Cross-chain revocation is uncovered: revoking an agent key on chain A leaves its authority live on chains B..N in every found mechanism (7702 delegations, session modules, ERC-8004 agentWallet, ERC-1056 delegates are all per-chain).
- Reputation/Sybil resistance for economic agents is effectively uncovered despite ERC-8004 shipping: the only independent measurement found (erc8004-reality-check) shows 0.30% of 34,566 registrations pass a minimal ≥3-unique-reviewer bar and 28.8% of registrations come from one wallet — registration ≠ standing, and no found standard supplies costly-signal identity.
- No standard connects payment authorization to work verification: x402/AP2 authorize payments, ERC-8004 Validation Registry (the piece that would attest work) is unfinished with no mainnet deployment — consistent with the audit charter's 'payments are not proof of work performed'.
- P-256/passkey custody is now verifiable on L1+Berachain (EIP-7951) but there is no standard WebAuthn-validator module interface — each smart-account vendor ships its own envelope parser, so passkey-held agent keys are not portable across account implementations.

### 3.2 Credentials, capability delegation & revocation

| Standard / system | Maturity (exact, quoted) | Registry dependency | Covers | Does not cover |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| W3C Verifiable Credentials Data Model 2.0 (with Data Integrity and VC-JOSE-COSE) | "W3C Recommendation 15 May 2025" — https://www.w3.org/TR/vc-data-model-2.0/ (accessed 2026-08-06). Companion specs vc-data-integrity and vc-jose-cose each also carry "W3C Recommendation 15 May 2025" (https://www.w3.org/TR/vc-data-integrity/, https://www.w3.org/TR/vc-jose-cose/). Spec adds: "W3C recommends the wide deployment of this specification as a standard for the Web." Errata exists. | None for signature verification (issuer public key suffices, e.g. did:key/did:web). Status checking, if credentialStatus is present, requires dereferencing an issuer-hosted status list URL — issuer-availability dependency, not a shared registry. DID method choice can reintroduce a registry (e.g. ledger-based DIDs). | Registry-free verification of signed claims: verification needs only issuer verification material (public key); 'verifiable data registry' is defined as 'a role a system might perform', a logical abstraction, not a mandatory network dependency (https://www.w3.org/TR/vc-data-model-2.0/); Extensible claim vocabulary — could carry 'this agent is authorized by controller X' as a claim; Selective disclosure via Data Integrity / SD-JWT securing mechanisms; credentialStatus extension point for revocation (delegates mechanics to Bitstring Status List) | Capability/delegation semantics: VCDM 2.0 defines claims about subjects, not chained authority-to-act; holder re-delegation and attenuation are not part of the data model; Any native revocation mechanism (delegated entirely to separate status specs); Economic scoping (spend caps, obligation limits) — no standard vocabulary for monetary authority; Enforcement — a VC proves a statement was made; nothing stops an agent acting outside its claimed authority |
| SD-JWT (IETF RFC 9901) and SD-JWT VC (IETF draft) | SD-JWT: "RFC 9901 — RFC - Proposed Standard (November 2025)", formerly draft-ietf-oauth-selective-disclosure-jwt-22 — https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-oauth-selective-disclosure-jwt/ (accessed 2026-08-06). SD-JWT VC: NOT an RFC — "Active Internet-Draft" draft-ietf-oauth-sd-jwt-vc-18 (2026-08-03), WG state "Submitted to IESG for Publication", IESG state "AD Evaluation", intended status "Proposed Standard" — https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-oauth-sd-jwt-vc/ (accessed 2026-08-06). | None for signature verification. Issuer key discovery typically via issuer metadata URL (did/web PKI) — issuer-availability dependency. Status checking requires fetching an issuer-hosted Status List Token. | Selective disclosure of signed claims with holder binding (key binding JWT); Registry-free verification: verifier needs issuer key material; RFC 9901 is a finished IETF standard; Status via companion IETF Token Status List (see Bitstring entry) | The VC-specific profile (SD-JWT VC) is still pre-RFC (AD Evaluation) — treating it as adopted infrastructure would violate its actual status; No delegation/attenuation chains — same claims-not-capabilities limitation as W3C VCs; No economic authority semantics; B2B/economic-claims production adoption: none found beyond government-ID wallet programs |
| W3C Bitstring Status List (revocation) + IETF Token Status List + OCSP-style alternatives + on-chain status registries | Bitstring Status List: "W3C Recommendation" dated "15 May 2025" — https://www.w3.org/TR/vc-bitstring-status-list/ (accessed 2026-08-06). IETF Token Status List: NOT yet an RFC — draft-ietf-oauth-status-list-21 (2026-06-21), IESG state "RFC Ed Queue", RFC Editor state "In Progress", intended "Proposed Standard" — https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-oauth-status-list/ (accessed 2026-08-06). OCSP-analog for credentials (Status Assertions): "Expired Internet-Draft (individual)" — 'not endorsed by the IETF', 'no formal standing', draft-demarco-oauth-status-assertions-03 — https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-demarco-oauth-status-assertions/ (accessed 2026-08-06). | Issuer-hosted URL (not a shared registry) in the base spec; holder-stapling removes the verifier→issuer call at the cost of freshness; cheqd variant deliberately reintroduces a ledger as the status registry. | Revocation ('not reversible') and suspension ('reversible') status purposes for VCs, bit-per-credential in a compressed list of minimum length 131,072 for herd privacy; Registry-free-ish checking: verifier dereferences the statusListCredential URL, but the list can be delivered by the holder ('stapling'), which 'increases privacy for the holder by ensuring that the verifier does not need to contact the issuer'; On-chain anchoring option exists (cheqd DID-Linked Resources) if ledger-grade availability/provenance of the status list is wanted | Immediate revocation with offline verification: stapled lists can be stale; fresh status requires fetching from the issuer-controlled URL — the fundamental freshness-vs-availability tradeoff is not solved, only parameterized; OCSP-style fresh per-credential proofs: the only candidate (Status Assertions) is an expired individual draft with no formal standing; Status of anything other than issuer-asserted credential validity — cannot express 'delegation partially narrowed' |
| UCAN (User Controlled Authorization Networks) | Spec text at https://github.com/ucan-wg/spec displays "Version 1.0.0" (accessed 2026-08-06), BUT the repo has zero GitHub releases ('There aren't any releases here' — https://github.com/ucan-wg/spec/releases) and the latest signed tag is "v1.0-rc.1" (Dec 22, 2024) per https://github.com/ucan-wg/spec/tags. Delegation sub-spec: "Version 1.0.0", envelope tag "ucan/dlg@1.0.0" — https://github.com/ucan-wg/delegation. Revocation sub-spec: "UCAN Revocation Specification v1.0.0-rc.1" (release candidate, RECOMMENDED not REQUIRED) — https://github.com/ucan-wg/revocation (accessed 2026-08-06). Net: community spec, v1.0 substantially settled but revocation still rc; no SDO imprimatur. | None for delegation-chain verification (fully self-contained, offline-capable). Revocation requires a reachable/synced revocation store per resource controller — eventually consistent, no shared registry but no global freshness guarantee either. | Offline-verifiable, chained, attenuated delegation between DIDs with NO registry: 'transfer authority without transferring cryptographic keys'; chain validity = signature + principal alignment ('the aud field of every proof MUST match the iss field') + time bounds (https://github.com/ucan-wg/delegation); Scoping: required `cmd` (command path) + required `pol` policy (predicate logic with ==, inequalities, like-globs, and/or/not, all/any quantifiers over invocation args) + required `exp` expiration — can encode spend-cap-style constraints as policy over invocation arguments; Revocation by any issuer in the proof chain ('An Issuer of a particular Delegation in a proof chain MAY revoke that Delegation'), expressed as a ucan/revoke invocation; revocations 'MUST be immutable and irreversible'; revocation authority itself delegable | Strongly consistent revocation: verifiers learn of revocations from a locally maintained/gossiped revocation store — 'deliberately weak on consistency assumptions'; a third party that hasn't received the revocation will still accept the chain; No standard vocabulary for economic authority (earn/spend/obligation caps are ad-hoc policy encodings, not interoperable semantics); No enforcement rail: UCAN authorizes invocations against a cooperating executor; it cannot bind external systems (banks, chains) that don't speak UCAN; Not an SDO standard; revocation sub-spec still v1.0.0-rc.1; single-digit production adopters found |
| ZCAP-LD / Authorization Capabilities | "Authorization Capabilities v0.4.0-draft" — the document states it 'is currently being drafted to be considered as a work item' for the W3C-CCG; no formal status line, no conformance section, 'v0.5 is not yet planned' — https://w3c-ccg.github.io/zcap-spec/ (accessed 2026-08-06). Pre-standards-track draft. | None for chain verification; revocation checking is application-defined (typically an issuer-operated revocation endpoint). | Object-capability delegation chains with caveats/attenuation over linked-data resources, invoked via HTTP signatures or Data Integrity proofs; revocation via caveats; Registry-free chain verification (same cryptographic-chain model as UCAN) | Any standards-track maturity — early evolving draft with TODOs; Multi-vendor adoption; Economic semantics, enforcement, or consistent revocation (same class of gaps as UCAN, with far less momentum) |
| Biscuit tokens | Eclipse project state: "Incubating" — https://projects.eclipse.org/projects/technology.biscuit (accessed 2026-08-06); spec at v3.3 (feature table v3.0–v3.3; releases 'biscuit v3.3 tooling' 2025-08-06) — https://github.com/eclipse-biscuit/biscuit (accessed 2026-08-06). README notes the project is still 'looking for an audit of the token's design, cryptographic primitives and implementations'. Not an IETF/W3C standard. | None for verification (root public key only). Revocation requires distributing revocation IDs to verifiers — a deny-list channel, not a shared registry. | Offline attenuation: holders can append restriction blocks without issuer involvement ('delegated, decentralized, capabilities based authorization token'); Datalog-based policy language for scoping; third-party blocks (v3.2) allow external attesters to add conditions; Registry-free verification against the root public key; Revocation hooks: every block carries a unique revocation ID that verifiers can check against a deny-list (verifier-side list, not in-token) | Consistent revocation without a verifier-maintained deny-list (same eventual-consistency gap as UCAN); No cryptographic design audit completed (self-declared); No economic-authority vocabulary; no enforcement rail; minimal adoption footprint |
| GNAP (Grant Negotiation and Authorization Protocol) | Core: "RFC 9635 — Grant Negotiation and Authorization Protocol (GNAP)", Proposed Standard, October 2024 — https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/rfc9635/ (accessed 2026-08-06). Resource servers: "RFC 9767 — Grant Negotiation and Authorization Protocol Resource Server Connections", "RFC - Proposed Standard", April 2025 — https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/rfc9767/ (accessed 2026-08-06). | HARD dependency on a central Authorization Server per controller — the strongest registry/central-party coupling in this landscape, in exchange for the strongest revocation consistency. | Delegating authorization to software with negotiated, scoped access tokens, token rotation and revocation, asynchronous/software-only flows ('defines a mechanism for delegating authorization to a piece of software'); Immediate revocation: the AS invalidates tokens instantly (strong consistency); Third-party verification via RS→AS introspection (RFC 9767) | Registry-free third-party verification: the Authorization Server IS the central party — verification and revocation both route through it; without the AS online, nothing is verifiable; Portable proof-of-authority artifacts a third party can verify independently (tokens are opaque or AS-bound); Adoption: GNAP explicitly 'is not an extension of OAuth 2.0 and is not intended to be directly compatible'; found adoption is essentially the Interledger/Open Payments ecosystem |
| ERC-7710 / ERC-7715 + MetaMask Delegation Framework (on-chain delegation with caveats) | ERC-7710 'Smart Contract Delegation': Status "Draft" ('⚠️ Draft', Standards Track: ERC) — https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-7710 (accessed 2026-08-06). ERC-7715 'Request Permissions from Wallets': Status "Draft" — https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-7715 (accessed 2026-08-06). Both are DRAFT ERCs; the deployed MetaMask Delegation Framework contracts are at v1.3.0 (audited tags; audits folder present, firms not named on README page) — https://github.com/MetaMask/delegation-framework (accessed 2026-08-06). | Delegation signatures verifiable offline, but scope enforcement and revocation status require the blockchain (shared global registry). Registry-free verification of CURRENT validity is impossible. | Exactly the economic-agent shape, chain-scoped: off-chain-signed delegations from a controller account to an agent, attenuated by caveat enforcers (spend limits — 'Alice delegates the ability to use her USDC to Bob limiting the amount to 100 USDC'; time limits via TimestampEnforcer; target/method limits via AllowedMethodsEnforcer; value caps via ValueLteEnforcer); Third-party verifiability WITH enforcement: any party can verify a delegation chain's signatures off-chain, and the DelegationManager enforces caveats at redemption — authority to SPEND is not just claimed but mechanically bounded; Revocation: on-chain disabling of delegations through the DelegationManager (strongly consistent once mined) | Registry independence: enforcement and revocation state live on a specific chain — the chain IS the shared registry; nothing is verifiable about revocation status without reading chain state; Off-chain obligations or earnings: scope is on-chain asset actions; 'incur obligations' beyond prefunded assets is out of scope; Standards maturity: both ERCs are Draft — the deployed framework is proprietary-de-facto, not a finalized standard; interop between competing 7710 implementations UNVERIFIED |
| Session keys in ERC-4337/ERC-7579 smart accounts (Smart Sessions, ZeroDev Kernel permissions) | ERC-4337: Status "Final" ('🎉 Final') — https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-4337 (accessed 2026-08-06). ERC-7579 'Minimal Modular Smart Accounts': Status "Draft" — https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-7579 (accessed 2026-08-06). SmartSession module: 'This software is in beta and should be used at your own risk' (README; audits directory exists, joint Rhinestone/Biconomy project) — https://github.com/rhinestonewtf/smartsessions (accessed 2026-08-06). | Blockchain-resident: policies and revocation state live in account storage on-chain. | Controller-to-agent scoped keys with policy bundles (targets, params, gas caps, rate limits, timeframes) enforced by the account contract at validation time — spend-scoped agent authority on-chain; Revocation by uninstalling/disabling the session on the account (chain-consistent) | Registry independence — same chain-as-registry coupling as ERC-7710; Standardization: module interop rests on Draft ERC-7579; policy vocabularies are vendor-specific (Rhinestone vs ZeroDev policies are not interchangeable); Third-party verification of a session's scope without reading chain state and understanding vendor-specific policy encodings |
| Zodiac Roles Modifier (Safe module role-based scoped execution) | No spec-track status (it is a product, not a standard). Contracts audited: "Audits have been performed by the G0 group and Omniscia", 'All identified issues have been resolved as of commit a19c0ebda97f7d645335f2c386818546641f832b' — https://github.com/gnosisguild/zodiac-modifier-roles (accessed 2026-08-06). SDK at v4 (v3→v4 migration guide) — https://docs.roles.gnosisguild.org/ (accessed 2026-08-06). | Blockchain-resident (Safe account storage); no off-chain verifiable artifact exists. | Non-custodial scoped execution authority over a treasury: role members transact on the Safe's behalf only within allowed targets/functions/parameter conditions plus rate limits ('Set limits on how frequently a given role or permission can be used'); Revocation via role revocation/permission changes by the Safe (chain-consistent); This is the closest live pattern to 'agent earns/spends for controller under enforced mandate' in DeFi treasury management | Registry independence — Safe + chain state is the registry; Portability: permissions are per-Safe configuration, not portable credentials; a third party verifies by reading chain state, not by checking a presented artifact; Off-chain actions or obligations |
| Permit2 (Uniswap) — scoped spending capability | Not a standard — a deployed singleton contract (MIT licensed). No EIP status applies. Canonical deployment 0x000000000022D473030F116dDEE9F6B43aC78BA3; 'Permit2 is deployed to the same address across mainnet, Ethereum, Optimism, Arbitrum, Polygon, and Celo' — https://blog.uniswap.org/permit2-integration-guide (accessed 2026-08-06). | Blockchain-resident allowance ledger. | Time-bound, amount-bound spending capability: AllowanceTransfer sets allowances 'on a specified amount for a specified duration of time'; 'Expiring Approvals: Approvals can be time-bound'; one-time SignatureTransfer 'valid only for the duration of the transaction'; Revocation: batch 'Remove allowances on any number of tokens and spenders in one transaction' (lockdown) — https://github.com/Uniswap/permit2 (accessed 2026-08-06) | Delegation chains or attenuation — flat owner→spender grants only; Anything beyond ERC-20 spending; no conditions on WHAT the spend is for; Registry independence — allowance state is chain-resident |
| Ethereum Attestation Service (EAS) | Not a standards-track spec — deployed protocol contracts, versions v0.26–v1.4.x per chain. OP Stack canonicalization: SchemaRegistry (0x4200...0020) and EAS (0x4200...0021) are official predeploys — 'implements the Ethereum Attestation Service protocol', introduced in Bedrock, not deprecated — https://specs.optimism.io/protocol/predeploys.html (accessed 2026-08-06). | HYBRID — this is the precise answer to the registry question: off-chain attestation SIGNATURE verification is registry-independent (EIP-712 check against the signer, needing only the domain config: contract address, version, chain ID). But (a) schema definitions/semantics live in the on-chain SchemaRegistry, (b) revocation of BOTH on-chain and off-chain attestations is recorded in on-chain state (revoke/revokeOffchain), and (c) attester identity/authorization (e.g. Coinbase's resolver-gated schemas) is chain state. So EAS is registry-independent for 'was this signed?' and registry-DEPENDENT for 'is this still valid and does it mean what I think?'. | Schema model: 'Schemas can be registered using the SchemaRegistry.sol contract'; revocability 'controlled at the schema level which defines if attestations made of that schema type can be revoked'; revoking sets 'the attestation's revoked field to true' while the attestation 'retains its history' — https://github.com/ethereum-attestation-service/eas-docs-site (accessed 2026-08-06); Both 'onchain or offchain' attestations; off-chain ones are EIP-712-signed objects 'containing the UID, signature, and attestation data', verified via verifyOffchainAttestationSignature 'rather than querying chain state'; off-chain revocation exists (revokeOffchain) and on-chain timestamping of off-chain UIDs exists — https://github.com/ethereum-attestation-service/eas-sdk (accessed 2026-08-06); Resolver contracts enable gated logic (e.g. restricting attesters, 'attaching payments') | Capability semantics: attestations are statements, not enforceable authority — nothing executes or bounds an agent's spending based on an attestation without custom resolver/consumer contracts; Fully registry-independent operation: see registry_dependency; Cross-chain unification: each chain's SchemaRegistry/EAS instance is separate; a Base attestation is not natively visible on Ethereum |
| GLEIF vLEI (contextual: organizational role credentials) | Operational governance framework at 'EGF version 4.0' with documents dated through March 2026; built on KERI ('Technical Requirements Part 1: KERI Infrastructure', GLEIF Root AID and Delegated AIDs), per Trust-over-IP metamodel — NOT W3C VCs — https://www.gleif.org/en/vlei/introducing-the-verifiable-lei-vlei (accessed 2026-08-06). | KERI witness/watcher infrastructure plus the GLEIF-rooted governance chain — decentralized-verifiable but governance-anchored to GLEIF. | Delegated organizational role authority with revocation, rooted in the LEI system, designed for B2B/regulatory contexts | Economic scoping (no spend/obligation limits in role credentials); W3C VC / IETF interop (KERI/ACDC stack); Software-agent principals (roles are for human representatives) |

**Adoption evidence (with sources):**

- *W3C Verifiable Credentials Data Model 2.0 (with Data Integrity and VC-JOSE-COSE)*:
  - https://www.w3.org/press-releases/2025/verifiable-credentials-2-0/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — 9 company testimonials at REC publication; production-relevant B2B/economic claims: Tradeverifyd (VCs 'at the core' of supply-chain platform), Digital Bazaar ('already deployed these technologies into production environments' affecting tens of millions), Conexxus/NACS TruAge age-verification program, GS1 ('conducted pilots and is implementing cautiously for now'). Testimonials are vendor statements, not audited deployment data.
