  - https://mytruage.org/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — TruAge (retail age verification, W3C-standards based) claims '300+' retail brands and '3M+' age-restricted transactions processed; named users Circle K, TXB, Tiger, Rotten Robbie; POS integrations (Verifone, Clover). This is the strongest non-government production volume claim found, but figures are self-reported on TruAge's site.
  - https://ref.gs1.org/docs/2025/VCs-and-DIDs-tech-landscape (accessed 2026-08-06) — GS1's own Feb-2025 landscape report explicitly recommends AGAINST production commitment: technologies 'are yet to fully mature, stabilise or see widespread adoption' and 'ubiquitous use may be a decade away. Proceed with caution!' — i.e., the flagship B2B supply-chain adopter is still wait-and-see.
  - https://eudi.dev/latest/main/05-data-model-and-data-exchange-protocols/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — EUDI Wallet ARF v3.0.0 lists W3C VCDM v2.0 as 'Optional' and 'meant for non-qualified EAAs only' — the largest regulatory deployment program did NOT make W3C VCs mandatory.
- *SD-JWT (IETF RFC 9901) and SD-JWT VC (IETF draft)*:
  - https://eudi.dev/latest/main/05-data-model-and-data-exchange-protocols/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — EUDI Wallet ARF v3.0.0 (release dated 21 July 2026 per https://eudi.dev/latest/): wallet support for SD-JWT VC is 'Mandatory' ('Wallet Units will support the first two formats above', detailed requirements delegated to Topic 12). This is regulatory-mandated future deployment across EU member states, not yet measured production volume.
  - https://openid.net/specs/openid4vc-high-assurance-interoperability-profile-1_0.html (accessed 2026-08-06) — OpenID4VC High Assurance Interoperability Profile: 'Status: Final' (24 December 2025); wallets/issuers 'MUST support at least one of the following Credential Format Profiles ... IETF SD-JWT VC or ISO mdoc' — a Final interop profile anchoring SD-JWT VC in the OpenID ecosystem.
  - Draft authorship spans production vendors: Terbu (MATTR), Fett (Authlete), Campbell (Ping Identity) — https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-oauth-sd-jwt-vc/ (accessed 2026-08-06). No independent B2B/economic-claims production volume figures found — adoption is predominantly government-ID programs (EUDI). UNVERIFIED beyond this.
- *W3C Bitstring Status List (revocation) + IETF Token Status List + OCSP-style alternatives + on-chain status registries*:
  - https://w3c.github.io/vc-bitstring-status-list-test-suite/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — implementation report run 2 Aug 2026: 8 tested implementations (apicatalog.com, Digital Bazaar, Gen Digital, Mavennet, OpSecId, Procivis One Core, SpruceID, Trential) plus Tradeverifyd self-report. This shows interop conformance, not deployment volume.
  - https://docs.cheqd.io/product/studio/status-lists/bitstring-status-list (accessed 2026-08-06) — cheqd 'stores each Status List and subsequent entries on-ledger as DID-Linked Resource versions' — a live on-chain revocation-registry product for VCs (volume figures: none found).
  - OCSP direction-of-travel evidence: https://letsencrypt.org/2024/12/05/ending-ocsp/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — Let's Encrypt 'will turn off our OCSP responders' on August 6, 2025, moving exclusively to CRLs for privacy ('CAs could be legally compelled to collect' visit data). Web PKI is RETIRING online per-credential status checking in favor of list-based revocation — same architectural direction as Bitstring/Token Status Lists.
- *UCAN (User Controlled Authorization Networks)*:
  - https://github.com/storacha/upload-service (accessed 2026-08-06) — Storacha (successor of web3.storage; web3.storage 301-redirects to storacha.network) 'implements the storacha.network UCAN protocol'; CLI 'verify your email to sync your existing ucan delegations to this agent'; CI keys get delegated 'permissions to it as a proof'. This is a production storage service using UCAN as its authorization layer. Caveat: storacha.network now 301-redirects to fil.one, whose public pages describe S3-API-key auth and do not mention UCAN — current branding/product direction is in flux; UCAN remains the protocol of the upload-service codebase.
  - Bluesky: NO production UCAN use found. Daniel Holmgren (Bluesky) is a UCAN spec author, but https://atproto.com/specs/oauth (accessed 2026-08-06) contains no UCAN mention — atproto uses an OAuth 2.1 profile with mandatory PKCE, DPoP, and PAR. Claiming Bluesky as a UCAN adopter would be false.
  - Implementations exist in TypeScript, Rust, Go (ucan-wg org repos ts-ucan/rs-ucan/go-ucan — https://github.com/ucan-wg, accessed 2026-08-06); production feedback credited to Storacha/DAG House team in spec acknowledgments.
- *ZCAP-LD / Authorization Capabilities*:
  - https://w3c-ccg.github.io/zcap-spec/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — backlog notes v0.3 published 2022 and 'some implementors have made changes to their implementations based on their experience in production'; linked Digital Bazaar libraries ezcap-express / zcapld. No named production deployments, volumes, or third-party integrations found beyond Digital Bazaar's own stack. Effectively single-vendor.
- *Biscuit tokens*:
  - https://github.com/eclipse-biscuit/biscuit (accessed 2026-08-06) — 'Currently deploying to real world use cases such as Apache Pulsar' at Clever Cloud (biscuit-pulsar integration). Implementations: Rust, WASM, Python, Haskell at v3.x; Java/Go/.NET at v1. No other named production users found — adoption evidence is essentially one company (Clever Cloud) plus its Pulsar plugin.
- *GNAP (Grant Negotiation and Authorization Protocol)*:
  - https://openpayments.dev/identity/grants/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — Open Payments (Interledger Foundation) 'leverages the Grant Negotiation and Authorization Protocol (GNAP)' for delegating authorization to client software — a payments-specific API standard, i.e. directly economic delegation.
  - https://rafiki.dev/overview/overview/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — Rafiki is the open-source reference implementation making customer accounts 'Open Payments-enabled' for licensed account servicing entities, stewarded by the Interledger Foundation. Named production ASE deployments: none found on these pages — production footprint UNVERIFIED beyond the existence of the software and standard.
- *ERC-7710 / ERC-7715 + MetaMask Delegation Framework (on-chain delegation with caveats)*:
  - https://github.com/MetaMask/delegation-framework/blob/main/documents/Deployments.md (accessed 2026-08-06) — v1.3.0 deployed via CREATE2 at identical addresses on 24 mainnets (Ethereum, Polygon, BSC, Optimism, Arbitrum One/Nova, Linea, Base, Gnosis, Berachain, Unichain, Ink, Sei, Sonic, Monad, MegaETH, Ronin, Tempo, Celo, Citrea, Mantle, Katana, Intuition); DelegationManager 0xdb9B1e94B5b69Df7e401DDbedE43491141047dB3; ~40 caveat-enforcer contracts deployed (AllowedMethodsEnforcer, TimestampEnforcer, ValueLteEnforcer, ...).
  - https://support.metamask.io/configure/accounts/what-is-a-smart-account/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — 'Smart accounts are now auto-enabled for new MetaMask users'; switching 'points your existing EOA to a MetaMask Delegator smart contract' at 0x63c0c19a282a1b52b07dd5a65b58948a07dae32b — which matches EIP7702StatelessDeleGatorImpl in Deployments.md, i.e. the delegation stack is live in the production MetaMask wallet. Same page cautions listed benefits 'are not all available in MetaMask yet'.
  - https://docs.metamask.io/delegation-toolkit/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — toolkit supports 'Advanced Permissions (ERC-7715), which are fine-grained permissions dapps can request from users directly' via the MetaMask extension. Transaction/redemption volume figures: none found (UNVERIFIED).
- *Session keys in ERC-4337/ERC-7579 smart accounts (Smart Sessions, ZeroDev Kernel permissions)*:
  - https://github.com/rhinestonewtf/smartsessions (accessed 2026-08-06) — 'an advanced module for ERC-7579 compatible smart accounts, enabling granular control over session keys'; 'temporary, limited-permission access to their accounts through configurable policies'; supports ERC-7715 permission requests. Deployment/usage volume: none found (UNVERIFIED).
  - https://docs.zerodev.app/sdk/permissions/intro (accessed 2026-08-06) — ZeroDev Kernel: 'Permission = 1 signer + N policies + 1 action'; built-in policies: call policy ('you can only call certain contracts or functions (and only with certain params)'), gas policy, rate limit policy, timestamp policy, signature policy. Production volume claims: none found on cited page (UNVERIFIED).
- *Zodiac Roles Modifier (Safe module role-based scoped execution)*:
  - https://github.com/gnosisguild/zodiac-modifier-roles (accessed 2026-08-06) — deployed on Mainnet, Optimism, Gnosis, Polygon, Arbitrum, Base and others; capabilities: 'Scope which functions a role can call on given address', 'Define conditions on function parameters', execution via execTransactionWithRole().
  - https://github.com/karpatkey (accessed 2026-08-06) — karpatkey/kpk ('Scalable onchain asset management') maintains defi-kit: 'Permissions for Zodiac Roles covering interactions with DeFi protocols' and client-configs ('hosts roles permissions and other configurations for clients') — a professional treasury manager operating client mandates through Zodiac Roles permissions. AUM figures and named DAO clients (Balancer/ENS/GnosisDAO): UNVERIFIED — kpk.io returned 403 and the Roles app/docs name no users.
- *Permit2 (Uniswap) — scoped spending capability*:
  - https://docs.0x.org/evm/0x-swap-api/additional-topics/how-to-set-your-token-allowances.md (accessed 2026-08-06) — 0x Swap API has a dedicated /swap/permit2 endpoint; 'ONLY set allowances on AllowanceHolder or Permit2 contracts'; spender in example quote is 0x...ac78ba3 (Permit2). Third-party production integration confirmed. Notably 0x labels Permit2 '(Advanced Use Only)' vs AllowanceHolder '(Recommended)'.
  - https://blog.uniswap.org/permit2-integration-guide (accessed 2026-08-06) — Uniswap's own Universal Router integration ('Permit2 is already improving Uniswap's product UX').
  - Aggregate transaction volume: UNVERIFIED (Etherscan blocked, 403; no Dune figure retrievable).
- *Ethereum Attestation Service (EAS)*:
  - Deployment footprint: mainnet contracts on Ethereum (v0.26), Optimism + Base (v1.0.1 predeploys), Arbitrum One (v0.26), Arbitrum Nova/Polygon/Scroll/zkSync/Celo/Blast (v1.3.0), Linea (v1.2.0), Telos (v1.4.0), Soneium/Ink/Unichain (v1.4.1-beta.1) — https://github.com/ethereum-attestation-service/eas-contracts README (accessed 2026-08-06).
  - Attestation counts from official explorers (accessed 2026-08-06): Base https://base.easscan.org/ — 'Total Attestations: 3,465,775', 'Total Schemas: 1,752', 'Unique Attestors: 2,290'; Optimism https://optimism.easscan.org/ — '1,318,168' attestations, '835' schemas, '20,689' attestors; Ethereum mainnet https://easscan.org/ — '14,342' attestations ('13,325' of them offchain), '398' schemas, '822' attestors; Arbitrum https://arbitrum.easscan.org/ — '113,180' attestations. Activity is overwhelmingly on OP-Stack L2s, not Ethereum L1.
  - Production attester: Coinbase Verifications — 'a set of Coinbase-verified onchain attestations' on Base via EAS ('included as a predeploy in the OP Stack'); Verified Account / Verified Country / Coinbase One schemas with published schema IDs and an Indexer contract (0x2c7eE1E5f416dfF40054c27A62f7B357C4E8619C on Base mainnet) — https://github.com/coinbase/verifications (accessed 2026-08-06).
- *GLEIF vLEI (contextual: organizational role credentials)*:
  - https://www.gleif.org/en/vlei/introducing-the-verifiable-lei-vlei (accessed 2026-08-06) — five credential types including Official Organizational Role and Engagement Context Role credentials, plus an Authorization vLEI Credential letting a representative 'authorize issuance/revocation of role credentials'. Issuer/credential counts: none found on page (UNVERIFIED). Included because it is the only found production-track framework for third-party-verifiable ORGANIZATIONAL authority-of-representatives — the closest existing analog to 'controller grants agent verifiable authority', albeit human-role-focused and KERI-based.

**Coverage gaps found in this domain (conservative statements):**

- No adopted standard expresses ECONOMIC scope (spend caps, earn caps, obligation ceilings, per-counterparty limits) as an interoperable vocabulary in a portable credential/capability. UCAN `pol` predicates and Biscuit Datalog CAN encode such limits ad hoc; on-chain caveat enforcers (ValueLteEnforcer etc.), session-key policies, Zodiac conditions, and Permit2 allowances ENFORCE them — but only chain-residently and with vendor-specific encodings. There is no shared schema a third-party verifier can interpret across systems.
- The KEY QUESTION is only partially satisfiable, and by combination rather than any single standard: registry-free verifiable scoped delegation exists (UCAN v1.0/Biscuit v3.3 — cryptographic chain verification with attenuation, no registry), but REVOCATION without a shared registry is only eventually consistent (UCAN revocation stores/gossip, Biscuit deny-lists). Every strongly consistent revocation mechanism found reintroduces a dependency: an issuer-hosted status URL (Bitstring/Token Status List), a central AS (GNAP), or a blockchain (ERC-7710 DelegationManager, EAS revocations, Zodiac, Permit2). No found standard achieves fresh revocation + offline third-party verification + no shared registry simultaneously; short expirations (`exp` in UCAN, Permit2 expiring approvals) are the only registry-free approximation.
- 'Incur obligations' is uncovered everywhere: all enforcement-capable systems (Permit2, session keys, ERC-7710 caveats, Zodiac Roles) bound spending of EXISTING assets. No found standard lets an agent verifiably bind its controller to FUTURE liabilities (credit, debt, contractual commitments) with third-party-checkable scope. The nearest analogs — GNAP grants (AS-mediated) and vLEI role credentials (human organizational roles, no monetary scope) — do not close this.
- 'Earn' authority (third party verifying an agent is authorized to perform revenue-generating work such that payment obligations bind the controller) has no standard at all; it is implicitly the legal-agency problem, and neither the VC stack (claims without enforcement) nor the capability stack (enforcement without legal semantics) addresses it.
- No standardized bridge exists between the off-chain capability ecosystem (UCAN/ZCAP/Biscuit — registry-free but unenforced) and the on-chain enforcement ecosystem (ERC-7710/7579/Permit2 — enforced but chain-bound). ERC-7710 delegations are structurally UCAN-like (signed, attenuated, redeemable) but share no format, verification procedure, or revocation channel with any off-chain capability standard.
- Holder re-delegation is not defined in the W3C VC Data Model 2.0 — the claims stack and the capability stack remain disjoint, so 'credentialed agent identity + delegated economic capability + coherent revocation across both' requires gluing at least three systems (e.g. VC/EAS attestation for agent identity + UCAN/7710 for capability + status list/chain for revocation) with no standard describing the composition.
- Cross-chain and cross-registry portability: EAS instances are per-chain with no native aggregation; MetaMask delegations and session keys are enforceable only on the chain holding the state; issuer-hosted status lists are per-issuer. Any multi-rail economic agent must verify against multiple registries with no standard for combining their answers.
- Maturity asymmetry as a gap in itself: the pieces with real adoption volume are either claims-only (EAS: 3.4M+ attestations on Base; VC family: W3C REC since May 2025 with government/age-verification adoption) or chain-bound enforcement (MetaMask delegation contracts on 24 mainnets, auto-enabled in the production wallet; Zodiac audited and used by professional treasury managers), while the registry-free capability specs (UCAN rc-tagged/1.0, ZCAP v0.4.0-draft, Biscuit Eclipse-Incubating-unaudited) have single-digit identified production adopters. The architecturally correct primitives for registry-free agent authority are precisely the least adopted.

### 3.3 Agent communication & machine payments

| Standard / system | Maturity (exact, quoted) | Registry dependency | Covers | Does not cover |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A2A / Agent2Agent Protocol | "Latest Released Version 1.0.0" — https://a2a-protocol.org/latest/specification/ (accessed 2026-08-06). GitHub releases: v1.0.0 published 2026-03-12, v1.0.1 published 2026-05-28 (api.github.com/repos/a2aproject/A2A/releases, accessed 2026-08-06). Repo states: "The A2A Protocol is an open source project under the Linux Foundation, contributed by Google" (github.com/a2aproject/A2A, accessed 2026-08-06). | None. AgentCard is self-published by each server; no central registry required by spec. | AgentCard: JSON metadata document (identity, capabilities, skills, endpoints, auth) incl. card signing (spec §4.4); Task lifecycle states (SUBMITTED/WORKING/COMPLETED/FAILED/CANCELED/INPUT_REQUIRED/REJECTED/AUTH_REQUIRED); Message parts (text/bytes/file URL/structured data), streaming, push notifications, multi-turn contextId/taskId; Bindings: JSON-RPC, gRPC, HTTP+JSON/REST; security schemes OAuth2/API key/mTLS/OIDC; extension mechanism (§4.6) | NO payment/economic semantics in core spec (spec page silent on payments/pricing; confirmed by direct read, accessed 2026-08-06); No receipts, no escrow, no outcome verification; Payments only via the separate a2a-x402 extension (google-agentic-commerce/a2a-x402): 'A2A Protocol: x402 Payments Extension v0.1' — taskId correlation lives in A2A Message metadata, NOT inside the signed PaymentPayload (spec/v0.1/spec.md: 'This message MUST include the taskId ... so the Merchant can correlate the payment'; PaymentPayload §5.3 has no taskId field). 550 GitHub stars; no production deployments found. |
| MCP / Model Context Protocol | "The current protocol version is 2026-07-28" — https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/versioning (accessed 2026-08-06). Revisions marked Draft/Current/Final; 2026-07-28 is Current. | None for protocol operation; optional MCP Registry for server discovery (status UNVERIFIED). | Resources, Prompts, Tools (server features); Elicitation (client feature); JSON-RPC 2.0 base; per-request capability negotiation; Optional extensions: Tasks (io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks, SEP-2663, being stabilized for core promotion per Agents WG charter), Skills over MCP, MCP Apps; Version negotiation via _meta protocolVersion key and MCP-Protocol-Version header | NO native economic semantics: no payments, receipts, pricing, or billing anywhere in spec or official extensions list (spec + extensions overview read directly, accessed 2026-08-06). No payments working group exists (working-groups page enumerated, accessed 2026-08-06).; Payment is layered on by third parties: Vercel x402-mcp ('We built x402-mcp to integrate x402 payments with MCP servers and the Vercel AI SDK', vercel.com/blog/introducing-x402-mcp), civicteam/x402-mcp, Coinbase CDP 'MCP Server with x402' guide (docs.cdp.coinbase.com/x402/mcp-server) — all found via search 2026-08-06; none are official MCP extensions.; No receipt or outcome-verification concept. |
| x402 | "Protocol Version: 2" — specs/x402-specification-v2.md, v2.0 dated 2025-12-9 in version history (raw.githubusercontent.com/x402-foundation/x402/main/specs/x402-specification-v2.md, accessed 2026-08-06). x402.org FAQ claims 'production-ready and has processed millions of transactions' (accessed 2026-08-06). Offer/receipt extension: v0.6 (2026-02-04), 'First approved release' v0.5 (2026-01-29); 'Wire shape and field placement are not considered stable'. | Facilitator is OPTIONAL by spec (resource server can verify/settle itself) but in practice Coinbase's facilitator processes ~83% of indexed 24h transactions (x402scan, 2026-08-06). Bazaar discovery relies on facilitator-operated catalogs. Receipt verification may require external key-authorization registries (did:web, DNS TXT) per extension text. | HTTP 402 flow: PAYMENT-REQUIRED response header (b64 PaymentRequired object), PAYMENT-SIGNATURE request header, PAYMENT-RESPONSE settlement header (v2 naming; spec + x402-foundation README, accessed 2026-08-06); WHO SIGNS WHAT: client/payer signs an EIP-712 EIP-3009 transferWithAuthorization with EXACTLY {from, to, value, validAfter, validBefore, nonce} (scheme_exact_evm.md, accessed 2026-08-06). Facilitator is 'the transaction broadcaster' — submits transferWithAuthorization on-chain, pays gas, 'cannot modify the amount or destination'. SettlementResponse: {success (req), transaction = 'Blockchain transaction hash' (req), network CAIP-2 (req), payer (opt)}.; RECEIPTS (optional extension only): extension-offer-and-receipt.md — resource server (seller) signs both artifacts; offer payload {version, resourceUrl, scheme, network, asset, payTo, amount, validUntil?}; receipt payload {version, network, resourceUrl, payer, issuedAt, transaction?}; formats eip712 or jws; 'designed to be portable and independently verifiable'; receipt issued 'only on success, confirming that payment was received and service was delivered'; 16 chain scheme specs (EVM, SVM, Sui, TON, Aptos, Stellar, XRPL, etc.); Bazaar discovery extension (facilitator-operated catalogs, decentralized in principle); payment_identifier idempotency extension | PAYMENT↔TASK BINDING: the signed EIP-3009 authorization contains ONLY payer/payee/amount/time-window/nonce — the resource URL sits OUTSIDE the signature ('Nothing binds the signature to a specific resource URL or task'; PaymentPayload.resource is optional). Payment proves transfer, not work.; RECEIPT GAPS: receipt has NO amount field ('privacy-minimal'; amount only recoverable via on-chain lookup of optional transaction hash) and NO explicit payee field (payee implied via signer-key authorization). Full (payer,payee,task,amount) binding requires combining offer+receipt, and the extension is optional + wire-unstable. Receipt is seller-signed self-attestation of delivery — no third-party attestation of outcome quality.; No escrow, no refunds/disputes in core, no outcome verification. |
| L402 (Lightning HTTP 402) | No formal standards status — community spec repo with 'Protocol Specification', 'Macaroon Technical Specification', 'Agent Specification' docs (github.com/lightninglabs/L402, accessed 2026-08-06; last commit 2026-03-20). Lightning Labs blog 2026-03-11: 'The L402 protocol is production-ready.' Not an IETF RFC; references RFC 7231's 402 code only. | None — self-verifiable bearer credential; requires Lightning Network liquidity/channels as infrastructure dependency. | 402 challenge: WWW-Authenticate: L402 macaroon="<base64>", invoice="<bolt11>"; retry with Authorization: L402 macaroon:preimage; Proof of payment: 'The preimage is cryptographic proof of payment'; macaroon 'commits to the invoice by containing its payment hash'; stateless server verification ('no database, no session'); macaroon attenuation for scoped sub-credentials (caveats, e.g. volume limits) | Receipt is a BEARER credential: macaroon+preimage proves payment happened for that payment hash but identifies no payer, no task description, no outcome; not a portable third-party receipt binding parties to work; No escrow, no outcome verification, no dispute mechanism; amount binding only via the invoice; Adoption remains essentially confined to Lightning Labs' own products on evidence found |
| AP2 / Agent Payments Protocol | v0.2 — GitHub releases: v0.1.0 published 2025-09-16, v0.2.0 published 2026-04-28 (api.github.com/repos/google-agentic-commerce/AP2/releases, accessed 2026-08-06). docs/overview.md self-describes as 'this proposed protocol' with no formal status line. v0.2 adds 'Human Not Present' autonomous payments. Positioned as 'an extension to the A2A protocol and the Universal Commerce Protocol'. | No registry described in spec; relies on role trust — Credential Providers and Trusted Surfaces are trusted intermediaries; FIDO Alliance now owns standardization. | MANDATE MODEL (current terms per site: Checkout Mandate + Payment Mandate as Verifiable Digital Credentials, each with Open/Closed stages; earlier Intent/Cart terminology appears in docs/overview.md concepts): USER signs the Checkout Mandate via a 'Trusted Surface' with user authentication (biometric for human-not-present) — 'signed approval must create a cryptographically signed Checkout Mandate' containing 'the explicit goods being purchased & their confirmation of purchase'; MERCHANT 'must sign the Cart that they create for a user, signaling that they will fulfill this cart'; Payment Mandate 'may be shared with the network & issuer' — its signer is NOT explicitly specified in overview.md (gap in spec text as read 2026-08-06); Verification/consumption: Merchant retains Checkout Mandate 'as evidence in case of disputes'; Merchant Payment Processor verifies credential authorization; networks/issuers receive 'AI agent presence signals'; Roles: Shopping Agent, Credential Provider, Merchant, Merchant Payment Processor, Trusted Surface, Network/Issuer (roles may combine); Human-not-present flow: Checkout Mandate 'Open' while agent tries to meet user constraints (price/quantity limits), 'Closed' on match | TASK↔PAYMENT binding is authorization-side only: mandates bind payment authorization to goods/constraints and merchant-signed cart commits fulfillment, but 'there is no described mechanism that conditions payment settlement on verified task completion or delivery' (overview.md, accessed 2026-08-06). Dispute evidence, not settlement condition.; Payment Mandate signer under-specified in public docs; verification chain not fully spelled out; No measured volume; zero production deployments found |
| ACP / Agentic Commerce Protocol (OpenAI + Stripe) | "is currently in `beta`" with badge 'Status-Beta'; date-based versioning, latest stable release 2026-04-17 (README, accessed 2026-08-06). Individual RFCs carry own statuses: rfc.agentic_checkout.md '**Status:** Draft' (Version 2026-01-16); rfc.delegate_payment.md '**Status:** Draft' (Version 2025-09-29); rfc.intent_traces.md '**Status:** Proposal' (2025-12-16). Repo: 1,500 stars, pushed 2026-07-18. | Heavy platform dependence: merchants apply per agent-platform (ChatGPT); OpenAI is currently the only agent platform, Stripe the first PSP (Shared Payment Token). Not registry-based but gatekept by platform onboarding. | Agentic Checkout Spec (checkout session REST API), Delegated Payment Spec, Product Feed Spec; OpenAPI YAML + JSON Schemas; capability negotiation, payment handlers, orders, discounts RFCs; EVIDENCE TRAIL (rfc.agentic_checkout.md, accessed 2026-08-06): client SHOULD sign requests — 'Signature: <base64url>' header over canonical JSON + 'Timestamp' header (RECOMMENDED, not required; Ed25519/ES256); order object {id, checkout_session_id, permalink_url} — no signature/hash fields; webhooks (order_create/order_update) HMAC-authenticated via 'Merchant-Signature'; Idempotency-Key REQUIRED on POSTs; Delegated payment token binding (rfc.delegate_payment.md): vault token 'MUST ONLY be usable within the provided Allowance' = {reason MUST be one_time, max_amount, currency, checkout_session_id, merchant_id, expires_at} — scopes a payment credential to one checkout at one merchant with amount cap | NO signed receipt object anywhere in spec ('No signed receipt object is defined anywhere in the spec' — checkout RFC read 2026-08-06). Order object is unsigned merchant-side state; merchant is 'system of record'.; NO cryptographic binding of payment to cart contents/totals ('nothing binds the token or cryptogram to the cart contents, totals, or session hash'); Intent traces are unsigned advisory analytics ('not signed, and no verification mechanism is defined'), cancellation-only; No outcome/delivery verification; disputes ride existing card rails |
| Task/payment binding systems (escrow + verified-work): Virtuals Agent Commerce Protocol; x402 offer-and-receipt; adjacent verifiable-compute schemes | Virtuals ACP: "ACP v2.0 ... after 18 months in production and over 2,000 agents onboarded" (April 2026 changelog, whitepaper.virtuals.io queried 2026-08-06). x402 offer-and-receipt: v0.6 (2026-02-04), first approved v0.5 (2026-01-29), 'Wire shape and field placement are not considered stable'. zkML proof-of-inference payment binding: NO adopted standard found — vendor/blog claims only (e.g. Inference Labs 'live on testnet with mainnet eyed for late Q3 2025', decentralizedinference.org, accessed 2026-08-06 — low-authority source, UNVERIFIED). | Virtuals ACP: depends on Virtuals platform contracts + evaluator marketplace (platform-centralized governance over a decentralized chain). zkML schemes: N/A (not deployed). | Virtuals ACP: on-chain escrow + signed Proof of Agreement + evaluator-gated release = closest existing production binding of (payer, payee, agreed task, amount, evaluated outcome), recorded on-chain on Base; x402 offer-and-receipt: seller-signed portable artifacts (see x402 entry) — partial binding, no escrow; a2a-x402 v0.1: taskId correlation between A2A task and x402 payment at message level (unsigned linkage); L402: preimage = proof-of-payment bound to payment hash (not to work output) | NO neutral standard (W3C/IETF/LF) exists that binds signed payment to cryptographically verified work/outcome. Conservative statement: everything found is either (a) platform escrow with human/agent evaluators (Virtuals ACP — attestation-based), (b) payment-proof without work-proof (x402, L402, ACP, AP2), or (c) research/vendor-stage verifiable compute (zkML: EZKL/Giza/Modulus/Gensyn named in secondary sources; no production payment-conditioned-on-proof-of-inference deployment verified; streaming-payment protocols like Sablier/Superfluid stream by TIME not outcome — status not re-verified, UNVERIFIED).; Proof-of-inference-gated settlement: no measured production volume found anywhere; all sources encountered were blogs/marketing (per epistemic rules: not treated as deployments) |

**Adoption evidence (with sources):**

- *A2A / Agent2Agent Protocol*:
  - https://a2a-protocol.org/latest/community/ (accessed 2026-08-06) — lists frameworks with 'built-in A2A integration': Google ADK, Microsoft Agent Framework, LangGraph, CrewAI, Pydantic AI, LiteLLM, Agno, AG2, BeeAI, Strands Agents; cites Microsoft LinkedIn announcement 'Semantic Kernel now speaks A2A'. NOTE: page cites ZERO production deployments — use cases framed as 'examples of what's possible'.
  - https://www.linuxfoundation.org/press/linux-foundation-launches-the-agent2agent-protocol-project... (accessed 2026-08-06) — LF launch 2025-06-23; named supporters AWS, Cisco, Salesforce, SAP, Microsoft, ServiceNow; 'more than 100 technology companies' claim is unverified marketing.
  - github.com/a2aproject/A2A: 25.2k stars, 2.5k forks (accessed 2026-08-06) — developer interest signal only, not deployment evidence.
  - No measured transaction/message volume for A2A found anywhere. UNVERIFIED whether any named framework integration carries production traffic.
- *MCP / Model Context Protocol*:
  - Massive tooling ecosystem is common knowledge but NOT re-verified here with volume data; treated as directionally true given working groups staffed by AWS/Microsoft (https://modelcontextprotocol.io/community/working-groups, accessed 2026-08-06, Agents WG charter 2026-08-04 lists AWS and Microsoft leads).
  - MCP Registry exists at registry.modelcontextprotocol.io but its GA/preview status could not be extracted (page body empty on fetch, accessed 2026-08-06) — UNVERIFIED.
- *x402*:
  - MEASURED VOLUME (conflicting sources, both accessed 2026-08-06): x402.org homepage 'Last 30 Days': 75.41M transactions, $24.24M volume, 94.06K buyers, 22K sellers. x402scan.com homepage 'Past 30 Days': 11.63M transactions, $781.39K volume, 18.67K buyers, 82K sellers. ~6.5x transaction and ~31x volume discrepancy between the protocol's own site and the independent explorer is UNRESOLVED (likely different indexing coverage); treat x402.org numbers as self-reported.
  - CHAIN SPLIT (x402scan.com/networks, 'Past 24 Hours', accessed 2026-08-06): Base 223,959 txns / $23.97K / 16 facilitators / 825 sellers / 2,241 buyers; Solana 10,374 txns / $10.13K / 3 facilitators. Base carries ~95% of indexed tx count. Note: average tx value is tiny (~$0.10 Base, ~$1 Solana).
  - DEPLOYED FACILITATORS (x402scan.com/facilitators, 'Past 24 Hours', accessed 2026-08-06): Coinbase 193,846 txns/$21.04K; FluxA 28,214/$1.19K; PayAI 6,680/$199.89; Dexter 4,481/$210.92; Meridian 288/$11.01K; Polymer 193; Primer 188; Cascade 153; AnySpend 139. Coinbase facilitator ≈83% of 24h indexed transactions — de facto centralization.
  - x402.org 'Trusted By' logos: Alchemy, AWS, Cloudflare, Messari, Nansen, QuickNode, Stripe, Vercel, World (accessed 2026-08-06) — logo wall, depth of integration unverified per-name; Vercel's x402-mcp and Coinbase CDP docs are concrete integrations.
  - Ecosystem services live on x402scan, e.g. BlockRun ('14M+ on-chain transactions ... settled in USDC on Base & Solana', self-description on x402scan homepage, accessed 2026-08-06 — self-reported).
- *L402 (Lightning HTTP 402)*:
  - https://lightning.engineering/posts/2026-03-11-L402-for-agents/ (accessed 2026-08-06): 'Lightning Loop has used L402 via Aperture (the L402-aware reverse proxy) since the initial release five years ago.' — one first-party production deployment, NO volume numbers anywhere.
  - https://l402.tech/ (accessed 2026-08-06): Aperture 'Used in production by Lightning Loop and Pool'; lists lnget, L402sdk (Rust/WASM/Go/Python/npm), Wavelength wallet, LangChain integration (2023). All Lightning-Labs-adjacent; no independent volume data.
  - Repo signals modest: 90 stars, 19 forks (accessed 2026-08-06). No measured transaction volume found — UNVERIFIED beyond 'Loop uses it'.
- *AP2 / Agent Payments Protocol*:
  - NO production deployments found. ap2-protocol.org (accessed 2026-08-06) offers code samples only (human-present/not-present cards, x402 scenarios, Android digital-payment-credentials sample) — 'specification-plus-samples stage'.
  - Google blog 2026-04-28 (accessed 2026-08-06) names NO merchants/banks/platforms running AP2; only concrete partner is Mastercard as co-developer of the separate 'Verifiable Intent' standard (also being donated to FIDO). 'Growing support from leaders across the industry' — no names, no usage evidence.
  - Repo: 3,135 stars, pushed 2026-06-17 (accessed 2026-08-06).
- *ACP / Agentic Commerce Protocol (OpenAI + Stripe)*:
  - PRODUCTION: OpenAI Instant Checkout live in ChatGPT — 'U.S. ChatGPT Plus, Pro, and Free users can now buy directly from U.S. Etsy sellers right in chat, with over a million Shopify merchants, like Glossier, SKIMS, Spanx and Vuori, coming soon' (OpenAI blog snippet via search results incl. cnbc.com/2026/03/20/open-ai-agentic-shopping-etsy-shopify-walmart-amazon.html, accessed 2026-08-06; openai.com blocked direct fetch 403). Single-item purchases only at launch.
  - agenticcommerce.dev (accessed 2026-08-06): 'OpenAI is the first AI platform to implement ACP with ChatGPT'; 'Stripe is the first compatible PSP with its Shared Payment Token'. Shopify partnership confirmed by shopify.com/news/shopify-open-ai-commerce (search snippet, accessed 2026-08-06).
  - NO transaction volume figures published anywhere found. Merchants must apply per-platform — implementing ACP does not automatically list products with any agent.
- *Task/payment binding systems (escrow + verified-work): Virtuals Agent Commerce Protocol; x402 offer-and-receipt; adjacent verifiable-compute schemes*:
  - Virtuals ACP live on Base mainnet (chain 8453): ACP Core contract 0x238E541BfefD82238730D00a2208E5497F1832E0, FundTransferHook 0x90717828D78731313CB350D6a58b0f91668Ea702 (whitepaper.virtuals.io, accessed 2026-08-06). '2,000+ agents onboarded' is self-reported; Dune dashboard (dune.com/virtual_protocol/virtual-protocol-on-base/4d3ae4ed-...) failed to render in fetch — per-transaction volume UNVERIFIED.
  - Virtuals ACP mechanism (whitepaper.virtuals.io/about-virtuals/commerce-layer.md, accessed 2026-08-06): 'Payments and deliverables are held in escrow until an Evaluator verifies the work' against a 'cryptographically signed Proof of Agreement'; four phases Request/Negotiation/Transaction/Evaluation; roles Client/Provider/Evaluator; 'a market for specialized evaluation agents'. This is the ONLY found production system conditioning payment release on third-party work evaluation. Caveat: evaluator competence/incentives are not a cryptographic guarantee — evaluation is attestation, not proof.
  - Skyfire (skyfire.xyz, accessed 2026-08-06): identity (KYA) + payment tokens + user mandates; 'Nothing on the page describes verifying work or outcomes before payment'; demo transactions all show status 'Awaiting'; no volume stats — NOT a task/payment binder.
  - Nevermined (nevermined.app, via search 2026-08-06): commercial 'agentic payments platform' with x402/MCP/A2A access, payment verification + metering — merchant-side infrastructure, no outcome verification claimed; volume UNVERIFIED.

**Coverage gaps found in this domain (conservative statements):**

- No standard produces a single signed, portable, third-party-verifiable receipt binding ALL of (payer, payee, task, amount, outcome). Closest composite: x402 offer (payee+amount+resource, seller-signed) + x402 receipt (payer+resource+success, seller-signed, NO amount, NO explicit payee) + on-chain tx hash — three artifacts, optional extension, wire-format unstable, and 'outcome' is seller self-attestation. Virtuals ACP binds all five via escrow+evaluator but is platform-specific, not a neutral standard, and outcome is evaluator attestation rather than proof.
- In every payment protocol audited (x402 EIP-3009 auth, L402 preimage, ACP delegated token, AP2 mandates), the payer's signature authorizes a TRANSFER (payee, amount, time window) — the task/resource identity is outside the signed payload or absent entirely. x402 explicitly: resource field sits alongside, not inside, the signed authorization. Payment evidence is therefore never proof of work performed.
- No protocol conditions SETTLEMENT on verified outcome except Virtuals ACP's evaluator escrow; AP2's merchant-signed cart is dispute evidence only; x402/L402/ACP settle before or independent of delivery verification.
- Agent communication standards (A2A 1.0, MCP 2026-07-28) carry zero economic semantics natively; the bridges (a2a-x402 v0.1, x402-mcp community libs) link payment to task by unsigned message-level correlation (taskId in metadata), so the task↔payment link is not cryptographically portable.
- Receipt verification key-authorization is unsolved: x402 offer/receipt verifiers must establish signer authorization for a resourceUrl via payTo-key matching, did:web, DNS TXT, or 'external registries' — no operational registry found; post-key-rotation verification is specified only as guidance.
- Volume/adoption measurement is unreliable: x402.org self-reports 75.41M txns/$24.24M (30d) vs independent x402scan's 11.63M/$781.39K (30d) — unresolved 6.5x/31x discrepancy; A2A, AP2, L402, and OpenAI ACP publish no volume data at all.
- De facto centralization contradicts open-protocol framing: Coinbase facilitator ≈83% of x402scan-indexed 24h transactions; OpenAI is the only ACP agent platform and Stripe the only PSP; merchants must apply per-platform.
- AP2 spec gap: the Payment Mandate's signer is not explicitly specified in public docs (overview.md), and no production deployment exists 10+ months after v0.1.
- No found standard covers refunds, partial delivery, quality disputes, or clawback tied to outcome for machine-to-machine payments; ACP defers to card rails, x402/L402 have nothing.
- Verifiable-compute payment (proof-of-inference-gated settlement) remains unshipped as far as could be verified: zkML tooling exists but no production system paying against cryptographic proof of specific inference work was found (all claims were vendor blogs — UNVERIFIED).

### 3.4 Economic history, receipts-as-credit-evidence & underwriting landscape

| Standard / system | Maturity (exact, quoted) | Registry dependency | Covers | Does not cover |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ERC-8004 Trustless Agents (Identity/Reputation/Validation registries) | "⚠️ Draft" — Standards Track: ERC, created 2025-08-13 (https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-8004, accessed 2026-08-06). Contracts ARE deployed on mainnets despite Draft spec status; repo README warns the Validation Registry spec "is still under active update and discussion with the TEE community" (https://github.com/erc-8004/erc-8004-contracts, accessed 2026-08-06) | Per-chain singleton registry contracts (deterministic vanity deployments); permissionless writes, no central operator, but canonical addresses curated by the 8004 team; contracts are upgradeable (upgrade-key holders are a trust dependency) | Portable on-chain agent identity (ERC-721 token + agentURI registration file); Public, tamper-evident feedback trail (giveFeedback with score, tags, URI+hash); feedback files MAY reference x402 payment proofs; Validation request/response interface (0–100 scores; zkML/TEE/re-execution envisioned) | Payments are explicitly orthogonal — feedback is NOT bound to verified payment or verified work performed; No Sybil resistance; empirically the reputation data is majority-Sybil and evidence-free (see arXiv study); No cash-flow/revenue history; no repayment or credit events; no obligor identity/KYC; Validation Registry unfinished (no published mainnet address; spec under revision) |
| Recall (recall.network) — tokenized AI skill market / AgentRank | Live product, self-described "The world's first decentralized skill market for AI" (https://docs.recall.network/, accessed 2026-08-06); RECALL token launched ~2025-09-24 per blog post "$RECALL: Skill Markets for AI" (https://blog.recall.network/, accessed 2026-08-06). No formal standard; proprietary platform. | Platform-operated competitions and ranking; on-chain token but ranking pipeline is Recall-operated | Benchmark-style agent performance reputation from sandboxed competitions (paper/spot/perps trading); Token-staked prediction/boosting on agents | Real commercial revenue or receipts — competition results are simulated-task performance, not economic history of actual paid work; Portability/third-party verifiability of the AgentRank score outside Recall's platform: UNVERIFIED (docs pages fetched did not state where results are stored or whether signed) |
| Virtuals Protocol — Agent Commerce Protocol (ACP) / ERC-8183 | In production: "ACP v2.0 launched April 2026 after 18 months in production and over 2,000 agents onboarded; introduces a Hook-based architecture and ERC-8183 reference implementation" (https://whitepaper.virtuals.io/acp/acp-changelogs, via search snippet, accessed 2026-08-06). ERC-8183 status as an ERC: UNVERIFIED (not checked against eips.ethereum.org). | Depends on Virtuals' ACP contracts and evaluator-agent set; EVM-based (Base ~97% of message volume per governance snippet) | Closest existing thing to on-chain agent economic history: escrowed job payments + third-party evaluator verdicts recorded per job ID on Base, feeding persistent agent reputation; Smart-contract escrow releasing payment on evaluator approval (weak proof-of-work-performed, bound to payment) | Portability outside the Virtuals ecosystem — reputation is a Virtuals-platform construct, not a neutral cross-platform artifact; Volume quality: significant portion incentive-driven; 73% of Genesis agents fail a 30-day $250K liquidity floor per governance forum snippet (virtualsvotes.com, accessed 2026-08-06); No credit/repayment events; evaluator quality/collusion resistance unproven |
| Skyfire — KYAPay (agent identity + payment JWTs) | Company product live; protocol status is IETF "Internet-Draft" (draft-skyfire-oauth-kyapay-token-00 et al. on datatracker.ietf.org, accessed 2026-08-06) — Internet-Drafts are explicitly NOT standards; full KYAPay v1.0 spec "aimed to be released soon" per docs (docs.skyfire.xyz via search, accessed 2026-08-06) | Skyfire operates issuance/wallets; token verification is decentralized (JWKS) but identity vetting is Skyfire-centralized | Per-transaction agent identity + payment-capability tokens (KYA — 'Know Your Agent'), linking agents to human principals; Spending limits, wallets funded by cards/ACH/wires/USDC | Economic HISTORY by design: tokens include "only the minimum necessary information for its intended use" — minimal disclosure, no accumulated portable track record; No receipts-as-credit-evidence, no reputation |
| Payman AI | Active but pivoted: original agent-wallet product (2024) now marketed as AI banking automation — site says "AI that executes real banking transactions - payments, transfers, account analysis"; Tracxn 2026 profile describes "Developer of an AI platform for banking transaction automation", $13.8M raised (search snippets, accessed 2026-08-06). Pivot inferred from positioning change — direct pivot announcement: UNVERIFIED | Fully centralized service | Agent-initiated payments with human approval (legacy); bank-facing AI assistants (current) | No portable signed economic history; no reputation; not a standard |

Open-source contracts described as "ERC-8004 compliant" (https://github.com/nevermined-io/contracts, accessed 2026-08-06) | Platform-operated; smart contracts open-source but orchestration centralized | Usage metering, plan management, settlement orchestration for agents across x402/MCP/A2A and card rails | No portable signed economic-history artifact surfaced in any source; metering records live in Nevermined's platform |
| Catena Labs — Agent Commerce Kit (ACK-ID / ACK-Pay) | Open spec + reference code, explicitly pre-production: ACK "proposes solutions built on open standard protocols and patterns" and "offers developers and financial institutions a blueprint"; MIT-licensed docs + source (https://www.agentcommercekit.com/overview/introduction, accessed 2026-08-06) | TBD; company simultaneously pursuing a regulated (centralized) trust-bank model | DIRECTLY RELEVANT DESIGN: payments "coupled with standardized, verifiable digital receipts as proof of payment" — a proposed verifiable-receipt primitive; Cryptographic linkage of agents to accountable human/org owners (identity pillar) | Production use — blueprint stage only; Receipt portability/aggregation into credit history not demonstrated; page did not name DIDs/VCs explicitly |
| x402 (HTTP 402 payment protocol, Coinbase-incubated, now x402 Foundation) | Live protocol in production on Base, Solana, 12+ chains; governed by x402 Foundation — "Forty companies now govern x402, the protocol Coinbase built and handed away" (coindesk.com 2026-07-15 via search snippet, accessed 2026-08-06) | No registry; permissionless on-chain settlement + facilitator services | Machine-native stablecoin micropayments (on-chain transfer = permanent public payment record); Payment-history raw material: x402 settlements are what the arXiv ERC-8004 study used to test economic grounding | No protocol-level signed receipt or work-verification — a payment proves transfer of funds, NOT work performed (epistemic rule); no semantics for invoice/repayment/credit events; High share of test/gamified volume makes raw counts unusable as adoption proof |
| Google Agent Payments Protocol (AP2) | Open specification, announced Sept 2025 (kiadev.net snippet dates 2025-09-17); built on A2A/MCP; production deployments: none found in sources reviewed (ap2-protocol.org, cloud.google.com blog, accessed 2026-08-06) | Spec-level none; practical deployments would run through payment networks/issuers | Cryptographically signed 'mandates' (Intent Mandate, Cart Mandate) as verifiable digital credentials — "tamper-evident, cryptographically signed digital objects" (ap2-protocol.org) creating an auditable trail of agent payment AUTHORIZATION | Economic history/reputation: mandates prove authorization for a purchase, not a track record; no receipt aggregation, no credit semantics |

Underwrites on "factors like your payment volume and processing history with Stripe" (stripe.com/capital, accessed 2026-08-06, German-locale page); no application — offers pushed to Dashboard | Fully closed platform data | Cash-flow underwriting at scale on platform-internal payment history; automatic repayment as % of daily sales | PORTABILITY: no mechanism found by which a merchant receives a signed/verifiable revenue-history artifact usable with third parties — history is locked in Stripe and consumed only by Stripe's own underwriting. No 'verified data export for lenders' product found (targeted search returned zero results). |

Wayflyer: "deployed $3B+ to 3,500+ customers" (aifimap snippet). Clearco: "invested $3.3B+ into 10,000+ businesses" (clear.co snippet). (all via search snippets accessed 2026-08-06) | Central intermediary (aggregator) is the trust root; Plaid Check additionally a regulated CRA | Consumer-permissioned, real-time bank/accounting data for lender underwriting — the incumbent answer to 'verify revenue'; Plaid Check is the closest fiat-world thing to a reusable verified cash-flow report: an FCRA-regulated CRA artifact a consumer can have furnished to multiple lenders | Reports are per-request lender deliverables, not consumer/merchant-held signed portable artifacts; verification trust root is Plaid/Tink as intermediary, not cryptographic; Business (vs consumer) verified cash-flow report as a portable primitive: not found; Nothing agent-native; no machine-verifiable signatures on the data |

| 3Jane (unsecured on-chain credit via zkTLS) | Live/early: docs describe "0%-collateral, open-term, variable-rate USDC credit facility" underwritten against Plaid bank data (real-time balances, "90-day cash-flow", account history), Credit Karma scores, and ETH address history, all attested via zkTLS (Reclaim/zkFetch) so "no raw PII on-chain" (docs.3jane.xyz via search snippets, accessed 2026-08-06); reported $10M senior warehouse facility with LendSwift (guavy snippet); sources conflict on Ethereum vs Base and on 2025 vs June-2026 launch date — exact launch date UNVERIFIED | Depends on Reclaim Protocol attestors + Plaid/Credit Karma as data sources | MOST DIRECT EXISTING IMPLEMENTATION of 'verified cash-flow → credit' using cryptographic attestation of Web2 financial data (bank cash flow + credit score) for underwriting; Defaults "legally binding" — hybrid legal recourse | Proofs are consumed internally by 3Jane's '3CA' underwriting algorithm — borrowers do not receive a portable, reusable verified cash-flow history; still a closed pipeline (risk analysis notes single-dependency on Reclaim attestation integrity, hindenrank snippet); Consumer/merchant bank accounts, not agent-native economic history |
| zkTLS / web-proof infrastructure (Reclaim Protocol, zkPass, Opacity, TLSNotary) | Production but early and contested. Reclaim: self-reported "4M+" verifications as of July 2026, $0.10–$1 pricing, "several Fortune 500" clients unnamed (reclaimprotocol.org, accessed 2026-08-06). zkPass: token listed on Binance 2026-01-07 with Seed Tag; ZachXBT fraud warning May 2026 left token near all-time lows (ainvest snippet; allegation details UNVERIFIED — source page fetch did not return specifics). Opacity: mainnet AVS on EigenLayer since July 2024; XION/EarnOS integration. TLSNotary: no version/production statement on homepage. | Attestor/notary sets (single attestor in Reclaim's Base verifier; EigenLayer operator set for Opacity; notary server for TLSNotary) | Cryptographic attestation that specific data appeared in a TLS session with a given server — the enabling primitive for 'prove your Stripe/bank dashboard' revenue proofs; Three trust modes with different bounds: proxy mode (trusts a centralized attestor/proxy — Reclaim's on-chain verifier on Base runs on a single attestor per blokz.dev analysis), MPC mode (strongest; TLSNotary MPC-TLS costs ~30MB garbled circuits at 5Mbps vs ~4s proxy mode), TEE mode (trusts hardware maker + operator; IP-bannable) (blokz.dev, tlsnotary.org/blog/2026/05/10, stengarl.eth.limo snippets, accessed 2026-08-06) | Fraud bounds are structural, not statistical: a zkTLS proof shows what the server SERVED, not that the underlying revenue is genuine (fake Stripe accounts, refund-recycling, self-dealing are all out of scope); single-attestor proxy deployments add a corruptible trust root; No standardized revenue-proof schema; each provider template is bespoke; no production Stripe-revenue provider found |
| EAS (Ethereum Attestation Service) + Coinbase Verifications | EAS: production infrastructure — "9.5M+" attestations, "450k+" attesters, deployed on Ethereum + 12 named L2s (https://attest.org/, accessed 2026-08-06). Coinbase Verifications: production on Base — apps can "verify 500K+ user's Coinbase account, country, and Coinbase One membership status" (coinbase.com/developer-platform snippet, accessed 2026-08-06) | Attestation contracts permissionless; meaning depends entirely on issuer trust (e.g., Coinbase as issuer) | General-purpose on/off-chain signed attestations with schemas; production identity/KYC-lite attestations (Coinbase account, country, Coinbase One) | Economic facts in production: no credit-score, revenue, receipt, or repayment attestations found live at scale — the schema layer exists, the economic content does not |
| UBL 2.1 / ISO/IEC 19845 + UBL Digital Signature Profiles + Peppol | UBL 2.1: OASIS Standard (2013), adopted as ISO/IEC 19845:2015 (iso.org/standard/66370.html, accessed 2026-08-06). UBL Digital Signature Profiles 1.0: fetched copy is "Committee Draft 11", dated "21 May 2011" (docs.oasis-open.org/ubl/prd2-UBL-2.1/doc/dsig/cd11-UBL-DSig-1.0.html, accessed 2026-08-06); whether it advanced beyond CD status: UNVERIFIED (later-stage URLs returned 404). Peppol: production network — OpenPeppol Q1 2026 report cites 450M documents exchanged in Q1 2026 (+34% YoY), 430 certified access points in 38 countries (eu-einvoicing.com snippet); participant counts range 1.4M–4.5M across sources | Peppol: centralized PKI (OpenPeppol CA), SML/SMP registries, certified access points | Standard XML semantics for invoice/credit-note/order documents at massive production scale; A defined (if low-maturity) enveloped XAdES signature extension for UBL documents | End-to-end document signatures are NOT the norm in Peppol (hop-by-hop AP signing only) — an invoice extracted from the network is not independently verifiable by a third party; No repayment/receipt event semantics; no agent-consumable verification API; XML/enterprise tooling, not crypto-native |
| Mexico CFDI 4.0 (SAT clearance e-invoicing) — production precedent for third-party-verified receipts | Mandatory production system: CFDI required since 2011, v4.0 mandatory for all taxpayers since 2023-04-01; every invoice is XML signed with the issuer's CSD certificate, then validated and stamped ("timbrado") by an authorized PAC adding UUID + digital seal + SAT stamp; QR verification code on each invoice (e-invoice.app, einvoicestudio.com, brinta.com snippets, accessed 2026-08-06) | Fully dependent on central authority (SAT) + licensed PAC intermediaries | The strongest existing production example of portable, cryptographically signed, third-party-verifiable economic documents: issuer-signed + government-agent-countersigned receipts verifiable by anyone against SAT; Loss/fraud handling via CSD revocation when SAT detects irregularities (Rule 2.7.1.5) | Jurisdiction-bound (Mexico only), tax-purpose semantics, human/business-oriented; no repayment/credit events; not accessible to autonomous agents as a neutral primitive |
| GOBL (Go Business Language) — crypto-adjacent signed invoice schema | Open-source SDK/schema, no formal standards-body status; JSON Schema documents wrapped in an "Envelope, with a head and signature" using JSON Web Signature (JWS); converters to UBL, CII, Factur-X, ZUGFeRD, XRechnung (docs.gobl.org, pkg.go.dev snippets, accessed 2026-08-06) | None — self-contained signed documents (key distribution unspecified/app-level) | The closest crypto-native-style primitive found for signed accounting events: JWS-signed JSON invoices with tax validation logic | Signatures are issuer-only self-claims (no counterparty/third-party countersignature); no receipt/repayment event types beyond invoicing; no agent identity binding; minimal adoption evidence |
| ISO 20022 (financial messaging semantics) | International Standard (ISO 20022 series; framework standard per iso.org/standard/20022-1); migrated onto SWIFT CBPR+, Target2, Fedwire, CHAPS during 2023–2025 (audit-security.com snippet, accessed 2026-08-06) | Closed network trust (SWIFT PKI, market-infrastructure membership) | Rich, standardized event semantics for payment initiation (pain), clearing/settlement (pacs), cash management (camt) — a mature vocabulary for 'repayment events' | Portable third-party verifiability: messages are only trustworthy inside closed network trust domains (SWIFT PKI); an extracted pacs.008 is not independently verifiable; not agent-accessible |

**Adoption evidence (with sources):**

- *ERC-8004 Trustless Agents (Identity/Reputation/Validation registries)*:
  - https://8004scan.io/networks (accessed 2026-08-06) — shared vanity addresses IdentityRegistry 0x8004A169FB4a3325136EB29fA0ceB6D2e539a432 and ReputationRegistry 0x8004BAa17C55a88189AE136b182e5fdA19dE9b63 deployed on 25+ chains; per-chain counts e.g. BNB 242,562 agents / Base 40,662 agents + 426,439 feedbacks / Ethereum 29,232 agents. No Validation Registry address published.

  - COUNTER-EVIDENCE on quality: arXiv empirical study https://arxiv.org/html/2606.26028v1 (accessed 2026-08-06) — 170k+ registrations across ETH/BSC/Base but only 3%/4%/15% of agents expose a valid registration file AND a declared service; Sybil-flagged reviewers are 73.6%/59.2%/90.6% of reviewers; feedback with no payment proof or task linkage is 95.4%/100%/98.7%; median cost to flip an agent's score past trust threshold τ=90 is $0.0027–$0.055 (one feedback); 93.8% of Base reviewers never made an x402/USDC payment yet submitted 94.9% of feedback; authors conclude reputation signal "measurements suggest NOT" reliable.

- *ISO 20022 (financial messaging semantics)*:
  - Universal adoption in interbank payments (SWIFT/Fedwire/Target2 migrations, multiple sources accessed 2026-08-06).
  - Signature semantics: the standard itself defines message schemas, NOT cryptographic signatures for business payloads — authenticity handled at network layer (SWIFT PKI) and locally via LAU trailers between back office and gateway (aquaglobal.co.uk Swift LAU factsheet snippet, accessed 2026-08-06). No snippet found showing payload-level signature semantics in pain/pacs.

**Coverage gaps found in this domain (conservative statements):**

- KEY QUESTION (a) ANSWER: No system found provides portable, signed, third-party-verifiable ECONOMIC HISTORY for agents or small digital businesses. The hypothesis 'this already exists' is FALSE on the evidence gathered. Nearest neighbors each fail on one axis: ERC-8004 is portable+on-chain but empirically Sybil-dominated and unbound to payments/work (arXiv 2606.26028: 95–100% of feedback carries no payment proof or task linkage); Virtuals ACP binds escrowed payment + evaluation per job but is platform-captive and incentive-inflated; x402 produces public payment records but payments are not proof of work performed and ~half of activity is test/gamified; Plaid Check produces a regulated verified cash-flow REPORT but it is aggregator-trust-rooted, per-request, consumer-only, and not a merchant/agent-held signed artifact; Mexico CFDI proves the concept works at national scale but is jurisdiction-bound and government-rooted; Catena ACK specifies 'verifiable digital receipts' but is an MIT-licensed blueprint with no found deployments.
- No production system was found that binds the triple {verified payment, verified counterparty identity, verified work/service delivered} into one reusable signed record. ERC-8004 has identity+feedback without payment binding; x402 has payment without identity/work binding; AP2 has signed authorization mandates without history aggregation; Skyfire KYAPay deliberately minimizes history.
- No zkTLS provider for Stripe (or any merchant-processor) revenue proofs was found in production — Reclaim's listed provider categories are payroll/education/loyalty/gig; the only revenue-adjacent production consumer found is 3Jane (consumer bank cash flow via Plaid-over-Reclaim), which keeps proofs inside a closed underwriting pipeline. 'Prove your Stripe dashboard' remains unbuilt as a product despite the primitive existing.
- No EAS-based credit, revenue, or repayment attestations exist in production — EAS lending use-cases are documentation hypotheticals only, despite 9.5M+ attestations for identity/other purposes. The attestation rails exist; the economic attestation content does not.
- No repayment/loss-history registry exists for agents or on-chain SMB credit: Goldfinch (shut down, ~$56M frozen), Maple (post-2022 pivoted AWAY from unsecured cash-flow credit to overcollateralized), and Huma (off-chain human Evaluation Agent) all keep loss/underwriting history closed; sector default data surfaces only through news and dashboards, not a verifiable primitive.
- Accounting-event semantics gap: standards define invoice semantics (UBL/ISO 20022, massive adoption) and even signatures (UBL DSig at only Committee Draft maturity as verified; GOBL JWS with negligible adoption), but no standard defines counterparty-countersigned invoice→payment→repayment event chains verifiable outside a closed network. Peppol signs transport hops (access-point AS4 signatures), not payloads, so exchanged invoices are not independently verifiable artifacts.
- COMPETITIVE vs PUBLIC-GOOD split observed: (proprietary, well-served) platform cash-flow underwriting on captive data — Stripe Capital $12B+, Shopify Capital $5.1B+, Wayflyer $3B+, Clearco $3.3B+, Pipe/Parafin embedded capital; and aggregator-mediated verification (Plaid/Tink/Codat). (missing public-good primitive) a neutral, issuer-agnostic, signed, merchant/agent-held economic-history artifact that any third party can verify without re-granting raw data access to each new underwriter — every incumbent's history is locked in-platform, and every crypto attempt found either lacks economic grounding (ERC-8004) or portability (ACP, 3Jane).
- Fraud-bound gap: no found system states quantified error/fraud bounds for revenue proofs. zkTLS bounds are structural (attestor honesty, single-attestor proxy roots, TEE hardware trust) and say nothing about upstream data authenticity (fake accounts, refund recycling); ERC-8004 manipulation cost measured at $0.0027–$0.055 per feedback (259x below value-at-stake on Base). Any receipts-as-credit-evidence design must supply its own economic-authenticity layer; none found to copy.

---

## 4. Hypothesis tests A–G, with adversarial verification

Each hypothesis was tested by an independent advocate agent instructed to argue its strongest version from the shared evidence base, then attacked by a separate adversarial agent instructed to refute the verdict and re-verify load-bearing citations itself. The table summarizes; full arguments follow.

| H | Statement (short) | Advocate verdict | Attack outcome | Post-attack standing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Existing standards are sufficient | partially_supported (high) | **REFUTED** | False as stated (universal claim with confirmed counterexamples). Standards fully cover only commodity plumbing (SIWE/712/1271 auth, JCS/JWS envelopes) — which the docs adopt rather than claim as novel. At least three functions uncovered: composite receipts, portable economic history, legal-entity↔controller binding for agents. |
| B | Adapter SDK + conformance suite sufficient | partially_supported (high) | **REFUTED (upward)** | Revised to *supported at v0 scope (N=1), medium confidence*: vocabulary authoring on existing rails is glue-priced work of exactly the invoked in-house kind (hounfour's own practice IS authoring net-new payload vocabularies); the "4-schema semantic gap" belongs on D's ledger, not as B's refutation. Limits: vectors pin bytes/determinism, never economic meaning; with the Operator as sole issuer AND verifier, sufficiency proves nothing third-party until N≥2. |
| C | Registry-independent relationship model missing | partially_supported (medium) | **REFUTED** | Rejected as posed: C is a conjunction and its fill-claim leg is decisively false — D-4 is an operator DB schema (obligor_id = Operator-assigned UUIDv4; `arcturus_credit.obligors` single-writer; QRA carries `application_id` with **no obligor linkage**, so rotation-survival is a Postgres join, not a verifiable property). The real landscape gap (entity↔controller binding for agents, rotation continuity, registry-free fresh revocation) is recorded once, under D/world-gap, as a never-attempted-profile fact. |
| D | Signed economic-event semantics missing | supported (medium) | **REFUTED to partial** | Only the factual-absence clause survives (low-medium confidence): no adopted portable credit-event schemas with conservation/replay. "Counterparty-signed vocabulary" clause failed (payment countersignature already on the rail; default countersignature incentive-infeasible); "this is the real gap" failed against the no-demand null (five natural experiments). Residue: ~4 schemas of vocabulary on existing rails. |
| E | Return Ledger projection missing | partially_supported (high) | **REFUTED** | Not a distinct public-good primitive. Ledger = deterministic fold over D's events (needs a new fold engine — hounfour's DSL cannot express L-1/L-7/L-8: no filter/group-by/predicate aggregation); public seal = operator self-reporting without inclusion proofs, instantiating the existing authority-signed-aggregate pattern (CFDI, Provisions). The confirmed world-gap (no verifiable loss-history registry anywhere) is an adoption gap, filed under D. |
| F | Broader protocol necessary | rejected (high) | **NOT refuted — sharpened** | Rejection stands as a present-tense necessity claim: schema interlock is discharged by content-addressed references (policy_version_id, evidence_commitment), not joint standardization; most of the bundle is commodity or private; zero external implementers. Time-indexed residual: at N≥2 operators exchanging comparable histories, a strictly narrower joint-semantics requirement (qualification methodology + default definitions only) becomes live — never identity, envelopes, or private agreement bodies. |
| G | No distinct system justified (null) | partially_supported (high) | **NOT refuted — strengthened** | The attack agent re-verified all six factual predicates and concluded the "partially" qualifier was a category error: under G's own definition the verdict is **fully supported**. No distinct Loa-Arcturus system is justified on today's evidence; the surviving gap (§5) is filed as a contribution target, not a system justification. |

**Independence note:** the advocates for A and G reached opposite-signed verdicts from the same evidence, and the attacks moved them toward each other (A down, G up) — the intended behavior of adversarial verification, and grounds for confidence that the converged result (§0) is evidence-driven rather than framing-driven.

### 4.A Hypothesis A

**Stated:** HYPOTHESIS A: Existing standards are sufficient — every function docs 09–11 assign to the Loa-Arcturus "Protocol" (controller identity, delegation, revocation, signed economic events, receipts, portable history) is already covered by compositions of existing standards with production adoption.

**Advocate verdict:** partially_supported (high confidence)

**Advocate argument:** The strongest version of A wins on four of six functions — and it wins partly because v0's Protocol, as actually specified, is operator-rooted, so operator/issuer-rooted standards match its trust shape exactly.  (1) Controller identity: SIWE (ERC-4361 Final, 251k weekly npm) + EIP-712/EIP-1271 (Final; Safe: 57M+ wallets, $1T+ processed, validating signatures via 1271 with zero registry) cover "controller signs, key validity decided by controller's own contract, revocable instantly." The docs use exactly this: identity-api already runs SIWE in production, and legal-entity binding is a manual 2-of-3 allowlist plus a Postgres row (11 D-4, ladder step 1: "Obligor admitted — Operator, 1 sig + svc-JWT") — no standard is displaced because no cryptographic standard is even attempted.  (2) Delegation: 11 D-4's delegation is SIWE-proved DB rows (application_wallets) — weaker than what ships today: MetaMask Delegation Framework v1.3.0 deployed at identical addresses on 24 mainnets including Berachain, with ~40 caveat enforcers (spend/time/method limits), auto-enabled in production MetaMask (ERC-7710/7715 still Draft — noted).  (3) Revocation-without-history-reset: history attached to a stable subject id surviving key rotation is standard practice — VC 2.0 subjects + Bitstring Status List (both W3C Rec 15 May 2025), EAS on-chain revocation. v0's revocation is a row update in the Operator's database — the same issuer-rooted shape.  (4) Signed-event MACHINERY: JCS (RFC 8785) + Ed25519 + JWS + hash-chains are commodity; hounfour itself already builds on RFC-8785 + EdDSA-only JWS. Nothing envelope-level is net-new.  A fails on the remaining two, and the failures are load-bearing: (5) receipts binding payment↔work↔outcome — the x402 signed payload contains only {from,to,value,validAfter,validBefore,nonce}; the resource sits outside the signature; the offer/receipt extension is v0.6, no amount field, "wire shape... not considered stable"; the only production outcome-conditioned settlement (Virtuals ACP) is platform-locked. (6) Portable third-party-verifiable economic history — the dedicated landscape scan returned an explicit FALSE: Stripe/Shopify/Pipe history is platform-locked, Plaid Check is per-request and aggregator-rooted, EAS has 9.5M+ attestations but zero economic content at scale, ISO 20022/UBL have semantics without portable verifiability, CFDI 4.0 is jurisdiction-bound. No adopted composition carries counterparty-bound invoice→payment→repayment→default semantics, and "incur obligations" scope vocabulary is uncovered in every capability standard. The 10 credit schemas are net-new because that layer genuinely does not exist.

**Key evidence (advocate):**
- SIWE/712/1271 are Final with mass production use: ERC-4361 'status: Final'; siwe npm 251,068 weekly downloads; Safe '57M+ Wallets deployed, $1T+ Total value processed' exposing EIP-1271 isValidSignature — result-std-identity-accounts.json (eips.ethereum.org, api.npmjs.org, safe.global, all accessed 2026-08-06)
- The docs' own identity layer is these standards plus a DB row: identity-api@bca4412c runs 'SIWE + ES256 svc-JWT/JWKS; operator_grants is 2-of-3 manual allowlist; no agent entity' (09-decision-architecture.md:29 @1920bd6); obligor admission = 'Operator (1 sig + svc-JWT), legal_name + jurisdiction' and delegation = 'Obligor controller SIWE + Operator co-sign' into application_wallets (11-v0-reconciliation.md:346-347 @1920bd6)
- Scoped revocable delegation is deployed infrastructure: MetaMask Delegation Framework v1.3.0 at identical addresses on 24 mainnets including Berachain, ~40 caveat enforcers (ValueLteEnforcer, TimestampEnforcer), 'Smart accounts are now auto-enabled for new MetaMask users' — result-std-credentials-delegation.json (github.com/MetaMask/delegation-framework/documents/Deployments.md; support.metamask.io, accessed 2026-08-06); caveat: ERC-7710/7715 status Draft
- Envelope machinery is commodity — the Protocol already composes it from standards: hounfour@e24752e2 uses 'RFC-8785 JCS + ES256/EdDSA-only JWS (src/utilities/signature.ts:23)' (10-v0-build-pack.md:67 @1920bd6); EAS off-chain attestations are EIP-712-signed, verified 'rather than querying chain state', 9.5M+ attestations, OP-Stack predeploy 0x4200...0021 (result-std-credentials-delegation.json; attest.org, specs.optimism.io, accessed 2026-08-06)
- Raw payment evidence needs no new protocol: finn receipt-verifier parseTransferLogs — strict emitter==USDC, exactly-one-match fail-closed, payer from log.from not tx.from (loa-finn@69f3f8a7 src/x402/receipt-verifier.ts:317-373, re-verified by orchestrator 2026-08-06); HONEY EIP-3009 entry points 0xe3ee160e/0xef55bec6 live on 80094 (rpc.berachain.com eth_call, orchestrator-scope.md, 2026-08-06)
- COUNTER — receipt binding is uncovered: x402 exact-EVM signed authorization contains EXACTLY {from,to,value,validAfter,validBefore,nonce}; 'Nothing binds the signature to a specific resource URL or task'; receipt extension v0.6 has NO amount field and 'Wire shape and field placement are not considered stable' (result-std-comms-payments.json; x402-foundation/x402 specs/schemes/exact/scheme_exact_evm.md + extension-offer-and-receipt.md, accessed 2026-08-06)
- COUNTER — portable economic history does not exist: 'No system found provides portable, signed, third-party-verifiable ECONOMIC HISTORY for agents or small digital businesses. The hypothesis "this already exists" is FALSE on the evidence gathered' — Stripe Capital ($12B+ originations) and Shopify Capital ($5.1B+) keep history platform-locked; Plaid Check is a per-request CRA deliverable; EAS shows 'no credit-score, revenue, receipt, or repayment attestations found live at scale'; ISO 20022 'an extracted pacs.008 is not independently verifiable'; CFDI 4.0 jurisdiction-bound (result-std-history-underwriting.json coverage_gaps, sources accessed 2026-08-06)
- COUNTER — no economic-scope vocabulary anywhere: 'No adopted standard expresses ECONOMIC scope (spend caps, earn caps, obligation ceilings...) as an interoperable vocabulary'; ''Incur obligations' is uncovered everywhere' (result-std-credentials-delegation.json coverage_gaps); orchestrator grep confirms none of the 10 credit schemas exist in hounfour@e24752e2 — they are net-new (orchestrator-scope.md, 2026-08-06)
- The only production outcome-conditioned settlement is platform-locked: Virtuals ACP v2.0, '18 months in production and over 2,000 agents onboarded' on Base, escrow released on evaluator verdict — not a neutral standard, incentive-inflated volume (result-std-comms-payments.json + result-std-history-underwriting.json, whitepaper.virtuals.io, accessed 2026-08-06)

**Advocate's self-declared weakest point:** The gap half of the verdict rests on negative evidence ('none found' searches on 2026-08-06) against moving targets: the x402 offer/receipt extension already binds payee+amount+resource (offer) and payer+resource+outcome (receipt) across two seller-signed artifacts — one wire-format revision adding an amount field and stabilizing the extension would substantially close the receipt gap, flipping function (5) to covered; and my strongest-A argument for functions 1–3 leans on v0's operator-gated implementation rather than the docs' protocol ambition (permissionless, presentable-elsewhere history), so a critic can fairly object that I judged sufficiency against the weakest reading of what the Protocol claims to be.

**Adversarial attack — refuted: True**

Citations check out (09:29, 11:346-347, 10:67 verified verbatim; result-std files internally consistent) — the failure is logic and framing. (1) Quantifier mismatch: A claims standards cover EVERY function; the analyst's own COUNTER evidence proves two functions uncovered. A universally quantified hypothesis with confirmed counterexamples is refuted, not 'partially supported.' (2) Strawman wins: two of the four 'wins' are against claims docs 09-11 never make — doc 10:67 shows hounfour already composes RFC-8785 JCS + EdDSA JWS from standards, and doc 09 states identity-api already runs SIWE; the Protocol's claimed novelty is the schema layer on top, exactly where A fails. (3) Dropped caveat from the analyst's own file: result-std-identity-accounts coverage_gaps says 'No standard binds CONTROLLER-as-LEGAL-ENTITY to the on-chain controller address... no standards-based replacement to adopt today' — yet function (1) is scored covered by silently narrowing it to signature auth, though the legal-entity obligor model (11 D-4, 'identity-graph records', 'obligor model is net-new schema' per 09:531) IS assigned to the Protocol. That is a third uncovered function. (4) Charter violations: delegation 'covered' rests on Draft ERC-7710/7715 plus a vendor framework whose volume is 'none found (UNVERIFIED)' — draft standards ≠ adopted infrastructure; VC 2.0 anchors function (3) though the analyst's own file says 'B2B/economic-claims production adoption: none found' beyond gov-ID/age-verification. (5) The analyst's weakest_point concedes functions 1-3 were judged against v0's operator-gated reading, not the docs' 'present elsewhere' portable ambition (09:433-434), under which delegation/revocation records must themselves be third-party verifiable — their credentials file says no adopted composition provides that. confidence:high is indefensible.

**Surviving core:** Existing adopted standards fully cover only the commodity plumbing: controller authentication (SIWE/ERC-4361 Final + EIP-712/EIP-1271) and signature-envelope machinery (RFC-8785 JCS + Ed25519 JWS) — both of which docs 09-11 already adopt by name rather than claim as novel. Delegation and revocation-without-history-reset are covered only under v0's operator-rooted weak reading (DB rows behind SIWE), not the docs' portable third-party-verifiable ambition. The gap findings stand and are multi-scan corroborated: no adopted standard or production composition covers legal-entity-to-controller binding for agents, economic-scope vocabulary ('incur obligations'), receipts binding payer/payee/task/amount/outcome, or portable third-party-verifiable economic history. The design implication survives: express the genuinely novel semantic layer (credit schemas, qualified-revenue attestation, epoch-sealed replayable ledger) inside existing envelopes (VC 2.0/EAS off-chain/x402 extension) rather than specifying a parallel identity-and-signature stack.

**Post-attack verdict:** refuted — hypothesis A is false as stated: existing standards are insufficient for at least three of six functions (receipts, portable economic history, legal-entity obligor binding), and these are precisely the functions where docs 09-11 locate the Protocol's novelty; sufficiency holds only for plumbing the docs never claimed to invent. The prior 'partially_supported / high' verdict overstates coverage by counting strawman wins and understates the surviving Protocol core.

### 4.B Hypothesis B

**Stated:** B: An adapter SDK + conformance suite is sufficient — no new semantics needed; the missing work is glue (adapters to x402/EAS/VC/identity stacks) plus test vectors/conformance discipline of the kind loa-hounfour already practices in-house.

**Advocate verdict:** partially_supported (high confidence)

**Advocate argument:** Strongest B case: Arcturus needs no new cryptography, registry, or wire protocol — every MECHANICAL layer of the 10 schemas (doc 10 §4.2, grimoires/loa/next/10-v0-build-pack.md:424-435) rides existing rails via adapters. Mapping: (1) Obligor = Operator-issued VC 2.0 credential (W3C Rec 2025-05-15) carrying KYB claims; SD-JWT (RFC 9901) selective disclosure hides legal_name/jurisdiction, publishing only obligor_id+bands; status lifecycle maps to Bitstring Status List (suspension=reversible, revocation=irreversible — exact match).
