(2) DelegatedApplication = UCAN v1.0 delegation controller→app DID, SIWE (ERC-4361 Final) controller auth, EIP-712 wallet proofs; UCAN's weak revocation consistency is harmless because the Operator is sole verifier in v0. (3) EvidenceBundle = JCS(RFC 8785)+sha256 content addressing over EVM log refs — hounfour's existing commitment-root pattern. (4)/(5) QualifiedRevenueAttestation and CreditPolicyDecision envelopes = EAS off-chain attestations (production rails, OP-Stack predeploy, EIP-712-verifiable without a registry read) or VC envelopes; deterministic IDs are plain content addressing. (7) FacilityDraw = tx refs + hounfour saga-context states. (8) FacilityRepayment consent = EIP-3009 (Final) with chain-enforced nonce idempotency — pure glue. (10) EpochSeal mechanics = hash-chained signed checkpoints already implemented in-house (phase-completion-envelope prev_envelope_hash; freeside acvp-l1-v2). A conformance suite pins: JCS byte-identity; derivation vectors for the four content-addressed IDs; determinism (same evidence+policy ⇒ byte-identical attestation); bigint conservation I-3/I-13; reorg-invalidation vectors; fail-closed signature semantics — exactly hounfour's proven method (1,241 vectors; Python cross-runner validated 440 fixtures offline, no TS toolchain). Where B breaks — semantics, not glue: (a) #4's qualification vocabulary (qualified/flagged/dropped atoms, gate_breakdown): no standard defines what 'qualified revenue' means; (b) #5's binding_constraint decision vocabulary; (c) the obligation lifecycle in #6/#8/#9 — binding a controller to FUTURE liabilities is 'uncovered everywhere', no counterparty-countersigned invoice→payment→repayment chain standard exists, and no default/loss vocabulary exists anywhere; (d) #10's lane-partitioned double-entry projection semantics. Four independent standards scans converge on these gaps. B's mechanism claim (adapters + vectors, no new protocol) is right; its 'no new semantics' claim is false for the 4-schema credit-liability core — the exact part the thesis is about.

**Key evidence (advocate):**
- All 10 schemas are net-new — no adapter target in-house: 'hounfour schemas grep (e24752e2): NO facility/obligor/qualified-revenue/return-ledger/evidence-bundle schemas exist' (/tmp/arcturus-audit/notes/orchestrator-scope.md:86)
- Envelope substrate is commodity: Ed25519 over sha256 of RFC-8785 JCS with fail-closed CRYPTO_DEFERRED (schemas/signature-envelope.schema.json:1-77, src/validators/index.ts:1744 @ loa-hounfour e24752e2, result-repo-arcturus-hounfour.json)
- Conformance discipline proven in-house: 262 schemas/1,241 vectors (RELEASE-INTEGRITY.json @e24752e2); 'python3 vectors/runners/python/cross_runner.py → OK: 440 fixtures validated' offline, run 2026-08-06 (result-repo-arcturus-hounfour.json)
- Doc 10 already scopes the work as schema+constraints+vectors: build step 6 'The 10 credit schemas + constraints + vectors' (grimoires/loa/next/10-v0-build-pack.md:1056 @1920bd6)
- Repayment consent = existing standard: x402 exact scheme signs EIP-3009 transferWithAuthorization {from,to,value,validAfter,validBefore,nonce} (scheme_exact_evm.md, x402-foundation, accessed 2026-08-06; result-std-comms-payments.json); doc 10 #8 declares 'the EIP-3009 authorization is the obligor's consent' (10-v0-build-pack.md:433)
- Identity/delegation adapters exist: VC 2.0 'W3C Recommendation 15 May 2025'; SD-JWT RFC 9901; UCAN v1.0 registry-free attenuated delegation; SIWE ERC-4361 Final, 251k weekly npm (result-std-credentials-delegation.json; result-std-identity-accounts.json, all accessed 2026-08-06)
- Attestation rails exist but carry no economic semantics: EAS 9.5M+ attestations, OP-Stack predeploy 0x4200...0021, yet 'NO production credit or economic-history attestations found — all lending references are docs idea-zone hypotheticals' (attest.org + docs.attest.org, accessed 2026-08-06; result-std-history-underwriting.json)
- BREAK — obligations: "'Incur obligations' is uncovered everywhere: ... No found standard lets an agent verifiably bind its controller to FUTURE liabilities (credit, debt, contractual commitments)" (result-std-credentials-delegation.json coverage_gaps)
- BREAK — event chain: 'no counterparty-countersigned invoice→payment→repayment event chain standard anywhere' (evidence-digest.md:66); Peppol signs transport hops, not payloads (result-std-history-underwriting.json, peppol.helger.com accessed 2026-08-06)
- BREAK — default/loss vocabulary: 'No repayment/loss-history registry exists for agents or on-chain SMB credit' — Goldfinch shut down, Maple pivoted away from unsecured, Huma = off-chain human Evaluation Agent (result-std-history-underwriting.json, all accessed 2026-08-06)
- BREAK — revenue qualification: 'No standardized revenue-proof schema; each provider template is bespoke; no production Stripe-revenue provider found' (result-std-history-underwriting.json zkTLS entry); x402 receipt has no amount and binds no task (result-std-comms-payments.json)
- Seal mechanics are glue: prev_envelope_hash chain + sha256 genesis anchor on PhaseCompletionEnvelope (schemas/phase-completion-envelope.schema.json:119-123 @e24752e2) and freeside acvp-l1-v2 JCS+sha256+Ed25519+hash-chain envelope (evidence-digest.md:22) — same shape as #10's prev_seal_hash/seal_hash (10-v0-build-pack.md:435)

**Advocate's self-declared weakest point:** The glue/semantics boundary is definitional and my verdict leans on it. A B-proponent can reply that everything I call 'new semantics' is just payload vocabulary riding existing envelopes — EAS schema registration is permissionless, UCAN policies are arbitrary predicates, so authoring a 'qualified revenue' or 'facility default' schema IS adapter-level work by construction, collapsing partially_supported into supported. The counter-collapse also threatens: since the Operator is today the sole issuer AND sole verifier (zero external consumers; EAS/VC rails carry zero economic content at scale; arcturus evidence is 100% client-asserted per F-4), mapping onto those rails adds no third-party verifiability now — making B practically indistinguishable from 'operator bookkeeping with nicer envelopes' (hypothesis G) until a second independent verifier exists.

**Adversarial attack — refuted: True**

Citations verify (hounfour@e24752e2 zero credit schemas re-grepped; doc 10 §4.2 rows 8/10; build step 6; minor nit: 241 not 262 schema files in schemas/ — immaterial). But the verdict's load-bearing glue/semantics boundary collapses, as the analyst's own weakest point warned. The hypothesis invokes hounfour's in-house practice as the model for the missing work, and that practice IS authoring net-new payload vocabularies (all ~241 schemas, none adapters); doc 10 build step 6 classifies all 10 credit schemas — semantic core included — as one work item of 'schemas + constraints + vectors', the same class as the glue. Under the audit's converged position (hypo-attack-D/E: residue = ~4 schemas of glue-priced authoring on existing rails; adoption gap, not infrastructure gap), the analyst's 4-schema 'new semantics' carve-out is D's gap double-counted, not a counterexample to B's sufficiency. What genuinely resists collapse is narrower: (i) conformance vectors pin bytes and determinism, not meaning — no vector certifies that 'qualified' measures real revenue; (ii) with the Operator as sole issuer AND sole verifier, sufficiency for the protocol ambition is undemonstrable until N≥2 (the G-collapse); (iii) the EAS-specific collapse route is registry-tinged (schema semantics + revocation are chain-resident; EAS not deployed on Berachain) — but the hounfour/VC route is registry-free, so the collapse still goes through. Confidence 'high' is unjustified for a verdict resting on an admittedly unstable definition.

**Surviving core:** Verified facts stand: the four credit vocabularies (qualification, decision, obligation lifecycle, default/loss) have no external source to adapt — four independent scans converge — so someone must author them; and vectors cannot pin economic meaning, only byte-identity/determinism. The envelope/identity/consent layers are commodity (JCS/Ed25519, VC 2.0, SD-JWT, UCAN/SIWE, EIP-3009), and hounfour's 1,241-vector method is proven in-house.

**Post-attack verdict:** supported at v0 scope (N=1), confidence medium — an adapter SDK + conformance suite covers the entire v0 build because vocabulary authorship on existing rails is glue-priced work of exactly the invoked in-house kind; the residue ('vectors cannot certify meaning'; 'nothing is third-party-verified while the Operator is sole verifier') limits what B's sufficiency proves, not whether the work class suffices. The 4-schema semantic gap belongs on D's ledger, not as B's refutation.

### 4.C Hypothesis C

**Stated:** C: A registry-independent relationship model (obligor = legal entity; controller proves control; applications/wallets are revocable delegates; economic history attaches to the obligor and survives rotation, third-party-verifiable without a shared registry) is missing from existing standards, and the D-4 model fills it

**Advocate verdict:** partially_supported (medium confidence)

**Advocate argument:** The standards gap is real, but D-4 is not the thing that fills it. (1) Gap: no single standard expresses the full model, and adversarial composition fails on three verified points: no standard binds a legal entity to a controller address for agent principals (vLEI OOR/ECR credentials cover human representatives only, no monetary scope); no mechanism achieves fresh revocation + offline third-party verification + no shared registry simultaneously (UCAN/Biscuit are eventually consistent; status lists are issuer-hosted; 7710/session-keys/EAS-revocation are chain-resident); 'incur obligations' has no vocabulary anywhere. Rotation-surviving verifiable continuity ('same agent, new key, no shared registry') is explicitly uncovered. (2) Counter-evidence, stated honestly: the model IS expressible today by composition — DID Core's controller/capabilityDelegation data model, VC 2.0 claims, UCAN attenuated delegation with revocation-by-reference, SIWE control proof, did:web rotation under DNS-only dependency. The orchestrator's own spot-check concluded 'controller→agent revocable scoped authority without a registry is expressible via VC 2.0 + UCAN chains.' What is missing is an interoperability profile, agent-shaped entity binding, and revocation freshness — a narrower gap than 'no standard expresses this.' (3) Decisive weighing: D-4 as specified is an operator database schema, not a registry-independent protocol. Admission is Operator-gated ('Enrollment is not permissionless in v0'); obligor_id is a UUIDv4 'Operator-assigned'; legal_name/jurisdiction are private; the single-writer table declares 'arcturus_credit.obligors owns obligor legal identity + delegation.' The continuity mechanism — 'history attaches to obligor_id' — is a foreign key in the Operator's Postgres. Worse for the portability claim: the portable artifact (QualifiedRevenueAttestation) carries application_id, not obligor_id; the obligor→application mapping exists only in the Operator DB, so rotation-survival is a DB join, not a cryptographic property of anything a third party can verify. Third-party verification reduces to 'trust the Operator's Ed25519 key' (and hounfour is CRYPTO_DEFERRED — it never verifies). So: the landscape gap exists; D-4 neither requires registry-independence (single operator) nor delivers it (operator-as-registry). The relationship model is a sound underwriting schema whose protocol-ness is aspirational.

**Key evidence (advocate):**
- D-4 is Operator-gated, not a protocol: 'Admission is Operator-gated... Enrollment is not permissionless in v0: a pseudonymous wallet cannot be sued' — /home/eileenspectremoon/loa-dev/loa-arcturus/grimoires/loa/next/11-v0-reconciliation.md D-4 (lines 118-121); single-writer: 'arcturus_credit.obligors owns obligor legal identity + delegation' (§5, line ~422)
- Obligor identity is an operator DB key: Obligor 'ID UUIDv4, Operator-assigned. S Operator... legal_name/jurisdiction are private; only obligor_id + bands are public' — 10-v0-build-pack.md §6.2 entity #1 (line 426); C-9 'identity-api has no agent/obligor entity; obligor model is net-new' (line 177)
- Portable history artifact omits the obligor: QualifiedRevenueAttestation fields carry application_id and no obligor_id — 10-v0-build-pack.md §6.2 entity #4 (line 429); so 'history attaches to obligor_id' (11 D-4, line 113) holds only inside the Operator schema, contradicting registry-independent third-party verifiability of rotation-survival
- Entity→address binding uncovered: 'No standard binds CONTROLLER-as-LEGAL-ENTITY to the on-chain controller address... vLEI, KYB attestations, EAS schemas exist as ingredients but no found standard covers it for agents' — result-std-identity-accounts.json coverage_gaps[2]; rotation continuity: ''Same agent, new key, no shared registry, verifiable continuity' is uncovered' (coverage_gaps[3])
- Revocation gap verified: 'No found standard achieves fresh revocation + offline third-party verification + no shared registry simultaneously' — result-std-credentials-delegation.json coverage_gaps[1]; 'incur obligations is uncovered everywhere' (coverage_gaps[2]); vLEI roles 'are for human representatives', no economic scoping (vLEI entry does_not_cover)
- Counter-evidence (composition expressibility): DID Core controller + capabilityDelegation 'the DATA MODEL for controller X controls key Y for purpose Z exists' (result-std-identity-accounts.json, DID entry); UCAN 'chained, attenuated delegation between DIDs with NO registry' + revocation-by-any-chain-issuer (result-std-credentials-delegation.json, UCAN covers); orchestrator: 'controller→agent revocable scoped authority without a registry is expressible via VC 2.0 + UCAN chains' — orchestrator-scope.md (Standards spot checks)
- Portability claim is issuer-dependent: 'signed QualifiedRevenueAttestations... the obligor can present elsewhere' (09-decision-architecture.md §6, line ~434) but hounfour 'never verifies crypto — CRYPTO_DEFERRED... consumers must' (evidence-digest.md, in-repo reality), i.e., verification = trusting the Operator key, the VC issuer model, not a registry-free relationship protocol

**Advocate's self-declared weakest point:** The verdict hinges on treating 'expressible by composition but with no interop profile, no agent-shaped entity binding, and only eventually-consistent revocation' as a partial rather than full gap. A defender could argue the composition failures (vLEI human-only, revocation freshness, missing profile) make the gap fully real; a skeptic could argue VC 2.0 + UCAN + did:web + SIWE covers every v0-relevant behavior since v0's single-operator setting never exercises registry-independence anyway. Both readings are compatible with the evidence; I split the difference.

**Adversarial attack — refuted: True**

C is a conjunction — (i) the relationship model is missing from standards AND (ii) D-4 fills it — and the analyst proved conjunct (ii) decisively false, then averaged to 'partially_supported'. I verified the kill evidence directly: doc 11:418-421 single-writer table — 'arcturus_credit.obligors owns obligor legal identity + delegation'; doc 10 §4.2 #1 — Obligor ID 'UUIDv4, Operator-assigned', legal_name/jurisdiction private; §4.2 #4 — QualifiedRevenueAttestation fields carry application_id and no obligor_id, so 'history survives rotation' is a Postgres foreign-key join and the portable artifact contradicts the portability claim; doc 11 D-4 — 'Enrollment is not permissionless in v0'. A failed conjunction is a rejected hypothesis, not a half-supported one. Framing attack: the model was never ATTEMPTED as a protocol (vLEI is the closest analog and covers human representatives only) — 'missing' misdescribes 'never posed'; and v0's single-operator, sole-verifier setting never exercises registry-independence, so C's claim has no observable consequence in the system under audit. The retained landscape gap (entity↔controller-address binding for agents, rotation-surviving continuity, fresh revocation without a registry — verified in result-std-identity-accounts/credentials coverage_gaps) is real but is already recorded on A's and D's gap ledgers and, per hypo-attack-D/E convergence, is an adoption/demand gap; keeping it under C double-counts one gap across three hypotheses.

**Surviving core:** Three verified landscape facts: no adopted standard binds a legal entity to a controller address for agent principals with monetary scope; no mechanism achieves fresh revocation + offline third-party verification + no shared registry simultaneously; rotation-surviving verifiable continuity is uncovered. D-4 survives as a sound operator underwriting schema, and the analyst's cryptographic upgrade path (obligor commitment inside portable artifacts; obligor-signed delegation/revocation credentials) is the actionable remainder if protocol-ness is ever wanted.

**Post-attack verdict:** rejected as posed (confidence high on the fill-claim leg): D-4 neither requires registry-independence (single operator) nor delivers it (operator-as-registry; third-party verification reduces to trusting the Operator's Ed25519 key under CRYPTO_DEFERRED). The genuine standards gap should be recorded once, under the D/world-gap ledger, as a never-attempted-profile fact, not credited to C.

### 4.D Hypothesis D

**Stated:** HYPOTHESIS D: Signed economic-event semantics are missing — no adopted standard defines a counterparty-signed, replayable, conservation-checkable event vocabulary for invoice→payment→repayment→default→writeoff→recovery chains usable by autonomous agents; and this is the real registry-independent gap, with a small minimal vocabulary.

**Advocate verdict:** supported (medium confidence)

**Advocate argument:** The factual core is verified across four independent standards audits: no adopted standard welds (a) economic-event semantics, (b) counterparty signatures, (c) portable verification, and (d) conservation/replay into one artifact. Each incumbent fails on exactly one axis. ISO 20022 has mature repayment vocabulary but an extracted pacs.008 is only trustworthy inside SWIFT's closed PKI; Peppol signs transport hops, not payloads. EAS has adoption (9.5M+ attestations, OP-Stack predeploy) but zero production economic content — lending is a docs hypothetical. AP2 mandates are authorization-only VDCs with no settlement-conditioning and no deployments. CFDI 4.0 proves counterparty-countersigned portable receipts work at national scale but is Mexico-bound with no credit events. GOBL is issuer-only JWS with negligible adoption. One correction to the hypothesis as worded: x402 now HAS a receipt format (offer-and-receipt extension v0.6) — but it is optional, seller-signed only, has no amount field, and is declared wire-unstable, so the substantive gap stands: nothing binds (payer, payee, task, amount, outcome) in one portable signed record except platform-locked Virtuals ACP.  No adversarial composition of existing standards closes it: EAS/VC give single-issuer statements without per-event countersignature roles; nothing external does cross-document conservation arithmetic or byte-identical replay — those dimensions have zero precedent anywhere, and in-house prior art exists (hounfour bigint_sum conservation factories, 9,568 tests green; freeside JCS+Ed25519+hash-chain envelope — real but not in any money path).  The minimal vocabulary is smaller than Arcturus's 10 schemas: ~4 net-new records — Agreement (genuinely countersigned), Repayment/Transfer-evidence (countersignature borrowed from the rail: the obligor's EIP-3009 authorization plus the Transfer log IS the payer signature), DefaultRecord, EpochSeal — because credit-note, dispute-record, and the signature envelope already exist in hounfour, and conservation ships as constraint files. Critically, full counterparty-signing is incentive-infeasible: even Arcturus's own design signs FacilityDefaultRecord with 2-of-3 Operator keys only (a defaulter won't countersign their default); the honest design substitutes disputability plus conservation-checking there, following CFDI's countersignature logic.  Caveats bounding "the real gap": registry-independence holds for "was this signed / does it conserve" but breaks at key-revocation freshness (no standard achieves fresh revocation + offline verification + no registry); and EAS's empty rails plus EXP-002's INSUFFICIENT verdict warn that vocabulary without an originating servicing business is another empty rail.

**Key evidence (advocate):**
- No standard binds (payer,payee,task,amount,outcome) in one signed receipt; payer signatures authorize transfers only, with task/resource outside the signed payload — result-std-comms-payments.json coverage_gaps[0]-[1], citing x402 scheme_exact_evm.md (signed EIP-3009 fields = {from,to,value,validAfter,validBefore,nonce} only) and extension-offer-and-receipt.md v0.6 ('Wire shape and field placement are not considered stable'; receipt has NO amount field), accessed 2026-08-06
- AP2 stops at authorization: 'there is no described mechanism that conditions payment settlement on verified task completion or delivery' (ap2-protocol.org overview.md, accessed 2026-08-06); v0.2, zero production deployments found — result-std-comms-payments.json AP2 entry
- EAS rails without economic content: 9.5M+ attestations (attest.org, accessed 2026-08-06) but 'targeted search found NO production credit or economic-history attestations on EAS — all lending references are docs idea-zone hypotheticals' — result-std-history-underwriting.json EAS entry
- ISO 20022/UBL semantics without portable verifiability: 'an extracted pacs.008 is not independently verifiable' (network-layer SWIFT PKI trust); Peppol 'Signs the AS4 message with the Peppol AP certificate' at transport hops only (peppol.helger.com PKI docs); UBL DSig verified only at Committee Draft 11, 2011 — result-std-history-underwriting.json, accessed 2026-08-06
- CFDI 4.0 = strongest production precedent of issuer-signed + government-countersigned portable receipts, mandatory for all Mexican taxpayers since 2023-04-01, but jurisdiction-bound with no repayment/credit events — result-std-history-underwriting.json CFDI entry, accessed 2026-08-06
- Explicit audit conclusion matching D: 'no standard defines counterparty-countersigned invoice→payment→repayment event chains verifiable outside a closed network' — result-std-history-underwriting.json coverage_gaps[5]
- In-house prior art for the two unprecedented dimensions: hounfour@e24752e2 src/commons/conservation-law-factories.ts (buildSumInvariant → 'bigint_eq(bigint_sum([...]), total)', @since v8.1.0) + src/constraints/evaluator.ts:157,620 (bigint_sum builtin), re-verified today; freeside@8e616437 packages/events/src/envelope.ts:123-135 (JCS+SHA-256+Ed25519, prev_hash chain) — but 'not imported anywhere in the money path' and apps/worker EventNatsConsumer.ts does not verify envelopes (result-repo-finn-freeside.json)
- The 10 proposed credit schemas are net-new: my re-grep of hounfour@e24752e2 today finds no facility/obligor/evidence-bundle/return-ledger/credit-policy schema files; nearest are mutual-credit, credit-note, dispute-record, x402-settlement — confirming orchestrator-scope.md:86
- Counterparty-signature asymmetry inside Arcturus's own design: doc 10 §4.2 row 8 — FacilityRepayment signer = 'Network collector key; the EIP-3009 authorization is the obligor's consent' (rail-borrowed countersignature); row 9 — FacilityDefaultRecord signer = '2-of-3 Operator' only (unilateral, dispute-record as counterweight) — grimoires/loa/next/10-v0-build-pack.md §4.2 @1920bd6
- Counter-evidence: Catena Labs ACK already specifies payments 'coupled with standardized, verifiable digital receipts' but is an MIT blueprint with no found deployments (agentcommercekit.com, accessed 2026-08-06); Virtuals ACP v2.0 binds all five elements in production (2,000+ agents, Base, escrow+evaluator) but is platform-locked — result-std-history-underwriting.json
- Demand-side caution: EXP-002 '39,999 registered → ~0 transacting' formal verdict INSUFFICIENT, raw funnel data never committed (result-repo-finn-freeside.json); doc 09:262 itself argues 'without servicing there is no repayment history, and the repayment history is the asset' — the vocabulary needs an originator

**Advocate's self-declared weakest point:** The strongest objection: the gap may exist because demand doesn't. EAS proves neutral signed-attestation rails with real adoption stay economically empty for years; GOBL proves a signed-invoice schema with working code attracts negligible adoption; Catena ACK proves someone already specified 'verifiable receipts' and nobody deployed it. On that record, 'no adopted standard exists' is equally consistent with 'the standard is the bottleneck' (hypothesis D) and 'portable economic history has no paying consumer yet' (null hypothesis G) — and the only evidence that could discriminate (EXP-002 funnel data, or a lender actually pricing off a portable event chain) is respectively uncommitted/INSUFFICIENT and nonexistent. Additionally, the 'conservation-checkable, replayable' dimensions having zero external precedent cuts both ways: uniquely defensible, or unwanted.

**Adversarial attack — refuted: True**

The verdict 'supported' fails because hypothesis D is a conjunction and its load-bearing clause collapses. (1) Evidence attack: the citations verify — I fetched extension-offer-and-receipt.md and scheme_exact_evm.md directly; the receipt truly has no amount and no payee field, is seller-signed, optional; EIP-3009 signs only {from,to,value,validAfter,validBefore,nonce} with resource outside the signature. But the analyst omitted that the spec declares 'Behavioral requirements are stable' (only wire placement is unstable) and underplayed that offer(amount,payTo,resourceUrl)+receipt(payer,resourceUrl)+tx-hash is an explicitly designed composite covering payer/payee/amount/resource. (2) Composition attack: under the charter, that draft extension cannot count as coverage — but the adopted-only composition (EIP-3009 rail + EAS off-chain EIP-712 attestations, registry-independent for 'was this signed' per the audit's own credentials result, + ISO 20022 vocabulary) reduces the residue to schema authoring on existing permissionless rails. The analyst's own argument concedes the payment-leg countersignature already exists on the rail and that default countersignature is incentive-infeasible — so 'counterparty-signed event vocabulary' as worded is wrong; what's absent is unilateral-but-disputable schemas plus conservation/replay conventions, ~4 records of glue. (3) Demand attack, decisive: five natural experiments (EAS 9.5M attestations with zero economic content, GOBL negligible adoption, Catena ACK specified verifiable receipts with zero deployments, UBL DSig frozen at Committee Draft since 2011, Pipe's revenue-stream market wound down) show supply of signed economic artifacts without demand. The analyst's weakest_point admits the evidence is 'equally consistent' with the null — a verdict of 'supported' for 'this is the REAL gap' is internally inconsistent with that admission, and hypo-test-G (high confidence) already concluded the gap justifies contributions, not a system.

**Surviving core:** Factual absence only: no adopted standard, and no composition of adopted infrastructure, today defines portable signed schemas for credit-lifecycle events (repayment, default, writeoff, recovery) with counterparty-role definitions, cross-document conservation checks, or deterministic replay — those three dimensions have zero external precedent. However, the payment-leg counterparty signature already exists on the rail (EIP-3009 authorization + Transfer log), the container rails exist (EAS off-chain EIP-712, registry-independent for signature validity), the field vocabulary exists (ISO 20022), and the residue is ~4 schemas plus constraint conventions that anyone could register today. The absence is real but is an adoption fact, not an infrastructure gap, and carries no evidence of demand.

**Post-attack verdict:** partially_supported (factual-absence clause only), confidence low-medium; the clauses 'counterparty-signed vocabulary' (contradicted by the analyst's own rail-borrowing and default-infeasibility findings) and 'this is the real registry-independent gap' (equally consistent with the no-demand null on five natural experiments) are unsupported

### 4.E Hypothesis E

**Stated:** HYPOTHESIS E: A "Return Ledger projection" — auditable, lane-partitioned, epoch-sealed double-entry projection over signed economic events, byte-identically replayable by third parties (docs 09 §5, 10 §4.2#10) — is a distinct missing public-good primitive, rather than (a) operator bookkeeping and/or (b) a derivable consequence of signed semantic events (hypothesis D).

**Advocate verdict:** partially_supported (high confidence)

**Advocate argument:** The Return Ledger decomposes into three layers, and only a thin slice survives as a distinct primitive.  (1) Mostly derivable from the event layer. Given signed semantic events plus a published posting-rule map (doc 10 §5.2's twelve concepts), the ledger is a deterministic fold: ledger = fold(posting_rules, events). The constraint machinery to check it already exists in hounfour today — bigint_sum (evaluator.ts:157), conservation-law factories, audit_trail_chain_valid (evaluator-spec.ts:1463) — and the seal envelope is freeside's existing acvp-l1-v2 (JCS+Ed25519+hash-chain). If D's primitive exists, anyone holding the events can project this ledger; if D fails, E cannot stand alone because replay consumes signed evidence bundles. E is downstream of D, not parallel to it.  (2) Mostly private bookkeeping as designed. All entries live in the Operator-run `arcturus_credit` Postgres schema; entry bodies, terms, and evidence are PRIVATE. The public gets five aggregate endpoints; the build-pack's own exit criterion is "an independent party verifies the chain + totals from public data alone" (10:1087) — chain and totals, not replay. Byte-identical replay is explicitly NDA-gated (09:390: "Auditors under NDA can replay... the public can verify the seals"). The sealer key is held by the Operator's own Railway deployment (11:392,415), seals are not on-chain anchored in v0 (10:334, anchor_tx_hash null), and there are no Merkle inclusion proofs (grep: zero hits in docs 09-11), so an obligor cannot verify their own facility is in a seal. This proves append-only consistency of what the operator chose to record — assured self-reporting, not a third-party-verifiable public good. Omissions before sealing are undetectable.  (3) The non-derivable residue is real but small: (i) the epoch seal as a set-commitment device — "the seal fixes the set" (10:460) — which D's per-event signatures do not provide; (ii) the lane-partition/attribution firewall L-7/L-8 (10:617-618), a policy invariant on the projection preventing emissions laundering into "value returned". And the world-gap is genuine: the underwriting scan found NO verifiable repayment/loss-history primitive anywhere (Goldfinch shut, Maple pivoted, Huma off-chain, EAS zero economic content), while the pattern class (hash-chained commitments over private books + public arithmetic) is well-trodden (RFC 6962 CT; Provisions 2015; score-api's own board_hash).  Verdict: a distinct gap exists (verifiable credit-program loss books), but Arcturus's Return Ledger as specified fills it as operator practice, not as a registry-independent public-good primitive.

**Key evidence (advocate):**
- Design purpose and substrate: 'one auditable, double-entry, lane-partitioned ledger... deterministically, per epoch' built on billing-api PgCreditLedger + freeside events envelope — 09-decision-architecture.md:339-349 @ loa-arcturus 1920bd6; billing-api 0002 migration verified real (append-only, RLS, BIGINT micro-USD) at 7903cf9 (orchestrator-scope.md:63)
- Replay is snapshot/NDA-gated, not public: 'reproducible... by a third party holding the evidence snapshot' (09:378); 'Auditors under NDA can replay byte-identically from snapshots; the public can verify the seals' (09:390); workstream #17 exit = 'verifies the chain + totals from public data alone' — not replay (10-v0-build-pack.md:1087)
- Seal is self-signed by the operator's deployment: ReturnLedgerEpochSeal signer = 'Network sealer key' (10:435); sealer keys held by Railway services (11-v0-reconciliation.md:392,415); Network holds 'Evidence, ledgers, computed decisions', Operator holds keys/capital (10:274); on-chain anchoring deferred — 'anchor_tx_hash left null in v0' (10:334)
- No inclusion proofs: grep -i 'merkle|inclusion proof' over docs 09/10/11 returns nothing (verified 2026-08-06) — counterparties cannot verify their entries are in a seal, weaker than Certificate Transparency (RFC 6962, Experimental 2013, obsoleted by RFC 9162; datatracker.ietf.org fetched 2026-08-06)
- Projection machinery already exists in-house: bigint_sum parser (hounfour src/constraints/evaluator.ts:157 @ e24752e2), conservation-law-factories.ts, audit_trail_chain_valid builtin (evaluator-spec.ts:1463), hash-chain checkpointing (commons/audit-trail-checkpoint.ts), commitment-root.schema.json with anchor_tx_hash field; freeside envelope acvp-l1-v2 JCS+Ed25519+prev_hash (packages/events/src/envelope.ts:24 @ 8e616437); none of the 10 credit schemas incl. return-ledger exist yet (orchestrator-scope.md:86, grep @ e24752e2)
- The non-derivable residue: epoch set-commitment — 'Per epoch: Total order across facilities... the seal fixes the set' (10:460); lane firewall L-7/L-8 — 'any reference to a Lane-A account fails' (10:617-618, 836); L-11/L-12 chain + byte-identical seal (10:621-622)
- World-gap confirmed but as practice-gap, not primitive-gap: 'No repayment/loss-history registry exists for agents or on-chain SMB credit... not a verifiable primitive' and 'No EAS-based credit, revenue, or repayment attestations exist in production' (result-std-history-underwriting.json coverage_gaps, accessed 2026-08-06)
- Pattern-class prior art for committed-private-books + public verification: Provisions — privacy-preserving proofs of solvency for exchanges without revealing raw data (eprint.iacr.org/2015/1008, fetched 2026-08-06); in-house analog: score-api scoring_runs append-only run manifest w/ board_hash (result-repo-sonar-score-loa.json @ 99487902)
- Program-specific chart of accounts (ATTRIBUTED_VALUE_BERACHAIN, WBERA_EMISSIONS_RECEIVED, HONEY_DEMAND_GROSS — 09:355-368) shows the ledger is this operator's Berachain-attribution book, not a neutral reusable vocabulary

**Advocate's self-declared weakest point:** The strongest objection: 'anyone can project a ledger from signed events' is only true in theory — in practice nobody in the verified landscape does it, and the standardized bundle (seal schema + conservation vectors + posting rules shipped as CI-gated ConstraintFiles) could be exactly the public good that makes multi-operator loss books comparable, filling the confirmed gap that no verifiable repayment/loss-history primitive exists anywhere. If graduation gate G3 ('Return Ledger publicly replayed by ≥2 independent parties · on-chain facility registry + epoch-seal publication live and audited', 09:514) were the v0 design rather than a deferred aspiration, my verdict would shift toward supported. I discount this because the v0 docs themselves choose the opposite: private entries, NDA replay, self-held sealer keys, no inclusion proofs, no counterparty countersignatures — the multi-operator-standard story exists nowhere in docs 09-11.

**Adversarial attack — refuted: True**

The verdict's negative findings survive, but its positive residue — 'thin slice (seal schema + conservation vectors + posting rules as ConstraintFiles) is a candidate public good, confidence high' — fails on three checked fronts. (1) Not a public good: the chart of accounts is the operator's Berachain-attribution book (09:355-368, the test's own evidence #9); the sealer is the operator's Railway service (11:415); the only designed consumer check is 'chain + totals' (10:1087), which proves internal consistency of self-reported data. Zero external consumers, no multi-operator design anywhere in 09-11: this is CI discipline plus open-sourced artifacts, and by the charter's own rule (proposed ≠ adopted) not a primitive. (2) The 'ConstraintFiles on existing machinery' formulation is technically infeasible: hounfour's DSL v2.0 (grammar.ts, evaluator.ts registry @ e24752e2, verified) has no filter/group-by/predicate-sum — bigint_sum aggregates unfiltered arrays. L-1, L-7, L-8 are inexpressible; doc 10's own mechanism column assigns them to DB triggers, reconcilers, and 'formula validation'. Only pre-aggregated L-2 checks and L-11 chain validation use existing builtins. The fold needs a new replay engine, so 'machinery already exists in-house' is overstated. (3) 'E is downstream of D' holds only for NDA replay. The PUBLIC artifact — self-signed epoch aggregates, anchor_tx_hash null (10:334), no inclusion proofs — needs no signed events at all: it is the CFDI/Provisions authority-signed-aggregate pattern, already in production/prior art per the test's own citations. So the epoch set-commitment residue is instantiation of an existing pattern class, not a missing primitive. Verdict-as-stated cannot stand at 'partially_supported, high'.

**Surviving core:** Three narrow facts survive: (a) the world-gap is real — no verifiable multi-operator credit-loss/repayment-history registry exists anywhere verified — but it is an adoption/coordination gap, since every technical ingredient (hash-chained commitments, authority-signed aggregates, solvency-proof patterns, chain validation builtins) already exists; (b) the lane-attribution firewall (L-7/L-8) is a genuine non-derivable policy invariant of the projection, but it is program-specific operator policy, not a reusable primitive; (c) conditionally: IF hypothesis D's signed-event layer exists AND >=2 independent operators adopt a shared posting-rule spec + seal schema WITH inclusion proofs and external anchoring (roughly doc 09's deferred G3 gate), an epoch-sealed loss-book commitment could then become a public good — nothing in the v0 design builds toward that.

**Post-attack verdict:** not_supported as a distinct missing public-good primitive (confidence: high). The Return Ledger as specified is cryptographically tamper-evident operator self-reporting: its replay property is derivable from D plus a new (not existing) deterministic fold engine, and its public seal property instantiates the existing authority-signed-aggregate pattern (CFDI, Provisions) rather than filling a primitive gap. The confirmed absence of any verifiable loss-history registry should be recorded as a market-adoption gap under the D/world-gap findings, not credited to E.

### 4.F Hypothesis F

**Stated:** F: A broader protocol is necessary — agent credit requires jointly standardizing identity graph + evidence admissibility + oracle methodology + policy decisions + facility lifecycle + disputes, because the parts are semantically interlocked (a QualifiedRevenueAttestation is meaningless without its admissibility checklist and methodology version; a FacilityDefaultRecord is meaningless without FacilityAgreement terms).

**Advocate verdict:** rejected (high confidence)

**Advocate argument:** The strongest F case is real: the 10-schema set (doc 10 §4.2) is a reference chain, not a list — EvidenceBundle(3)→QRA(4, id=sha256(evidence_commitment, policy_version_id, as_of_block, as_of_block_hash))→CreditPolicyDecision(5, id=sha256(inputs_hash+policy_version_id))→FacilityAgreement(6, one facility per decision_ref)→Draw/Repayment/DefaultRecord(7-9)→EpochSeal(10, prev_seal_hash-chained). D-3 (doc 11) makes admissibility a closed nine-condition fail-closed checklist, with condition 7 binding receipts 1:1 to work records and condition 9 pinning them into content-addressed bundles. A 'qualified_atoms' figure genuinely cannot be interpreted without the gates and policy version that produced it; a default record is uninterpretable without cure_window_days/default_after_days from the agreement. Narrow standards that omit interlocked pieces do produce meaningless artifacts — x402's receipt lacks even an amount field.  But the design itself defeats the necessity claim: interlock is resolved by versioned, content-addressed REFERENCES (policy_version_id, evidence_commitment, decision_ref), not by joint standardization. An artifact needs a resolvable pin to its methodology, which the operator can publish unilaterally — a far weaker requirement than standardizing the methodology. Second, most of F's bundle is commodity or private: identity/delegation/revocation/envelopes are covered by adopted standards (hypo-test-A: 4 of 6 functions — SIWE Final, MetaMask delegation on 24 mainnets, VC 2.0 Rec, JCS/JWS); and EvidenceBundle bodies, agreement terms, and legal names are PRIVATE by design, with replay NDA-gated (doc 09:390) and only the EpochSeal public — that is one operator's internal coherence with tamper-evidence, exactly the counter-argument. Third, adoption evidence favors small: x402's minimal exact scheme moves real (if disputed 6.5–31x) volume while broad-scope A2A v1.0 shows zero measured production traffic. Fourth, zero external implementers exist, none of the 10 schemas exist in hounfour@e24752e2, src/credit is absent at HEAD 1920bd6, and no external party has asked. Sibling analyses already locate the defensible residue in a ~4-schema event vocabulary (D) plus a seal schema and conservation constraints (E). Under the charter's highest burden of proof, 'standardize everything together, now' fails on every leg.

**Key evidence (advocate):**
- Interlock is real at the schema level: QRA id = 'sha256 of (evidence_commitment, policy_version_id, as_of_block, as_of_block_hash)'; CreditPolicyDecision id = 'sha256(inputs_hash + policy_version_id)'; FacilityAgreement idempotency '(decision_ref) unique — one facility per decision' — grimoires/loa/next/10-v0-build-pack.md §4.2 rows 4-6 @1920bd6
- D-3 admissibility is a closed nine-condition fail-closed checklist; condition 7 = work-receipt 1:1 binding (R-8), condition 9 = content-addressed EvidenceBundle pinned to as_of_block_hash re-checked on every replay — grimoires/loa/next/11-v0-reconciliation.md D-3 @1920bd6
- The interlock is discharged by reference, not joint standardization: policy_version_id fields and content-addressed ids appear in rows 3-5 of §4.2; bundle_id = sha256(safeCanonicalize(body)) — doc 10 §4.2
- The bundle is mostly private/operator-internal: EvidenceBundle 'body PRIVATE'; FacilityAgreement 'terms private'; Obligor 'legal_name/jurisdiction are private'; only EpochSeal 'PUBLIC — the whole point' (doc 10 §4.2 P column); 'Auditors under NDA can replay byte-identically from snapshots' (doc 09:390)
- Identity/delegation/revocation/envelope layers are commodity: SIWE ERC-4361 Final (251k weekly npm), EIP-1271 via Safe, MetaMask Delegation Framework v1.3.0 on 24 mainnets, VC 2.0 W3C Rec 2025-05-15, JCS RFC 8785 — hypo-test-A.json verdict partially_supported (4 of 6 functions covered), result-std-identity-accounts.json, result-std-credentials-delegation.json
- Standards succeed small: x402 exact scheme volume 75.41M self-report vs 11.63M x402scan per 30d (disputed but nonzero, Coinbase facilitator ≈83%); A2A v1.0 (LF) 'NO payment/economic semantics', community page 'cites ZERO production deployments', 'No measured transaction/message volume for A2A found anywhere' — result-std-comms-payments.json, accessed 2026-08-06
- Zero implementers and zero demand: all 10 credit schemas net-new (grep of 262 schemas in hounfour@e24752e2 → zero facility/obligor/qualified-revenue/return-ledger/evidence-bundle hits, orchestrator-scope.md:86, re-verified by hypo-attack-G); src/credit absent at loa-arcturus 1920bd6; no external adopter cited anywhere in docs 09-11
- Charter/orchestrator burden: 'F: broader protocol necessary — burden of proof HIGH' — orchestrator-scope.md:38; charter rule 'Draft/proposed standards ≠ adopted infrastructure' — evidence-digest.md
- The defensible residue is already captured by narrower hypotheses: hypo-test-D (supported, medium) locates it in ~4 net-new schemas (Agreement, Repayment/TransferEvidence, DefaultRecord, EpochSeal) + conservation constraints; hypo-test-E (partially_supported, high) finds the ledger 'a deterministic fold' over signed events with only a small non-derivable residue
- Doc 10 §3.1's own separation assigns evidence/ledger/decisions to ONE 'Canonical Network' deployment; the Protocol 'can be implemented by anyone' (doc 10:275) but is implemented by no one — the interlock's coherence lives inside one operator today

**Advocate's self-declared weakest point:** Multi-operator comparability. If the end goal is portable credit history that a SECOND lender can price from, then policy_version_id-as-pointer is insufficient — two operators' 'qualified_atoms' and loss rates are only comparable if admissibility (D-3) and qualification-gate semantics are shared, not merely referenced. CFDI 4.0, the only production precedent of portable signed economic documents in evidence (result-std-history-underwriting.json), succeeded precisely as a jointly imposed full document+validation standard. So F may become correct conditionally at N≥2 operators exchanging histories. Today N=1 and unbuilt, so this rescues F only as a future contingency, not a present necessity — but a critic could fairly say I rejected the hypothesis at the wrong time horizon.

**Adversarial attack — refuted: False**

I attacked the rejection at its declared weakest point and it holds. Load-bearing citations verified: content-addressed derivations in doc 10 §4.2 (QRA id = sha256(evidence_commitment, policy_version_id, as_of_block, as_of_block_hash); CreditPolicyDecision id = sha256(inputs_hash + policy_version_id); FacilityAgreement (decision_ref) unique) — interlock is discharged by versioned reference the operator can publish unilaterally; privacy column (bodies/terms private, only EpochSeal PUBLIC; 09:390 NDA-gated replay); 'Be implemented by anyone' vs zero implementers (src/credit absent at 1920bd6; zero credit schemas in hounfour@e24752e2, re-grepped); burden HIGH (orchestrator-scope:38). The N≥2 objection is not fatal, for four reasons: (a) F asserts present necessity ('requires jointly standardizing') for a system at N=1 with zero external demand — under the charter's highest burden, a future contingency cannot carry a necessity claim; (b) symmetry: applying the N≥2 public-good frame is exactly what refuted D's and E's positive residues — consistency requires it strengthen, not weaken, F's rejection; (c) even at N≥2, CFDI-style comparability requires sharing only qualification methodology + default-definition semantics (D-residue plus methodology publication) — identity/envelope layers stay commodity and agreement bodies stay private at any N, so the six-part joint bundle is never entailed; (d) the path-dependency worry (ship-now schemas foreclose later comparability) is mitigated by the design itself: version pinning lets a future shared methodology adopt at a version boundary without invalidating pinned history.

**Surviving core:** The rejection stands but should be explicitly time-indexed: F is rejected as a present-tense necessity claim. The surviving conditional — at N≥2 independent operators exchanging comparable histories, shared admissibility (D-3) and qualification/default semantics become necessary for comparability, per the CFDI precedent — is real but strictly narrower than F (methodology + event vocabulary, not the six-part bundle) and is already recorded in the analyst's implications and in D/E residues.

**Post-attack verdict:** rejected, confidence high — unchanged in substance; sharpen wording to 'rejected as a present necessity; a strictly narrower joint-semantics requirement (qualification methodology + default definitions) becomes live only at N≥2 operators, and never extends to identity, envelopes, or private agreement bodies'.

### 4.G Hypothesis G

**Stated:** HYPOTHESIS G (null): No distinct Loa-Arcturus system is justified — the credit thesis is untested, the "protocol" is 10 unwritten schemas, underwriting/servicing are competitive private services, and anything genuinely reusable should be contributed to existing standards bodies (x402 receipts WG, EAS schemas, ERC process) rather than built as a new system.

**Advocate verdict:** partially_supported (high confidence)

**Advocate argument:** Every factual predicate of G is verified at pinned SHAs. (1) The oracle has never scored a real transaction: scripts/verify.ts:109 constructs MockChainProvider unconditionally at loa-arcturus HEAD 1920bd6 (re-verified today), and doc 09 §0.4 concedes "no Bepolia tx ever broadcast." (2) Zero facilities exist: doc 09:261 admits "all 11 repos: zero lending primitives"; src/credit is absent at HEAD. (3) The "Protocol" is 10 unwritten schemas: grep of hounfour@e24752e2 finds no facility/obligor/qualified-revenue/return-ledger/evidence-bundle schema; doc 10 §4.2 defines all 10 as net-new. (4) EXP-002 is weaker than doc 09's "settled this empirically" (09:138): the formal verdict was B1=INSUFFICIENT, not FALSIFIED; raw funnel data (funnel.json, registry_sweep.json) was never committed; the branch is unmerged. (5) Demand is untested: falsifier F1 (09:244) is assigned to an MVE that has never run. (6) Underwriting/servicing are private by the docs' own design: the Operator "owns keys, capital, risk policy" (09:122-124); "the history is theirs; the underwriting is ours" (09:434). The candidate public-good core therefore reduces to schemas + signed events + a replayable ledger — none built, one implementer, no external adopter cited anywhere. EXP-002's own lesson applies reflexively: infrastructure without money flow is theater; a "protocol anyone may implement" (09:117) with one implementer is internal schema discipline, not a public good. For the reusable pieces, active venues exist: the x402 offer/receipt extension (v0.6, 2026-02-04, iterating) is exactly where amount/payee/outcome binding belongs; EAS off-chain EIP-712 attestations (production OP-Stack predeploy) can host QualifiedRevenueAttestation-shaped records registry-free. What blocks full support: the standards audit multiply confirmed that NO existing standard or composition binds (payer,payee,task,amount,outcome) in one signed receipt, and NOTHING provides portable third-party-verifiable economic history (result-std-comms-payments; result-std-history-underwriting: "hypothesis 'already exists' FALSE on evidence"). By the charter's own gap test, one genuine registry-independent primitive candidate survives adversarial review, and no current WG owns repayment/default/history semantics — so G's claim that existing bodies suffice is plausible but unproven. Disposition: G's negative claims all hold; no distinct system is justified on today's evidence; the correct next step is standards contributions plus, at most, the $10k MVE as the instrument that could defeat G — not a system.

**Key evidence (advocate):**
- Oracle never scored a real tx — scripts/verify.ts:109 `new MockChainProvider(SYNTHETIC_GRAPH)` unconditional @ loa-arcturus 1920bd6 (re-verified 2026-08-06); doc 09 §0.4 ruling: 'no Bepolia tx ever broadcast (e2e-evidence.md:47-63)'
- Zero facilities/lending primitives — 09-decision-architecture.md:261 'No repayment-history data exists anywhere in the stack (all 11 repos: zero lending primitives)'; `ls src/credit` @ 1920bd6 → No such file or directory (verified 2026-08-06)
- The 10 credit schemas are unwritten — grep facility/obligor/qualified-revenue/return-ledger/evidence-bundle across *.schema.json in loa-hounfour@e24752e2 → zero hits (verified 2026-08-06); 10-v0-build-pack.md:420 §4.2 'New schemas (10)'
- EXP-002 overstated — 09-decision-architecture.md:138 'finn EXP-002 settled this empirically' vs result-repo-finn-freeside.json: formal verdict 'B1=INSUFFICIENT ... NOT FALSIFIED'; funnel.json/registry_sweep.json 'NEVER committed anywhere in score-api git history'; branch feature/exp2-agent-commerce-forensics@96df0145 unmerged
- Demand unknown — 09-decision-architecture.md:244 F1 'Demand: no external counterparty accepts a fee-bearing facility' tested only in §7 MVE; MVE never executed (no facility code exists at any SHA)
- Underwriting is a private service by design — 09-decision-architecture.md:122-125 Operator 'owns keys, capital, risk policy, facility terms, servicing, collections'; :434 'the history is theirs; the underwriting is ours'; :308 'the Operator's book cannot [be forked]'
- Active existing venues for the reusable bits — x402 offer/receipt extension v0.6 (2026-02-04), 'first approved release' v0.5 (2026-01-29), seller-signed portable receipts (result-std-comms-payments.json, accessed 2026-08-06); EAS production predeploy 0x4200...0021 on OP-Stack, off-chain EIP-712 attestations verifiable without a live registry read (orchestrator-scope.md:84, verified 2026-08-06)
- COUNTER-EVIDENCE: no standards composition covers the composite — result-std-comms-payments.json: 'NO standard binds (payer,payee,task,amount,outcome) in one signed receipt'; x402 receipt 'has NO amount field' and is 'wire-unstable'; result-std-history-underwriting.json: portable signed third-party-verifiable economic history 'hypothesis "already exists" FALSE on evidence' (both accessed 2026-08-06)
- Registry lesson applies reflexively to a one-implementer protocol — ERC-8004 deployed on 25+ chains yet arXiv 2606.26028v1 (accessed 2026-08-06): 59-91% sybil-flagged reviewers, 95%+ feedback with no payment proof; and the corpus's own '59-91% peer-reviewed' phrasing is uncited (result-claims-a6b987b0 C1: unsupported_assertion)
- Doc 09's reuse substrate is runtime-broken and unacknowledged — result-repo-money-identity.json BILL-D1 (`SET LOCAL app.community_id = $1` PG syntax error) + BILL-D2 (lot_entries.lot_id NOT NULL blocks overspend rows) @ billing-api 7903cf9; result-claims-a47c9ab4 S5-04 classifies doc 09 §5/§7.2/§9's silence on these as a contradiction

**Advocate's self-declared weakest point:** The strongest objection cuts both ways on my 'partially' qualifier. Against fuller support: the multiply-confirmed gap (no standard anywhere binds payer/payee/task/amount/outcome or provides portable verified economic history) met the charter's own adversarial gap test, and no existing WG owns repayment/default/history semantics — so 'contribute to existing bodies' is an unproven sufficiency claim, and a genuinely novel registry-independent primitive candidate survives, which G denies. Against my withholding full support: a confirmed gap justifies work, not a system; the x402 receipts WG and EAS demonstrably accept exactly this class of extension, and the only thing no standards body can supply — an operator lending real money to generate loss data — is precisely the competitive private service G says is not a public good. On that stricter reading G is fully supported and my carve-out is semantic.

**Adversarial attack — refuted: False**

I re-verified the six most load-bearing citations myself: (1) verify.ts:109 MockChainProvider unconditional at HEAD 1920bd6 plus verify-live.ts:97, src/credit absent; (2) independent grep of all 262 schemas in hounfour@e24752e2 — zero facility/obligor/qualified-revenue/return-ledger/evidence-bundle hits; (3) doc 09 lines 117/122-125/138/244/261/308/434 all verbatim-accurate; (4) result-repo-finn-freeside.json confirms EXP-002 B1=INSUFFICIENT, funnel.json never committed, branch unmerged; (5) result-std-comms-payments.json confirms the composite-receipt gap quotes including "receipt has NO amount field" and wire-instability; (6) result-claims-a6b987b0 C1 confirms the 59-91% corpus claim is uncited. No fabrication or cherry-picking found; counter-evidence was reported honestly. My composition attack on the gap (x402 offer+receipt+tx-hash; Virtuals ACP) failed exactly as the results predicted. Three attacks landed partially: the "partially_supported" label is a category error — orchestrator-scope.md defines G as "no distinct system justified," the withheld sufficiency claim belongs to hypothesis A, and the analyst's own disposition and weakest-point concede G holds fully under that definition; "no WG owns repayment/default/history semantics" is overbroad (ISO 20022 owns the semantics, only portable verifiability is unowned); and the x402/EAS venue claims violate the charter's draft≠infrastructure rule (receipt extension is deliberately amount-free and wire-unstable; EAS has zero economic attestations at scale). None of these flip the verdict — they narrow the label and the gap statement while the factual core and disposition stand.

**Surviving core:** All factual predicates survive re-verification: (1) oracle never scored a real tx (verify.ts:109 MockChainProvider unconditional @1920bd6, re-verified; F-1 dune/rpc non-compiling); (2) zero lending primitives (src/credit absent; 09:261); (3) all 10 credit schemas net-new (my own grep of hounfour@e24752e2: zero hits); (4) EXP-002 formally B1=INSUFFICIENT, raw funnel data never committed, branch 96df0145 unmerged — doc 09:138 overstates; (5) demand untested (F1 assigned to a never-run MVE); (6) underwriting private by design (09:122-125, 308, 434 all verbatim-verified). The disposition survives: no distinct system is justified on today's evidence. The smallest surviving semantic gap, restated precisely and narrower than the analyst's version: a single signed, portable, third-party-verifiable artifact binding (payer, payee, task, amount, outcome), plus a counterparty-countersigned invoice→payment→repayment/default event chain verifiable without platform or registry access — where the SEMANTICS already exist (ISO 20022, production) and only the portable-verifiability layer is unowned; no standard or stable composition covers it (x402 offer+receipt: optional, wire-unstable, receipt lacks amount and explicit payee, outcome is seller self-attestation; Virtuals ACP: platform-locked). What does NOT survive: (a) the "partially" qualifier as applied to G — per orchestrator-scope.md, the withheld sufficiency-of-standards-bodies claim belongs to hypothesis A, and the surviving gap to C/D; under G's own definition the verdict should read fully supported, with the gap filed separately; (b) "no current WG owns repayment/default/history semantics" — overbroad, ISO 20022 owns the semantics; (c) the venue claims' evidence grade — the x402 receipt WG deliberately excluded amount ("privacy-minimal") and the extension is draft-unstable, so per the charter's own rule it is not adopted infrastructure; EAS has zero economic attestations at scale and is absent from Berachain.

**Post-attack verdict:** the attack failed to refute G; the attacker further argued the 'partially' qualifier is a category error and that under G's own definition ('no distinct system justified') the verdict should read **fully supported**, with the surviving semantic gap filed separately under D. This audit adopts that reading in §0 and §5.

---

## 5. The smallest semantic gap that survives adversarial review

Seven advocate verdicts went through adversarial attack (§4). The outcomes: A refuted; B revised upward (sufficient at v0 scope); C rejected as posed; D reduced to its factual-absence clause; E not supported as a distinct primitive; F's rejection sharpened and upheld; G strengthened. The one artifact that every attack agent independently converged on — across attacks arguing in *opposite directions* — is this:

> **Gap (surviving, minimal form).** No adopted standard, and no stable composition of adopted infrastructure, provides:
> **(i)** a single signed, portable, third-party-verifiable artifact binding *(payer, payee, task/resource, amount, outcome)* — x402's exact scheme signs only `{from,to,value,validAfter,validBefore,nonce}` with the resource outside the signature; the x402 offer/receipt extension (v0.6, 2026-02-04) is seller-signed, optional, wire-unstable, and its receipt omits both amount and explicit payee; AP2 mandates stop at purchase authorization; the only production outcome-conditioned settlement (Virtuals ACP v2.0, escrow + evaluator verdicts, Base) is platform-locked;
> **(ii)** portable signed schemas for the credit-lifecycle chain *(invoice → payment → repayment → default → write-off → recovery)* with counterparty-role definitions, cross-document conservation checks, and deterministic replay — verifiable without platform or registry access. ISO 20022 owns the *semantics* at production scale but an extracted `pacs.008` is trustworthy only inside SWIFT's closed PKI; Peppol signs transport hops, not payloads; CFDI 4.0 proves the portable-countersigned-receipt pattern works at national scale but is jurisdiction-bound; EAS has 9.5M+ attestations and zero production economic content; GOBL is issuer-only JWS with negligible adoption.

**And the equally load-bearing caveat that survived with it:** the adversarial pass on hypothesis D showed this absence is *equally consistent with absence of demand*. Five natural experiments — EAS's economically-empty rails, GOBL, Catena ACK (specified verifiable agent receipts, zero deployments), UBL's digital-signature profile (frozen at Committee Draft since ~2011), and Pipe's wound-down revenue-stream market — all supplied signed-economic-artifact infrastructure that nobody consumed. The payment-leg countersignature *already exists on the rail* (the obligor's EIP-3009 authorization + the Transfer log); default/write-off records cannot realistically be counterparty-signed (a defaulting party has no incentive to sign); so the truly novel surface is **unilateral-but-disputable event schemas plus conservation/replay conventions — roughly 4–5 schemas** (Agreement, Repayment/TransferEvidence, DefaultRecord, EpochSeal, plus constraint files), all registrable today on existing permissionless rails (EAS off-chain EIP-712 envelopes are third-party-verifiable with no registry read; hounfour's own JCS+Ed25519 envelope is equivalent machinery).

**Conclusion for §5:** a semantic gap exists and survived attack, but it is a *vocabulary-and-conventions* gap, not a *system* gap. The proportionate response is (a) contribute amount/payee/outcome fields to the x402 offer/receipt extension process (active, accepts contributions), (b) publish the ~4–5 credit-event schemas + conservation vectors in hounfour's existing corpus *if and when* a real loan book produces instances of them, and (c) nothing else.

---

## 6. ERC-8004 compatibility without an ERC-8004 registry

What was verified (2026-08-06): ERC-8004 is status **Draft** (eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-8004), yet its registries are *deployed* — `IdentityRegistry` at `0x8004A169FB4a3325136EB29fA0ceB6D2e539a432` returns EIP-1967 proxy bytecode on Ethereum mainnet (orchestrator-verified), with the same vanity addresses reported across 25+ chains. Deployment ≠ adoption: the only independent quality measurement found (arXiv 2606.26028v1, the "erc8004-reality-check") reports **0.30% of 34,566 registrations pass a minimal ≥3-unique-reviewer bar** and 59–91% of registry reviewers flagged as sybil-coordinated, with 95%+ of feedback carrying no payment proof. Note: the prior corpus asserted the 59–91% figure with "peer-reviewed evidence shows" and *no citation anywhere* (appendix-research-synthesis.txt:66; 01-blueprint.md:362 — classified UNSUPPORTED in the corpus, §1.3 C1); this audit located an actual arXiv source, so the figure is *externally supported but was uncited in the corpus*.

The structural facts that define compatibility-without-dependency:
- ERC-8004's Reputation and Validation registries hard-depend on its Identity Registry (`initialize(address identityRegistry_)`; permission checks resolve through ERC-721 ownership). The Identity Registry is a per-chain singleton **upgradeable proxy**.
- Payments are explicitly out of scope of ERC-8004; feedback need not be payment-backed (measured: overwhelmingly is not).

**What "ERC-8004 compatibility" can preserve with zero registry dependency:**
1. **Identifier shape** — accept `(agentRegistry, agentId)` pairs and ERC-8004 registration-file URIs as *one identity input* among several (alongside plain addresses, did:pkh, SIWE-proven controllers). This costs an input parser, not a dependency.
2. **Feedback/validation data shapes** — emit score data shaped like `giveFeedback()`/`validationResponse()` payloads (0–100 score + evidence URI) through a versioned adapter, exactly the A17 "versioning shim" pattern the prior corpus already adopted (WS3 §4.2 `Erc8004Adapter`, isolation compile-check DoD 14). The Draft's interface churn stays in the adapter.
3. **Wallet-proof semantics** — ERC-8004's `setAgentWallet()` requires EIP-712/ERC-1271 proof of wallet control; the same proof primitive (already Final standards) is usable directly without the registry.

**What must NOT be inherited:** the registry as identity root (that would violate the charter's registry-independence premise and import an upgradeable-proxy trust root controlled by others); reputation-without-economics (the measured sybil saturation is the docs' own EXP-002 lesson at protocol scale); any assumption that Draft interfaces are stable (they are not; the adapter boundary is the mitigation).

**Bottom line:** compatibility is an *adapter concern* — cheap, reversible, and already correctly designed in the prior corpus (A17). Nothing about a registry-independent core requires or is improved by depending on the ERC-8004 registries; conversely, emitting 8004-shaped data keeps the option of being legible to that ecosystem if it ever develops real adoption.

---

## 7. Public-good primitives vs competitive services

The charter requires a clean split. Based on §§3–5, the line falls as follows.

**Genuinely public-good-shaped (IF ever built, publish openly with vectors):**
| Item | Status today | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Credit-event vocabulary (~4–5 schemas: Agreement, Repayment/TransferEvidence, DefaultRecord, EpochSeal) + conservation ConstraintFiles + golden vectors | Does not exist (all net-new; hounfour grep = zero hits) | §5 surviving gap; hounfour's 262-schema/1,369-vector method is the proven in-house publishing pattern |
| Receipt-binding fields (amount, payee, outcome) contributed to the x402 offer/receipt extension | Extension exists (v0.6), fields absent | §5(i); active venue, contribution-shaped not system-shaped |
| Evidence-admissibility checklist (doc 11 D-3's nine conditions) *published as versioned, content-addressed documents artifacts pin by hash* | Doc-only | Hypothesis F implication: publication-for-reference, not standardization |
| The determinism discipline itself (asOf-threaded scoring, byte-identical replay, honest TESTER-REQUIRED verdicts) | Real and CI-green in loa-arcturus (mock-mode) | §2.1; a *practice*, exportable as vectors, not a product |

**Competitive/private service territory (do not dress as public goods):**
| Item | Why |
|---|---|
| Underwriting policy, risk parameters, caps, advance rates | Operator discretion by design (docs 09 §1.1 layer 3, 11 §5); Stripe/Shopify/Pipe all keep this closed — it is the business |
| Capital provision, first-loss, facility terms, collections/servicing | The Operator "is the only entity that can lose money" (09 §1.1); loss data is the crown-jewel asset |
| Obligor admission / KYB / legal-entity binding | D-4 is an operator DB schema with Operator-gated admission (11 D-4: "Enrollment is not permissionless in v0"); hypothesis C confirmed no standard covers agent-shaped entity binding — but v0 doesn't attempt the standard either |
| The Return Ledger's chart of accounts (`ATTRIBUTED_VALUE_BERACHAIN`, `WBERA_EMISSIONS_RECEIVED`, lane partition) | Hypothesis E attack: this is the operator's Berachain-attribution book — program-specific policy, not reusable vocabulary; its public seal is operator self-reporting without inclusion proofs |
| Hosted infrastructure (evidence store, oracle deployment, sealer, relayer) | "Canonical Network" = one operated deployment; billing-api/identity-api are `private: true` npm packages; the "forkable by anyone from a snapshot" claim is aspirational (doc 10 §3.1 classified UNSUPPORTED, §1.2a A-12) |
| Analytics, scoring models, sybil-graph intelligence | The prior corpus itself priced these as licensable IP (01-blueprint.md:362) |

**The item the docs most misclassify:** "history portability" (09 §6: signed QualifiedRevenueAttestations "the obligor can present elsewhere — the history is theirs; the underwriting is ours"). As specified, the portable artifact carries `application_id` but **no obligor linkage**; the obligor→application mapping lives only in the Operator's Postgres, so rotation-survival is a DB join, not a verifiable property (hypothesis C). Portability today reduces to "trust the Operator's Ed25519 key" — an issuer model, not a public good. The fix, if ever pursued, is cryptographic: obligor-signed delegation credentials referenced by hash inside the portable artifacts.

---

## 8. Falsification tests, kill criteria, and the next real-world evidence

### 8.1 Falsification tests for THIS audit's verdict

| # | This audit's claim | Falsified by |
|---|---|---|
| V-1 | No adopted standard/composition covers the §5 gap | Pointing to an adopted (per charter: shipped + production-consumed) standard binding (payer,payee,task,amount,outcome) in one signed portable artifact, or a portable countersigned credit-event chain — e.g., the x402 offer/receipt extension stabilizing WITH amount+payee+outcome fields and independent consumers |
| V-2 | The gap is demand-limited, not infrastructure-limited | An external party (not 0xHoneyJar) shipping consumers of signed credit-event artifacts within 12 months without being paid to; or ≥2 independent operators asking for comparable-history exchange |
| V-3 | No distinct Loa-Arcturus system is justified today | The MVE (doc 11 §8) running honestly and passing its own gates: ≥3 external obligors × ≥4 clean weekly cycles, red-team passed twice with forgery cost published, 100% byte-replayable decisions — that evidence would justify revisiting hypothesis F's N≥2 trigger, not reverse this verdict by itself |
| V-4 | ERC-8004 registries are deployed but adoption-hollow | Independent measurement showing economically-backed feedback at meaningful rates (reversal of the 0.30%/95%+ figures of arXiv 2606.26028v1) |

### 8.2 Kill criteria for any implementation that does proceed

If the operator nonetheless proceeds to the MVE, this audit endorses doc 11's K1–K5 kill gates verbatim (they survived claim audit: §1.2b C-19) with these audit-added preconditions, all of which are *currently failing*:
- **P-1 (blocks everything):** fix F-1 (typecheck), F-2 (BigInt→Number at `oracle.ts:208,:222`), F-3 (nonce double-burn), F-4 (client-asserted evidence — the core build item), F-5 (RLS), F-6 (no reconciler). All six are confirmed present at `1920bd6`.
- **P-2 (blocks any billing-api adoption):** fix BILL-D1 (`SET LOCAL app.community_id = $1` is a PostgreSQL syntax error — every tenant-scoped operation fails at runtime) and BILL-D2 (`lot_entries.lot_id NOT NULL` contradicts overspend/governance entries requiring NULL — the "honest overspend" path cannot write). Both were live-verified against Postgres 16 during this audit; billing-api has no CI. Docs 09/10 recommend this substrate without disclosing either defect.
- **P-3 (blocks any "portable history" claim):** portable artifacts must carry obligor linkage (obligor-signed delegation credential hash), else the portability claim must be dropped from all public materials.
- **P-4 (blocks any public-verifiability claim):** the epoch seal as designed has no Merkle/inclusion proofs — counterparties cannot verify their entries are included (weaker than Certificate Transparency, RFC 6962). Either add inclusion proofs + external anchoring or describe the seal as tamper-evident self-reporting.
- **P-5 (scope hygiene):** publish schemas only for event types the loan book has actually produced (per the EAS/GOBL empty-rails lesson).

### 8.3 The next real-world evidence required (in priority order)

1. **Demand signal for the gap:** take the ~4–5 schema sketch to the x402 Foundation extension process and to ≥2 prospective external consumers (a lender, an underwriting data vendor); a written "we would consume this" beats any architecture document.
2. **One real scored transaction:** the oracle has never scored a real chain transaction (verify.ts:109 MockChainProvider unconditional; Assertion C perpetually TESTER-REQUIRED). One Bepolia/mainnet transaction through a fixed pipeline, byte-replayed by a second machine, converts the determinism claim from mock-verified to real.
3. **The forgery-cost measurement** (doc 09 §10 concedes it does not exist): the sybil-economics invariant (cap < forgery cost) is unmeasurable until the red team produces the first number.
4. **Berachain P-256 precompile status** — verify before any passkey-custody design.
5. **F1 demand test** for the credit product itself (doc 11 §8): ≥20 qualified offers at real pricing; 0 signed in 8 weeks kills thesis A regardless of protocol questions.

## 10. Evidence gaps and unverifiable items (honest red)

Reported per the charter's instruction to stop rather than fill gaps by inference:

1. **EXP-002 raw data** — the "39,999 registered → ~0 transacting" funnel's primary artifacts (`registry_sweep.json`, `funnel.json`, `readout.json`) were never committed; the evidence lives on an unmerged score-api branch (`feature/exp2-agent-commerce-forensics` @ `96df0145`) whose formal pre-registered verdict was **B1=INSUFFICIENT, not FALSIFIED**. The claim's direction is plausible; its strength as stated in doc 09 §1.2 ("settled this empirically") is not supported. UNRESOLVABLE without re-running the sweep.
2. **x402 real volume** — self-reported and independently-indexed figures diverge 6.5–31×; no authoritative number exists. All volume-derived reasoning in the corpus (e.g., "93% Base") should carry this uncertainty.
3. **Berachain P-256 precompile** — unverified (inconclusive probe); blocks passkey-custody design claims.
4. **Berachain market figures** in doc 09 §0.3 (TVL, BERA price, emissions pool) — time-decayed snapshots; doc 10 C-17 already marks them UNVERIFIABLE/time-decaying; not re-verified here because no §5 conclusion depends on them.
5. **"~$30M Berachain PoL/DeFi revenue base"** (appendix; "BBB 2025 report") — no URL, never re-cited or refuted in 09–11; remains unsourced.
6. **Foundation willingness-to-pay** for referee/measurement services — the corpus itself flags this as undiscovered; unchanged.
7. **hounfour schema/vector counts** — this audit counts 262 `*.schema.json` files and 1,369 vector files at `e24752e2`; doc 09 says 234, doc 10 says "264 (241+21+2)" and separately "1,241 vectors"; doc 11 R-14 declined to reopen. Minor, but three documents now carry three different numbers; this audit's counts are reproducible by `find`.
8. **Claims-audit coverage** — the corpus is ~450KB; this audit extracted and verified the ~110 most material claims (§1). Coverage of docs 00–08 was deliberately restricted to claims that carry forward into 09–11.

## 11. What this audit does NOT say

For precision, three things this verdict does not assert: (1) it does not say the MRF credit *business* is a bad idea — it says the business hypothesis is untested and its protocol packaging is unjustified today; F1–F5 remain well-formed falsifiers worth running if the operator wants the business. (2) It does not say the engineering culture is weak — the determinism discipline, honest-red verdicts (TESTER-REQUIRED), anti-self-grading, and doc 10's §8.3 statistics honesty are genuinely strong and were repeatedly verified. (3) It does not say registries are useless — it says nothing in the evidence justifies *this project* building or depending on one, which is also the corpus's own stated position (09 §1.2).
